# Test Writing Guide

## Framework

- Bun Test (`bun:test`) — `describe`, `test`, `expect`, `beforeEach`
- Run all tests: `bun test`
- Run one file: `bun test <file>`
- Run `bun test` from the repository root or from `tests/`, nowhere else. Bun reads only the `bunfig.toml` in the directory `bun test` starts in (it does not search upward), and both `bunfig.toml` and `tests/bunfig.toml` name the host-safety preload guard in `[test]` `preload`, which loads before any test file (see Host safety under Isolation). Started in `tests/<subdir>/` or anywhere else, the run has no guard. Start every run with a scratch HOME and `SLACK_STATE_DIR` all the same, as `tests/README.md` shows
- When stdout is not a TTY (piped, or run by an agent), Bun prints only failing test titles. To see the passing titles, such as a `test.each` matrix's rows, run it in a TTY or add `--reporter=junit --reporter-outfile=<path>`

## Test File Organization

Each source module has a corresponding test file under `tests/`. Every `tests/*.test.ts` file appears in exactly one row below; a new test file gets a row, and a deleted one loses it:

| Source | Test File | What It Tests |
|--------|-----------|---------------|
| lib.ts, liveness-reading.ts (a `pending` row's launch start), server.ts (`_buildIsSessionAliveAdapter` and its liveness readings (C12), `_buildReconnectSessionAdapter`, `_buildKillSessionAdapter`, `deferPendingRow`, `_runCallTimeoutStartStep`, UNAVAILABLE and read errors at call sites (b.jg5 SRJ-105)) | server.test.ts | assertSendable (state-dir rule, with its path boundaries: an `inbox`-prefixed sibling such as `inbox-old`, a sibling named after the state dir such as `<state>2`, a trailing separator; the same rule on real paths through symlinks in a `mkdtempSync` dir, a state dir given through a symlink included; also refuses each listed credentials file by real path, symlinks included), chunkText, sanitizeFilename. A `pending` row's launch start (b.jg5 SRJ-115), through the pure readers: a `pending` `status` result showing a launch start, with or without fractional seconds, has it read raw (`pendingLaunchStartOf`), and `livenessReadingForStatus` gives a frozen `pending` reading carrying it, equal to `pendingLivenessReading(start)`, from which `launchStartOfReading` gives it back; a launch start that is absent, `null`, empty, not a string or behind a throwing getter gives none and the plain `pending` reading, with no throw; another state's launch start is ignored (each state reads as it would with none); no result, or one whose state cannot be read, gives no launch start and reads `unknown`; `pendingLivenessReading` with no or an empty launch start gives the plain reading; and `launchStartOfReading` gives none for a `pending` reading without a non-empty string launch start, for any other reading and for anything that is not a reading. The session-alive adapter over the agent-director stub answers a liveness reading (b.jg5 SRJ-314): each state `status` answers maps to its reading and clears the `ad-unreachable` and `tmux-unavailable` flags (every live state but `pending` reads `live`, `pending` reads `pending`, `ended` and `missing` read `dead`, a state CSCB does not know reads `unknown`), with one unknown-state line naming the persona for a state CSCB does not know and none for a known state; a `pending` result showing a launch start reads `pending` carrying it raw (b.jg5 SRJ-115), one showing none reads the plain `pending` reading with no `launchStartedAt`, and a `live` result carrying a launch start reads `live` with none (one case each here; the permutations are the pure readers' above); `ErrSpawnNotFound` clears both flags and reads `dead`; `ErrSystemInstallDisappeared` sets `ad-unreachable` with the binary path and reads `dead`; `ErrTmuxNotAvailable` sets `tmux-unavailable` and reads `unknown`, not dead; every other `status` error (a CONFIG answer, `ErrCallTimeout`, `ErrTmuxUnresponsive`, `ErrInternal`, a wrapped UnknownError, a name from a later binary, a plain `Error`, another STATE name, an UNUSABLE name, `ErrSchemaMismatch`, a GONE name) reads `unknown`, not dead, with no flag, nothing posted and one log line; each persona is probed as `cscb_<key>`; an unknown key, or no persona config, reads `dead` with no status call. The reconnect adapter (b.9a7), with the tmux-session prober injected (`_setTmuxSessionProber`) so no test shells out to tmux: a `working` row of a persona whose launch is in flight returns `transient` with no tmux probe, no reconnect typed and no sweep (bug b.d61); a `working` row whose tmux session is alive, or whose probe throws, returns `transient` on one attempt with no reconnect typed and no sweep; a `working` row whose tmux session is gone (bug b.d61) probes `slack_bot_<key>`, runs one `findMissing` sweep and returns `escalate-dead`, with no reconnect typed; a `pending` row (bug b.dup) returns `pending` (b.jg5 SRJ-303), with no send-keys, pane read, tmux probe or sweep, no notice and one `Deferring persona=<key>: its row reads pending` line, which names the row's launch start raw when its `status` result shows one (b.jg5 SRJ-115); `deferPendingRow` called directly (b.jg5 SRJ-314), with or without a launch start, returns `pending` with no agent-director call, no notice and that one line; the line names the launch start only when it matches `LAUNCH_START_LOG_RE` (timestamp characters, at most 40): every sample launch start passes and is named, while one holding a line break and a fake `[slack]` line, an over-long one (failing on length alone) or one with other characters fails, and `deferPendingRow` given it still returns `pending` with exactly one line, without the launch part or the injected text; a `status` error at the adapter's state read (`ErrSpawnNotFound`, `ErrSystemInstallDisappeared`, a CONFIG answer, `ErrCallTimeout`, `ErrTmuxUnresponsive` by name, a plain `Error`) returns `transient`, never `escalate-dead` (b.jg5 SRJ-115), after one `status` call, with no send-keys, pane read, tmux probe or sweep and one `status check failed` line; an UNAVAILABLE `send-keys` (b.jg5 SRJ-105; each of `ErrUnknownErrorName` from a later binary, `ErrCallTimeout`, a wrapped `UnknownError`, `ErrTmuxUnresponsive` and `ErrTmuxKillFailed`, by name), at the first try or at the retry after `ErrTmuxSendKeys`, is a refusal: `transient`, never `escalate-dead`, with one or two send-keys, no sweep, no spawn-failure notice and one refusal line describing the error with its marked text redacted, nothing leaking; as the contrast, an UNCLASSIFIED `send-keys` error (`ErrInternal`) keeps its handling: `transient` with one spawn-failure notice and no refusal line; a refused findMissing sweep on the restart path (b.jg5 SRJ-105; 15 cases, the harness's `findMissingError` failing every sweep) at each of five sweep sites (the escalations after a dead-session reconnect (`ErrTmuxSendKeys` twice) and after `ErrSpawnNotInteractive`, a `working` row and an `ask_user` row whose tmux session is gone, all logging under `escalate-dead`, and a live `check_permission` row's deferral at 10 min, under `reconnectSession: prompt row`), refused `ErrTmuxUnresponsive`, and at the `working`-row site each of the five forms: `transient` at every attempt, never `escalate-dead`, with one sweep, no row read after it, no kill, spawn or resume, only the site's own notices (the prompt row's first-attempt one), one `<prefix>: findMissing sweep refused for persona=<key>` line with any marked text redacted and no `findMissing sweep failed` line, nothing leaking; as the contrast, each site's sweep failing with an UNCLASSIFIED `ErrInternal` keeps its handling: `escalate-dead` at the swept attempt with one sweep, no kill, spawn or resume, one `findMissing sweep failed … — proceeding` line and no refusal line; `_buildKillSessionAdapter` (b.jg5 SRJ-105), run inside a recovery attempt as `runRestartWork` runs it: a kill refused with each of the same five forms answers `KILL_SESSION_REFUSED` after one kill with one described refusal line, arms the timer with its cause, starts no condition and posts or leaks nothing, while the same `ErrTmuxUnresponsive` from a kill declared of a row read live does start it (the control); a kill that succeeds or answers `ErrSpawnNotFound` answers nothing (go on) with no line, nothing armed and no condition started or ended; `ErrSystemInstallDisappeared` and `ErrTmuxNotAvailable` raise their outage flag and go on with no refusal line; every other class (UNCLASSIFIED `ErrInternal`, a CONFIG answer, an UNUSABLE NAME answer, a STATE and a GONE name) gives one "error for persona" line, answers nothing and arms nothing; a launch in flight or an unresolvable `claude_config_dir` still skips the kill and answers nothing, even with a kill that would be refused; another live state falls through to the `/mcp reconnect` send-keys, mapped to `success` or `escalate-dead`, whose escalate-dead line reads `verdict=dead-session — tmux session provably dead` after two `ErrTmuxSendKeys` and `verdict=row-not-interactive — row not interactive (…)` after `ErrSpawnNotInteractive` (bug b.jdc); bug b.jdc, prompt rows (`ask_user`, `check_permission`) on a fake clock: a gone tmux session returns `escalate-dead` with one sweep, the `prompt-row-tmux-gone` line, nothing typed or read and no notice; a live one defers with its notice, and from 10 min after the run's first deferral each deferral sweeps and reads the row again (`ended` or `missing` → `escalate-dead`, anything else defers); a failed probe is taken as alive; a launch in flight defers with no probe or notice; a row read in another state (a `pending` read answering `pending`, b.jg5 SRJ-303), or the episode's end, starts the 10 min over; a status error returns `transient` with nothing typed blind, one token-safe line, and ends the working-row evidence (bug b.f2b); the key passed selects `cscb_<key>`. Bug b.f2b, across attempts on a fake clock passed to `_setNow` (tmux alive): a `working` row whose pane keeps the same idle screen (reads of `cscb_<key>`'s last 40 lines) and whose transcript ends with a completed turn defers until that has held for 60 s, then types `/mcp reconnect` (`success`, no sweep, no notice); the adapter's persona lookup finds a fresh session's transcript under the persona's `claude_config_dir`; a running turn's pane is never typed into; an attempt that reads the row in another state ends the evidence, so the next `working` reading starts the 60 s over; a `working` row the adapter can't prove idle is reported (unproven-idle, auto-restart on) once its deferrals have run for 10 min, a failed tmux probe counting as one and a failed status call leaving the run as it is, and an attempt that reads another state starts the run over; an `ask_user` or `check_permission` row whose tmux session lives returns `transient` on every attempt with one probe of `slack_bot_<key>` and no pane read, send-keys or sweep, and raises one blocked-on-prompt notice for the episode; a `waiting` row whose pane shows a running turn or a dialog returns `transient` (a dialog raises the notice). AC 20 (b.av2 SR-10.3): an `isSessionAlive` status error that is not an agent-director error, carrying fake tokens, gives one `status error` line naming its type, code and redacted message only, reads `unknown` and raises no flag, and nothing logged leaks. The call-timeout start step `_runCallTimeoutStartStep` (b.jg5 SRJ-213), run with the production `buildPersonaClientOrExit` over a stub `createClient` that records the options it is given, recording startup-error and exit seams, a capturing log, and the gate's client (a close-counting stub) installed first: a configured `agent_director_call_timeout_ms` (the setting's maximum) and the default configuration (`DEFAULT_AGENT_DIRECTOR_CALL_TIMEOUT_MS`) each build the persona client once with that value and install it in place of the gate's client, which is closed once, with no warning at the default settings, no startup error and no exit; a setting 1 ms below the need at the defaults writes exactly the one `buildAdCallTimeoutWarningLine` line, and the step still builds the client with that setting and resolves with no startup error and no exit; with no values injected, the check reads the values the startup read left in effect (`installAdSettings` over a temp HOME whose file holds `[pause] timeout_seconds` 60, which puts the default setting at or below its need), so the step warns once and the client is still built and installed |
| config.ts | config.test.ts | expandTilde; resolveServerStateDir / resolveServerConfigPath (`SLACK_STATE_DIR` made absolute, the home default when it is unset or empty, read at call time); the start-path primitives (b.av2 SR-8.7): readPersonaConfigBytes returns the file's exact bytes (a byte order mark and invalid UTF-8 included), and a missing file, a path under a regular file or a directory throws a read error whose start message (configFileReadFailureMessage) names the path and says what failed, while a read error with no errno code is "cannot be read" and not a missing file; its stat-first rule, through a recording `PersonaConfigFs` seam: the file opened once, its descriptor stat'ed, only a regular file read through it, and the descriptor always closed (a directory, `/dev/zero`, an injected FIFO, an injected fstat, read or close failure), plus a real FIFO in a child process; parsePersonaConfigBytes resolves bytes and text to what loading by path gives, and in record mode accepts a real-path collision (the same literal or a symlinked working_directory or credentials_file; the bring-up fails those personas instead) while still rejecting every other rule (duplicate name or key, a pre-persona key, an unknown key, a per-entry violation) with the default-mode error naming the record path; a malformed JSON file is reported at its line and column (CRLF, a byte order mark, a character outside the BMP) and echoes no file content, checked with `assertNoLeak` over a pasted fake token; resolveRealPath (real path, lexical fallback, injected realpath); resolveRealPathStrict (bug b.g57, no lexical fallback), on the real file system and through injected realpath and lstat: an existing directory or a symlink to one gives its real path, a directory not created yet (several levels down, a trailing slash, under a symlinked parent) gives its nearest resolvable ancestor's real path plus the rest, and a symlink that points to nothing (or a path under one), a path under a regular file (`ENOTDIR`), a symlink loop (`ELOOP`) and an injected `EIO`, `ESTALE` or `EACCES` are unresolvable with that code (`danglingSymlink` only for the dangling symlink), while an error with no code, a thrown string or a code that is not an errno code (a fake token) is `unknown` and never echoed; persona loader (resolvePersonaConfig, loadPersonaConfig), cross-persona rejections (duplicate name or key, shared working_directory or credentials_file), non-collision controls, SR-14 rejection table; the prefix-related key rule (b.1ix follow-up): `dev`/`dev_2` pinned word for word, then either order, `horde`/`horde_admin`, an in-form name beside a hashed key that starts with it, pairs among three, a suggestion numbered past a taken `_main` and none when another key is a proper prefix, each naming both personas and no other, `dev_a`/`dev_b`, `alpha`/`beta`, same-stem hashed keys and the suggested renames loading, equal keys keeping the uniqueness message, the check order (per-entry and duplicate rules first, the shared-path rule after), record mode rejecting with the default-mode error and a token-shaped name leak-checked; `personaKeysPrefixRelated` and `suggestNonPrefixingName` directly (either order, the `_main` numbering, a candidate over 40 characters checked by its derived key, no candidate equal to another persona's name or key); two personas sharing a channel both load it as `delivery: all` (AC 3 / AC 16); credentialsFilesToProtect (applied and current-file credentials paths, tolerant of an unreadable or unparseable file; a config.json that is not a regular file, injected, `/dev/zero` or a real FIFO in a child process, is never read and adds nothing, so only the applied paths are returned); referencedCredentialsPaths (b.av2 SR-8.3), reading no file: each `credentials_file` in declaration order with duplicates kept, from bytes or text alike, relative and `~user` paths made absolute as written, entries without a usable `credentials_file` skipped, and a top-level array, a top-level string or bytes that are not UTF-8 giving no paths without throwing (its `~` expansion and validation tolerance are covered through credentialsFilesToProtect); every persona rejection passes `assertNoLeak`; the 64 KiB cap (`MAX_RELOAD_FILE_BYTES`, b.av2 SR-8.1): a config file of exactly 65,536 bytes is read in full with the read asking for at most 65,537, one byte more is refused on its stat and never read (`EFBIG`, the path loader failing the same way) with the limit-naming read error and start message, which carry none of its sentinel-bearing content (`assertNoLeak`), and a 192 KiB file whose injected stat under-reports or omits its size, or whose read double returns more than it was asked for, is still refused by the bounded read. Unknown keys at every level (top level, persona entry, `dm`, channel entry), `bot_token` and `app_token` holding fake tokens included, are rejected naming the level and the key, never the value; a token-named key at any level is counted, not shown. Token-shaped persona names (b.av2 SR-1.2: no format rule; the AC 20 config leg): a name that is a fake token, or holds one, loads from `config.json`, from the record (record mode) and through `resolvePersonaConfig`, as written and keyed as usual, as do names near the token shape ("Xoxo bot", `inboxapp-bot`, `sandboxapp-1`, a prefix glued after a letter, a prefix with no dash or followed by a space or a letter, an upper-case prefix); every error of such an entry, from every stage (an unknown key, a `bot_token` or `app_token` value in the entry or in `dm`, a bad channel ID, a missing `permission_prompts`), names `personas[i]` with the name and key as written, and the same name twice gives the duplicate-name error naming it as written. Because the name carries the sentinel on purpose, each artifact is checked with `assertNoLeak` after the name and its key are masked (`withoutName`), which proves no credential value got in. The file's `loadError(input, name?)` expects a rejection, runs `assertNoLeak` over the whole error (with `name` given, over the error with that name and its key masked by `withoutName`) and returns its message, so every rejection case built on it is leak-checked. The server-wide reply settings (b.av2 SR-1.6, SR-10.1, SR-10.2; AC 49): `ack_reaction`, `reply_chunk_limit` and `reply_chunk_mode` load as set, default to no reaction, 4000 and `newline`, and an empty `ack_reaction`, a non-positive or fractional limit or an unknown mode is rejected naming the key; the old `access.json` names (`ackReaction`, `textChunkLimit`, `chunkMode`) at the top level are rejected as unknown keys, and a leftover `access.json` beside `config.json` supplies none of the settings and is left byte-identical, with no file added to the directory. The server-wide `agent_director_call_timeout_ms` (b.jg5 SRJ-213): the default input resolves to 60000 (the one pinned literal; every other case uses the exported constants), both range ends (`MIN_AGENT_DIRECTOR_CALL_TIMEOUT_MS`, `MAX_AGENT_DIRECTOR_CALL_TIMEOUT_MS`) load, and one below the minimum, one above the maximum, zero, a negative value, a non-integer, a numeric string and a non-numeric string (a placeholder, checked never to be echoed) are each rejected with exactly the full message, the loader's prefix naming the file (`loadPersonaConfig: invalid persona config in "<dir>/config.json": `) followed by the validation error naming the setting and both ends of its range; a persona entry that sets it is rejected as an unknown key of the entry; `agentDirectorCallTimeoutMsOf` returns a configuration's value, and the default for a null or undefined configuration |
| config.ts (cron settings) | config-cron.test.ts | The top-level `cron_table_path`, `cron_log_path` and `cron_log_max_bytes` through `loadPersonaConfig` and `resolvePersonaConfig`, each case in its own `mkdtempSync` config directory with an injected temp home: accepted values load (paths made absolute, `~` expanded without mutating the input, a byte limit of 1 accepted); bad paths and limits are rejected, and a rejection message does not embed the offending JSON value; absent paths default under the loaded config's directory (or an explicit `configDir`); `cron_log_max_bytes` has no default; an unknown key is still rejected beside the cron keys |
| registry.ts, persona-bringup-controller.ts (the session drop) | registry.test.ts | Persona-keyed session registry CRUD (newer session replaces older, stale-close guard), pending sessions and in-place promotion, closePendingSession (removes the pending entry, stops the keep-alive, then closes the transport, in that order; a rejecting close is swallowed), matchPersonaByRootsPath (real-path roots cwd → exactly one persona), the posting-scope check checkPersonaTarget (a table over target kind, `dm.enabled` and the `post`/`act` action, with the allowed kind or the refusal reason) and the persona-scoped tools' refusals as tool errors; the MCP instructions' content (`chat_id`, `user_id`, `bot_id` and `via` with its values, `dm` included, `<@ID>` reaching a user or persona, a message without `via` being an injected prompt, a DM conversation and a user ID as `reply` targets, no DM target with DMs off). The unadvertised reload gesture (b.av2 SR-8.8, AC 74; see Unadvertised Reload Checks): each listed tool's whole definition, serialized to JSON (name, description, every schema property name and description), carries none of the reload terms; calling a `reload` or `apply_config` tool gets the unknown-tool refusal itself (a tool error whose content is a single text item matching `/unknown tool/i`, or a protocol error matching unknown tool or not found; any other tool error fails, so a hidden tool that refuses is caught) with no Slack call, no fetch and nothing leaked; the reload terms come from the shared `tests/test-helpers/reload-terms.ts`. The instructions sent: a session's instructions equal the exported `MCP_INSTRUCTIONS` exactly, so the content audit in shipped-docs.test.ts (reload terms, forbidden terms) reads what instances receive; the example `<channel>` tag's `source` is built from `MCP_SERVER_NAME`; the instructions still say to pass `chat_id` back. DM targets through the MCP server (b.av2 SR-5.1): with DMs off, every tool given a `D…`, `U…` or `W…` target is refused with a tool error naming the persona and target and no Slack call on any client, `reply` included, before the dry-run branch (AC 36); with DMs on, a reply in the delivered DM posts there through the persona's own client and removes its ack there (AC 37); a reply to a user ID runs `conversations.open` then posts to the returned conversation on the persona's own client, and a multi-chunk reply with a file opens once and sends everything there, removing the ack in the opened DM (AC 38); two replies to the same user open twice (nothing cached); a comma-separated user list is refused; a refused file stops a user-ID reply before any open; a post that fails after the open is a tool error naming the user ID and the opened conversation; the four act tools act on a `D…` conversation but refuse a user ID with no Slack call; a failed open (`missing_scope` with the `im:write` re-install hint, `user_not_found`, no conversation ID returned) is a token-safe tool error with nothing posted; a `D…` conversation Slack refuses is a tool error naming the DM target with no open fallback or retry; a zero-channel persona with DMs on answers its DM in place and is refused on any channel (AC 40/41); an unconfigured channel or group DM stays refused with DMs on; `dm.enabled` is read per call, so turning DMs off between two replies refuses the second; dry run for a `D…` conversation and for a user ID makes no Slack call. `download_attachment` token scope: isSlackHostedFileUrl accepts only https://files.slack.com; the persona's own bot token is sent as the bearer there, with manual redirects; external or third-party files are refused before any fetch; off-origin redirects are refused and never fetched; at most `MAX_DOWNLOAD_REDIRECTS` redirects are followed; a redirect with no `Location` or a non-2xx response skips the file; refusals are tool errors naming the persona that never echo the URL. A non-Slack failure (a fetch that throws) is a tool error with the generic `tool call failed` wording, nothing saved, and one log line that keeps the thrown message redacted. Not-up personas (b.av2 SR-6.3, SR-6.4): decideSessionAdmission over a stubbed up check and not-up description refuses a not-up persona's session with exactly one token-free line naming it and its cause, keeps its older registered session and neither promotes nor maps the pending one, and admits an up persona with no line; over the real bring-up controller and connection manager, a persona whose controller was cancelled is refused while its connection still serves, a roots path under a missing working directory (in several spellings) maps to that persona only and is refused with nothing thrown, a healthy persona beside a not-up one is admitted and replaced by a newer session as before, a Slack-unreachable persona that comes up on its own retry timer is admitted with no restart, and dry run; dropPersonaSession removes the entry and its MCP session IDs before closing the transport, so the close restarts nothing, and a persona with no session is a no-op; after a drop a new session is refused until the up query reports the persona up; a persona that stops being up with no session drops nothing. The reopen cases are in persona-connections.test.ts. Ack removal by the calling persona (b.av2 SR-4.5), with the entry tracked under the session persona's key and the settings injected through the local `depsWithReplySettings(overrides)` over the harness's `getReplySettings`: a tracked ack is removed once, by the configured `ack_reaction` name, through the persona's own client; a failing `reactions.remove` doesn't fail the reply; no removal for an ack tracked on another message, a reply without `message_id` or no `ack_reaction`; two personas tracking one message each remove only their own reaction through their own client; a `message_id` only another persona tracked removes nothing on any client; a multi-chunk reply removes it once, after its first chunk. Chunking by the server-wide settings (b.av2 SR-1.6, SR-5.2), a table through the real `reply` tool: the defaults (4000, `newline`) split 5999 characters on newlines and post exactly 4000 once; `length` with a limit of 12 splits at exactly 12 and `newline` splits on newlines keeping lines that fit together; no chunk is over the limit, the chunks rejoin to the text, and no other persona's client is called (`chunkText`'s own edge cases are in server.test.ts). The bring-up controller it builds injects `checkConfigDir` over a scratch home (`configDirCheckUnder`), never the default's OS home. |

Destinations (b.av2 SR-7.1): a channel destination posts with no `conversations.open`; a `dm` destination with DMs on runs `conversations.open` for its contact, then `chat.postMessage` to the returned `D…`, both on the persona's own client (AC 29), including a DM-only persona's first prompt with no DM yet (AC 44); one open serves every prompt of the persona, across ticks, and an injected destination resolver's cached DM is reused; two personas in one tick each route by their own setting on their own client (AC 34); a failed open or post is not tracked and emits one `ok: false` trail event; a `dm` destination with DMs off or no contact (which the loader rejects) makes no Slack call; the wedge warning opens and posts in the DM and latches, a refused one neither calls Slack nor latches, and a failed one logs the episode line, not a per-attempt line, and does not latch. A token-shaped Slack error is logged and trailed as `unknown_error`, checked with `assertNoLeak` (the trail-event table and wedge test 6b). A throw while a persona's retry is due (a row the prompt builder rejects, a trail emitter or logger that throws) ends the tick with one rejection and leaves the persona retrying: its prompt, or its warning at the next throttle window, still posts; these cases run the real poller in a child `bun` process (see Process-Level Listeners). Wiring (permission-relay-wiring.test.ts, source audit): the poller and the notifier get the one shared resolver and the one `createPersonaDestinationHold` built over it, and shutdown calls its `cancelAll()` once. Updates after a destination change (b.av2 SR-5.1): the closing update of a DM prompt after DMs are turned off, and of a channel prompt after the destination changes to `dm`, is one `chat.update` in the conversation it was posted in, with no post or open (AC 36); a click on a DM prompt is decided once and updates that `D…` conversation through the persona's own client only, and after DMs are turned off still makes that one update and no post or open; A's channel prompt and D's DM prompt, clicked in one run, each update through their own client. A persona that is not up (b.av2 SR-6.4, each not-up state through the real createPersonaUpPredicate): its rows get no `get`, post, update or trail event while an up persona's row in the same tick posts; a skipped request posts once after it comes up; no stuck-prompt warning while it is not up (after, K fresh empty ticks); its wedge count and a posted prompt (even one whose row closed) are held, not advanced, dropped or re-posted, until it is up; a request not posted because its client is unavailable keeps its one line; the skip is logged once when it starts and once when it ends, and a persona dropped from the applied set ends it with one line, its row then logged as not applied on each tick. In-place changes (b.av2 SR-8.6, AC 58): a prompt posted before a `dm.contact` change closes with one `chat.update` on its recorded DM and ts, with no open and no post, while the persona's next prompt opens the DM with the new contact and posts there, and another persona's tracked prompt stays put; with the applied set swapped mid-tick (a confirmed reload's step 1 landing during an await of the tick), a prompt goes to the destination applied now when the destination or DM settings change while the row's `get` is in flight (a DM destination moved to a channel, a channel moved to a DM with a new contact) and when the destination changes between two prompts of one row; a persona removed during the `get` (on the prompt path or the stuck-prompt path) or between two prompts posts nothing more, logs the not-applied line once and keeps its tracked prompt. A click on a prompt posted before an in-place change (its channel dropped from `channels` and its destination moved, or `dm.contact` changed) is decided once and makes its one `chat.update` where the prompt was posted, through the persona's own client, with no post and no open, and another persona's prompt clicked in the same run is unaffected. `forgetPersonaPrompts(key)` at a teardown (b.av2 SR-6.5, AC 57): drops B's tracked prompts (matched by key or `cscb_<key>`), its not-posted record, stuck-prompt count and not-up skip episode, none of A's, with no Slack call and no trail event; a later tick makes no closing update for B, and a leftover row still labelled for B gets no get, post or tracked prompt. The Web API auth-error watch (bug b.ujn): each of `invalid_auth`, `token_revoked`, `account_inactive` and `not_authed` on a watched call marks A broken at once (the caller gets the original rejection, one line naming A and its path, broken reported once, the socket closed), the next call refused locally; two concurrent rejections log once; the mark happens while A's serializer turn is held, and only the socket's network close waits for it (`stop` closes it at once); a stale client (after a reconnect's swap or `stop`) marks nothing; a reconnect left retrying still swaps A back up, its new socket forwarding nothing while A is broken; a reopen of A waiting on its retry timer or with its `start()` in flight is cancelled by the mark (A stays broken, no timer left, no further `start()`); A already broken by a refused reopen gets no second report or line and keeps the reopen's refusal as its status; the client a reconnect's swap installs is watched (`token_revoked` on it marks A at once, its next call refused locally); `apiCall` and `filesUploadV2`, which need the client as `this`, reach Slack once through A's watched client and resolve with Slack's answer; an `apiCall` method name carrying a token is never echoed (A is marked with no method named, and neither the line, the status nor the next call's local refusal carries it); dry run has no client to watch. Library loggers: `socketModeSlackLogger` forwards only the allowlisted connection-health lines (the ping and pong timeouts, and a failed ping without its error text, even one spanning lines) as the persona's `[slack] persona Socket Mode:` line, and drops every other library line at every level (the ticket-bearing handshake and "WebSocket error!" lines, a failed WSS URL retrieval or WebSocket send, a received message, the `apps.connections.open` client's failed request and rate-limit wait); a throwing sink is caught; each call gives a fresh logger; `socketModeClientOptions` is exactly the SR-3.3 options with the persona's socket logger, its `clientOptions` with no logger, nothing shared between calls. `redactingSlackLogger` and `redactSlackLogText`: URL-like text (`http`, `https`, `ws`, `wss`, any case; in quotes, parentheses or ending a sentence; a URL holding a token replaced whole) becomes `<redacted-url>`, then token-like text `<redacted-token>`, and text with neither is unchanged; a non-string argument (a Slack warning's shape, an object, an Error) is inspected, then redacted; the level label per method and the `web-api:WebClient` name until `setName`. `describeThrownValue` (b.av2 SR-10.3, AC 20): the fields come in order (type, code, message, frames); the message goes through `redactSlackLogText` (bot, app and user tokens, a token glued to a word, a `ticket=` WebSocket URL, an https URL and a URL holding a token each become a placeholder, the rest kept as written), is put on one line (each line break kind joined by a space, a frame-shaped line in a multi-line message shown once, in the message, never as a frame), JSON-escaped (quotes, backslashes, control characters) and capped at 300 characters (redacted first, then cut); an empty, whitespace-only or non-string message, or a message getter that throws, gives no message field; a thrown string is described as `string message="…"` under the same rules (none when empty), and any other non-Error value shows only its type; `describeLogMessage` is the one rendering. For a base `AgentDirectorError`, an `errName` that is a safe identifier (up to 64 characters, beside a safe `code` too) is printed as `errName=`, then the redacted description; one that is a fake token, a sentinel-bearing phrase, two lines, 65 characters, empty or not a string is left out of `errName=`, the message still kept redacted; agent-director or CSCB subclasses and plain errors carrying an `errName` get their type and redacted message with no `errName`, and objects and strings carrying one are described as any other value of their type. AC 20 connection leg, one occasion table (`client options (SR-3.3, AC 20 connection leg)`): for each occasion that builds clients (bring-up and its retry, a reopen and its retry, a credentials reconnect and its backoff retry, a retrying persona's replaced tokens, the recovery bring-up of a credentials-broken persona), A's builds are exactly the listed kinds in order, each with exactly its SR-3.3 options (`attachOriginalToWebAPIRequestError` false on every Web API leg), a socket its persona's socket logger and a Web API client a redacting logger, nothing shared, A ending up and B undisturbed. Reconnect secrecy: the old connection's close after a swap, and a reconnected socket's close after its bot token is revoked, whose `disconnect()` rejects or throws, leave no unhandled rejection and no extra line; a refused connection, DNS failure or timeout on the new long-lived client surfaces without its original request and leaks nothing, with a control built without the SR-3.3 option that keeps it; an abandoned reconnect `start()` that settles late leaks nothing. The SR-3.3 unhandled-rejection table (called directly, never installed) gives one token-free `[slack]` line for sentinel-bearing errors, a multi-line message, `undefined`, `null`, a string, an object, and two failures built by a `makeStubSlack` client (leak marker on, no SR-3.3 options): a Socket Mode connect request error and a WebSocket error event, each named by type, code and redacted message only. Output probes come from `tests/test-helpers/slack-logger-probes.ts`. `stop(key, endReason)` during an open episode (a lost connection whose reopen could not reach Slack, a reopen Slack refused, a bring-up that cannot reach Slack) ends it with exactly one line of its class and `cleared: <endReason>`, a second stop logs nothing, no timer is left and B is untouched; with no episode open (A up) it logs nothing, and `stop` with no reason during an open lost episode (a teardown, b.av2 SR-6.5) logs nothing. Bring-up outcomes, the `bring-up outcomes (E5)` block (persona-bringup-controller.ts, registry.ts admission and drop): createPersonaBringUpController through the real `startupSessionManager` and `spawnForPersona` over the agent-director stub (AC 23/24): one broken persona per cause beside a healthy one (credentials missing, unreadable, locally invalid or refused; working directory missing or unusable; Slack unreachable at `auth.test`, an `auth.test` never answering, and the connect legs that end Slack-unreachable), each ending `broken` or `retrying` with nothing posted and no launch while B is launched and serves; in each, A's agent-director row survives the start sweep (a deferred-check line only when its directory has no real path), the pass and its retries with no agent-director call for A, and A's instance's MCP registration is refused (one line; pending entry removed, keep-alive stopped, transport closed, in that order, through the real closePendingSession) while B's is admitted; the start pass returning while a persona is still retrying; one `persona-start` line per applied persona and each broken line's class, name, key, `personas[i]` and path; a running persona's reopen refused (`token_revoked`) ending broken with no retry timer. Per-persona retry timers (AC 66): a missing or unusable directory re-checked on 5 s doubling to 300 s with no cap and no Slack client, then brought up with no confirmation using the credentials read at the original bring-up, not the edited file; a Slack-unreachable bring-up coming up on its own timer and launched once; combined causes; two retrying personas independent; dry run. Edge cases: a reopen blip right after a directory retry's Slack step still counts as `up` and launches once; an `auth.test` answer arriving after the attempt was abandoned; cancelling a persona mid-retry; a launch that fails after a retry (logged); side-effect audits (no post, no agent-director call for the broken persona). A not-up persona's surviving instance (AC 23/24, SR-6.4): a directory-broken persona's surviving row (waiting, ended, or recorded under a now-dangling symlink's old target) is kept, its instance refused while it retries; once its directory is created it comes up with no confirmation, its launch reuses the row through the collision ladder (reconnect or resume, no kill, delete or fresh spawn) and its instance is then admitted; a running persona whose reopen is refused (`token_revoked`) has its MCP session dropped (one line) with no agent-director call, restart or post and its next registration refused, while a reopen still in flight (`lost`), still retrying or succeeding keeps the session. Leaving up: `onLeftUp` is never called for a persona that was never up, and exactly once on up → broken however many status events follow; a throwing or rejecting listener is logged once; the pure `describePersonaNotUp` table. The held credentials digest (`controller.credentialsDigest(key)`, b.av2 SR-8.3): an up persona holds the digest of the bytes it connected with, unchanged when its file is edited or `bringUp` is asked again (nothing re-read); a persona outside the applied set or never brought up holds nothing; a retrying persona (Slack unreachable, or its directory missing) keeps the digest of its first read across retries after its file is edited and after it comes up, the file opened once; a persona broken by a missing file, a directory at the path or bytes that are not valid UTF-8 holds what broke it (the missing or unreadable marker, never an empty file's digest, or the bytes' digest) and keeps it once the file is fixed; personas broken by a shared credentials file (a record-start real-path collision) each hold the digest of their own path's bytes, opened once and never used to connect (no Slack client is built for either), keep it after the shared file is edited, and a third holds its own (Director decision 1 reversed); in dry run no file is read and nobody holds anything; cancelling a persona forgets its digest, and a fresh bring-up holds the file as it then stands. Teardown cancels every controller and asserts no fake-clock timer is left. The live applied set (b.av2 SR-8.6, `bring-up retries and the live applied set`): a directory-broken persona removed from the applied set stats nothing at its next re-check, builds no Slack client, launches nothing and ends its retries with one line; a Slack-unreachable persona removed from it is not launched when the manager's retry brings it up, with one line; in both, the healthy persona keeps serving. `makeBringUpFixture` always wires a real `createPersonaSerializer` as the controller's `serialize` (`f.serializer`), as `server.ts` does. Serialization (b.av2 SR-6.6): a directory re-check (through the launch it triggers) and a launch after a Slack retry wait behind an operation the test holds for their persona, never another persona's, and re-check cancelled, applied and already launched when they start. A confirmed apply (b.av2 SR-6.1, SR-6.5; AC 19, AC 57): a persona outside the start set brought up against the applied set ends up, broken or retrying with the start pass's classes and lines, takes its tokens from its file (never the token environment) and holds the digest read then, while the running personas get no Slack call; the teardown's `cancel` then `stop` leave no retry attempt (a re-check queued behind the teardown included), close the socket with no reopen and no lost or restored line, and forget its state, digest and identity; `cancelAll` covers a persona added after the start; the Slack API base URL override (E14 Task 4, director decisions 1 and 14), through `resolveSlackApiUrlOverride`: it reads only `CSCB_SLACK_API_URL` from the env it is given (never `process.env`), returns nothing and logs nothing when it is unset or empty; an accepted value (`127.0.0.1` or `[::1]` over `http:`, short and long forms included) returns its parsed href and logs one line naming only the URL's origin and the variable, each row pinning its expected origin; every other value (another scheme or host, `localhost` plain, with a port and path, upper-case or as a subdomain, user info, a query or a fragment) returns nothing, so the start goes on, with one warning naming the variable and the accepted forms `(http://127.0.0.1 or http://[::1])` that never echoes the value (the result passes `assertNoLeak` for secret-bearing values too: fake tokens in the user info, path, query or fragment, and in the path of a `localhost` URL); the option builders add `slackApiUrl` (inside the socket client's `clientOptions` only) and change nothing else, and without a URL give exactly the SR-3.3 sets with no `slackApiUrl` key; the manager passes it to every client it builds at a bring-up, a bring-up retry, a reopen and a credentials reconnect; an honoured `127.0.0.1` URL with a fake token in its path gives the validation, long-lived and socket clients the full href while the logged line is origin-only and passes `assertNoLeak`; and a rejected value leaves every client on its SR-3.3 options |

Once per episode: the kind labels are distinct; for each kind, a post with no open episode posts nothing, the first post of an episode posts and later ones (a kept `begin` included) do not, `end` is silent and false when nothing is open, and a new episode posts again; a post with no mark uses `PERSONA_EPISODE_DEFAULT_MARK`; a sink that posts again for the same episode from inside its call posts nothing twice. Cases and marks: the same case, or no case, keeps the episode (`kept`, same view), a different case begins a new one (`new-case`, new start time, posts again), and so does a case given while the open episode has none; each of two marked texts (the stuck-launch kind) posts at most once per episode, and a new episode, by `end` then `begin` or by a new case, resets both marks. Isolation: each persona has its own latch for every kind; ending one kind (the first of `PERSONA_EPISODE_KINDS`, in one case) leaves every other kind of the persona open and posted; `forget(key)` ends only that persona's episodes, posting and logging nothing, and its later episodes post again; `forget` of a key with no episode changes nothing; `forgetAll()` ends every persona's episodes silently and later episodes post again; two instances keep separate episodes. Start time: an episode's `startedAt` is the clock's time at `begin`, kept by a kept episode and retaken by a new one. A failing sink: one that throws or rejects is logged once, redacted (`[slack] persona-episodes: persona=<key> <kind> notice failed: …`), and the text still counts as posted. Episodes: `missing_scope` on a `dm` destination's open (AC 44, the line naming `im:write`) and `not_in_channel` on a channel post each hold the persona, retried on its own schedule (5 s doubling to 300 s, never giving up, failed retries logging nothing), one start line and one cleared line per episode, and a later failure opening a new episode with its backoff back at 5 s; held notices post once each in submission order after the cause clears, and a later notice queues behind them with no attempt of its own. An attempt that started before the episode opened neither logs, moves the due time nor ends the episode; one that fails after a retry cleared the episode opens no second episode and its notice posts at once. The gate (`begin`): refused until the due time and while a retry is in flight; a gated success posts the held notices at once; a failed gated retry only moves the due time; `release()` gives an attempt back; a gated retry whose resolver throws still ends. Held-notice retries: one in flight at a time; no client at retry time waits again with no line and no Slack call; a throwing persona or client lookup waits the current backoff step with one line per run of throws, which keeps the thrown message redacted; a persona no longer applied drops its notices with one line. The `MAX_HELD_NOTICES_PER_PERSONA` cap drops the oldest, never the one being retried. `cancel` and `cancelAll` drop notices and timers with no further call and one `hold cancelled` / `shutting down` line per persona with notices held (none otherwise; named by key when the name is unknown), and an attempt in flight at cancel changes nothing when it settles. Payload errors and a refused `dm` destination open no episode. Isolation (SR-3.3): one persona's episode never delays, holds or logs for another. Secrecy: a code that is not a short identifier is logged as `unknown_error`. A destination changed in place during an open episode (`permission_prompts` moved): a failure at a destination the episode has not named logs exactly one more opening line naming it, and never again for that destination (a failure back at one already named logs nothing); a stale failure at the new destination logs nothing and moves nothing; the backoff is not reset; the cleared line names the destination the successful post went to, with the latest named cause, and no line names that destination as failing when it never failed |
| persona-destination.ts | persona-destination.test.ts | The destination resolver over `makeStubSlack` stubs: a channel destination makes no open and posts exactly `{channel, text}` synchronously; a `dm` destination opens the DM with its contact on its own client and posts to the returned `D…`, never the user ID, with no username or icon override (AC 29/44); the per-persona cache keyed by persona and contact (a second post reuses it, concurrent posts share one open, a changed contact opens again, two personas open their own conversations on their own clients); `forget(key)` clears only that persona's entry, and an open in flight across a `forget(key)` (an in-place update, with or without a new contact) that succeeds afterwards is never reused: the post that started it finishes there, the next post opens for the current contact, and another persona stays cached; a failed open or post (each class, and one shared by concurrent posts) is returned with its step and code, not cached and leaves other personas' entries alone, and a late failure never evicts a newer entry; `channel_not_found` on the cached DM drops, at once, the entry that post used and never one that now names another conversation; nothing rejects; a `dm` destination with DMs off or no contact is refused with no Slack call, even with a DM cached; describeDestinationFailure is token-safe: a token in the thrown message never appears, and the message is logged redacted (`<redacted-token>`, `<redacted-url>`); a platform error carries its Slack reason beside the redacted message |
| session-manager.ts (start bring-up, start sweep, collision ladder, restart launch, persona notices, stale `working` rows, UNAVAILABLE and read errors at call sites (b.jg5 SRJ-105)) | session-manager.test.ts | Persona notices (b.av2 SR-7.2, `persona notices` describe block, through `makeNotifierHarness`): spawn-failure notices held before validation whose flushed DM open fails `missing_scope` are held under one `persona-destination-failed` line naming `im:write`, with a notice raised during the episode joining them with no Slack call; nothing is retried before the 5 s retry, a failed retry logs nothing and doubles the wait, and once the cause clears every held notice posts once to the opened DM in raised order with one cleared line and no timer left; failure callbacks run only at a notice's first failed attempt (one `spawn-failure-post` record, not one per retry); a channel persona flushed beside it posts at once with no episode. startupSessionManager with `bringUp` (the real bring-up controller): every persona's steps 1–3 run at once, only the launches share the concurrency-limited pool (in readiness order); a persona not brought up ends `broken` or `retrying` by cause (credentials missing, directory missing, Slack refused, Slack unreachable), takes no launch slot and is counted apart from spawn outcomes; at concurrency 1 a retrying persona that comes up later is launched outside the pool, neither waiting for the pass nor blocking it; without `bringUp`, config order through the same pool; launchSession returning `skipped` with no spawn and no trust patch when its `canLaunch` gate refuses. A persona whose working directory can't be resolved to a real path (b.av2 SR-6.4; missing, or a dangling symlink): the start sweep keeps its rows with one deferred-check line per persona while its other sweep conditions still remove rows, and a row whose `cwd` is an existing directory elsewhere is swept as `wrong cwd` with no deferred line; in the collision ladder, a row whose `cwd` has no real path either (a dangling symlink's old target; ended and waiting) is kept (no kill, delete, resume or reconnect; `cwd-unreachable` and `failed`), a row holding the configured path is kept and its resume fails in the missing directory (`cwd-unreachable`, `failed`), and a row whose `cwd` is another existing directory is killed, deleted and spawned fresh (the fresh spawn fails `ErrCwdNotFound` → `cwd-unreachable`); once the directory exists the launch reuses an old-real-target row (reconnect or resume, by state), and a stale `config_dir` label still forces a fresh spawn launchSession asks its gate before it looks up the key: an unknown key the gate lets through is false, with the gate asked once and nothing patched or launched. A key outside the applied set (b.av2 SR-8.6, `launchSession: a key outside the applied set`), refused by the real relaunch gate over a live applied set, is `skipped` with no agent-director call, trust patch or reply-guard record whether or not the config passed still holds it; an applied key through the same gate launches as before. The teardown's quiet kill and delete (b.av2 SR-6.5): `killPersonaInstance` / `deletePersonaInstance` address only `cscb_<key>` and resolve true with no line, startup error or notice; a row already gone (`ErrSpawnNotFound`) resolves false; any other error is rethrown unchanged; both go through the outage wrapper (B's `ad-unreachable` flag raised, then cleared with its all-clear, A's untouched). `whenLaunchSettled(key)` (b.av2 SR-6.6) resolves at once with no launch in flight, waits only for that key's launch held open, never rejects and starts nothing. Next launch (b.av2 SR-8.6, AC 59): with `claude_config_dir` and `stop_hook_bootstrap` changed between two launches, the first uses the old values and the second deletes the old-label row and spawns fresh with the new `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` (not created yet, under a symlinked parent) and writes the new reply-guard record; a configured directory not created yet gets the label of the path it will have. An unresolvable `claude_config_dir` (bug b.g57): for an `ended`, `missing` or dead-session `waiting` row with a matching label, and realpath failing before the launch or during it (after its pre-launch check), the launch is `skipped` with no kill, delete, resume or fresh spawn, one line naming the persona and path and one hold, and once it resolves the same row is resumed; with no hook installed, a hook that holds the persona or a hook that throws, the launch is deferred with no agent-director call and the line logged by the session manager only when nothing holds it; dry run runs the same check (deferred, not the dry-run no-op); the start pass counts such a persona as not brought up (`retrying`, its cause the `claude_config_dir`) while the other is spawned; a directory not created yet under a symlinked parent spawns fresh with its parent's real path plus the rest, and once created the next launch computes the same label and resumes. `compareRowToPersona` and `personaConfigDirLabelValue` for an injected `EIO`, a dangling symlink (on the directory or an ancestor) and a path under a regular file: `configDirResolved` false, no `config_dir` verdict and no label, and the row's lexical label is not a match; a resolvable directory (and one not created yet) keeps the verdict as before. AC 20 (b.av2 SR-10.3), each error carrying fake tokens in its message and properties and every `console.error` argument kept unformatted: the mid-launch "could not be resolved during the launch" line, under a sentinel-bearing `claude_config_dir` and a realpath error, leaks nothing in any captured argument, record or result; a launch that throws gives an unexpected-error line, and a `startup-errors.log` detail, naming its type, code and redacted message only; a pre-launch reply guard, or its undo, that throws leaves the launch going on with one line naming the error by description; the pre-session dialog approver's (`approvePreSessionDialogs`) status, readPane/sendKeys and raw-tmux error lines name the error by type, safe code or `errName` and redacted message, and the approver still reaches the ready state; the `ErrJsonlMissing` diagnosis `get`, in two tables over `installDiagnosisGetFailure` (the collision `get` reads an `ended` row, the resume answers `ErrJsonlMissing`, every later `get` throws the case's error): where the failure leaves the diagnosis inconclusive (`ErrSpawnNotFound` as the typed subclass, and a CONFIG and an UNUSABLE NAME answer, each with a URL and a fake token in its description, named by its `errName` and the client's own description), `spawnForPersona` answers `fresh-after-inconclusive-amnesia` and the failure is named once, redacted, in the `could not fetch the agent-director row` log line, in `startup-errors.log` and in the persona notice; where it is refused (a base `AgentDirectorError`, one whose `errName` is token-shaped, one whose `errName` spans two lines, a plain `Error` with a safe code, a rejected string), `spawnForPersona` answers `{ key, action: 'failed' }` with exactly one `spawnForPersona: ErrJsonlMissing diagnosis get refused` line, a single line naming the error redacted, and no notice, no `jsonl-diagnosis-inconclusive` or `spawn-failed` entry, no `could not fetch` line, no delete and no spawn after the colliding one; with an UNUSABLE NAME answer there and an `ErrJsonlMissing` whose description holds a URL and a fake token, the `AD reported:` text is redacted in the line, the `jsonl-diagnosis-inconclusive` record (its cause ending `ErrJsonlMissing message="<redacted description>"`) and the notice. The inconclusive (a) cases fail the diagnosis `get` with answers that are not read errors: an UNUSABLE NAME answer through `spawnForPersona` (`fresh-after-inconclusive-amnesia`, one delete, two spawns) and a CONFIG answer through `startupSessionManager` (the inconclusive counter at 1, `freshAfterAmnesia` at 0, and a `jsonl-diagnosis-inconclusive` record saying the row could not be fetched); a notifier that throws or rejects is contained, the spawn still reports failed and its one `notifier failed` line names the error by description; a failed spawn, orphan kill, orphan delete or orphan list with a base `AgentDirectorError` whose `errName` is token-shaped and whose description holds a URL and a fake token gives a `startup-errors.log` record naming its type and the redacted description once, and a safe `errName` ends the spawn-failed record in `<errName> message="<redacted description>"` with no cause tail; the spawn-failure notice's Error line is the label (the `errName` when safe, else the type and frames) and the description, redacted, then capped at 300 characters, and shown once. Stale `working` rows (bug b.f2b; screens and transcripts from `tests/test-helpers/working-row-panes.ts`, time on a fake clock passed to `_setNow`): `classifyWorkingPane` reads `busy` (a spinner line in its own shape: a hyphenated verb, a status of thinking only, a custom `spinnerVerbs` verb of several words with or without its status, a task's `activeForm`, and a `●` line as a single word with no status or with a timer and token status; an interrupt or usage-limit hint, each API-retry row), `prompt` only for a dialog anchored to its layout (a numbered list with the `❯` cursor plus a question above it, a known option or footer, or its box; each anchor alone, the trust and development-channels dialogs, a dialog under a spinner), `blank`, or `idle` (spinner text or a dialog quoted in a reply, a retry mentioned without Claude Code's separator, a dialog scrolled above; a Linux screen, `LINUX_IDLE_PANE`, whose `●` reply and tool lines hold ellipses, each such line alone, and a glyph line whose ellipsis is followed by more text); `foldWorkingPaneRun` keeps an idle run only while the screen and the ended transcript's snapshot are unchanged (a grown, rewritten or replaced file starts a new run; an open, unreadable or unread transcript ends it) and a prompt run across differing prompt screens. `checkWorkingRowPane` across restart attempts (reads of `cscb_C`'s pane and row asserted): the same idle screen and ended transcript defer until they have held for 60 s, then reconnect, pending (`hasPendingWorkingRowEvidence`) until it concludes; a changed screen or a transcript written to mid-window restarts the window; a running turn, blank pane or failed read (one token-safe line) ends the evidence; a still, idle screen whose transcript is open (a prompt, tool call or tool result waiting, a queued prompt), unnamed or gone always defers and logs why; the transcript composed under the persona's `claude_config_dir` when the row has no persisted path; a prompt always defers and raises one blocked-on-prompt notice once shown for 60 s; `forgetNotConnectedEpisode(key)` drops only that persona's evidence and notice latch; any launch forgets its evidence. `checkWaitingRowPane`: a running turn (a custom spinner verb included) or retry row defers, a dialog defers with one notice saying the persona is reconnected once it is answered and the session can be told idle again, a failed read (token-safe line) or idle pane (a Linux screen with `●` lines included) reconnects. The notices (blocked on a prompt, not connected, not receiving messages for a streamless session, unproven idle with auto-restart on and off, and `notifyDisconnectedWithAutoRestartDisabled`'s wording per cause) are raised once per episode, each attach command naming the exact session (`tmux attach -t =slack_bot_<key>`, never a bare `slack_bot_`). The unproven-idle notice (`noteWorkingRowDeferral`): measured from the first deferral of the run (nothing 1 ms short of `UNPROVEN_IDLE_NOTICE_AFTER_MS`, raised at it, then nothing more in the episode or for another persona), a run ended and started again measured afresh; restart-path attempts 4 min apart on an idle screen whose row names no transcript, one whose transcript ends with a compaction summary, a screen that changes at every attempt, a running turn or a failed pane read defer with nothing typed and raise one notice at 12 min; a reconnect on idle proof, and any launch, start a new run; a prompt reported first holds the episode's one notice; a launch wait raises it mid-wait (bound 3 min here) at delay 60 and 0, worded for each, goes on waiting and raises nothing more at the deadline. The wait at a launch (`waitForWaitingAndReconnect`): a stale row is reconnected once the idle evidence has held for 60 s, not after 10 minutes; it reads the evidence every 5 s, not every poll; a spinner mid-window restarts the window; a stalled turn (a still, idle screen with an open transcript) and a running, blank or unreadable pane are never typed into; a row reading `waiting` at the deadline is reconnected; each give-up with the session alive (the deadline mid-turn, `ask_user`, the row lost, the deadline's status call failing with a CONFIG answer with tmux alive) returns `not-reconnected` with nothing typed, and at `session_restart_delay` 0 logs that nothing will reconnect it and raises one notice per episode saying why (`ask_user`: *Waiting on a prompt*; the deadline mid-turn: the unproven-idle *Not connected*), else logs what the health check does next and raises no notice, except the unproven-idle one for the deadline on a `working` row held back from for 10 min; a prompt shown for 60 s gives one line and one notice and the wait reconnects once the row reads `waiting`. At `session_restart_delay` 0 and 60, the deadline's status call failing with an UNCLASSIFIED error (`ErrTimeout`) with tmux alive gives `failed` (b.jg5 SRJ-105), never the tmux fallback: nothing typed, no probe, no notice for the deadline's read (only the poll's unproven-idle one, once) and one refusal line per wait. The wait's timeout liveness (bug b.3ce): at the deadline's status call, `ErrSpawnNotFound`, a CONFIG answer (`ErrConfigMalformed`) and an UNUSABLE NAME answer still fall back to the tmux probe (gone gives `dead-session`, alive `not-reconnected`); an UNCLASSIFIED `ErrTimeout` answers `failed` whatever tmux shows, with no probe, nothing typed, no notice and one `status read refused` line (b.jg5 SRJ-105); `ErrSystemInstallDisappeared` and `ErrTmuxNotAvailable` there answer `failed` with no probe, nothing typed and no refusal line, and at the wait's poll or timeout read in `spawnForPersona`'s working branch give `failed` with no refusal marker, no probe, no kill, delete, resume or second spawn, and one `spawn-failed` "reconnect failed (state=working)" entry. `cancelWorkingRowWait`: a running wait wakes from its poll sleep at once and its launch settles `not-reconnected`; a wait not yet started is cancelled from its first check, and the cancel ends with its launch; with nothing in flight it does nothing. The start pass (concurrency 1): a launch waiting on a `working` row is parked as `waiting-in-background` while the others launch and its outcome is logged when it settles; a teardown's cancel settles a parked launch at once; a parked launch that rejects gives one token-safe line and one `spawn-failed` startup error and no unhandled rejection; a start launch that joins a restart's waiting launch counts its give-up as `not-reconnected`. Every launch resolves an unset `claude_config_dir` against a scratch default spawn home set in `beforeEach` (`useSpawnHome` replaces it), and the controller it builds injects `checkLaunchConfigDir`. The start sweep's pre-persona rows (b.1ix): a live one gets one kill call and is kept, with no delete, whether its kill reports success or fails (the result logged, a failure recorded as `orphan-cleanup`); an `ended` or `missing` one gets no kill, no delete and no line; a row naming an absent persona is still killed and deleted, after a failed kill too. After the kills (a failed one included), one findMissing sweep runs, shared by every killed row, and one line gives which killed rows now read `missing` and which still read live, before the summary line; with no live pre-persona row there is no sweep; a failed sweep gives its failure line and one saying the rows still read live, raises no persona's outage flag or notice, and the start sweep finishes. Across two starts over agent-director 0.10.0 modelled (a kill ends the session but leaves the row's state; findMissing marks a live row whose session is gone `missing`), the second start doesn't kill a row whose session the first start's kill ended, and kills again one whose session survived. Raw tmux (b.1ix): `beforeEach` installs a failing tmux runner (`_setTmuxCommandRunner`), so no test reaches a real tmux server; through a tmux model that resolves targets as tmux 3.2a does, the b.vub self-heal kill, the approver's raw pane read and Enter and the liveness probe for persona `dev` target `=slack_bot_dev` / `=slack_bot_dev:` and never touch its prefix neighbour `slack_bot_dev_2`, whether or not `slack_bot_dev` exists; a source audit keeps the runner the only place `src/` starts tmux. Bug b.jdc, the ladder over an `ask_user` or `check_permission` row: with its tmux session gone (the real prober, `has-session -t =slack_bot_C` failing through the runner) it sweeps once and, the row then reading `missing`, resumes the persona with nothing typed, no notice and no `no action` line; a live tmux session or a failed probe is a `no-op` with no sweep; a row that still reads the prompt state after the sweep is a `no-op` with nothing resumed, killed or deleted, and its line names what retries it for the restart delay. `checkPromptRowDeferral` on a fake clock: each persona's run starts at its first deferral, `PROMPT_ROW_SWEEP_AFTER_MS` is 10 min, each deferral from then sweeps and reads the row again, a row read `missing` escalates once and ends the run, a failed read defers with one token-safe line, and a launch or the episode's end starts the run over. The escalate-dead line (`sweepDeadTmuxChannel`) is worded by its verdict: `tmux session provably dead` for `dead-session`, `working-tmux-gone` and `prompt-row-tmux-gone`, `row not interactive (…)` for `row-not-interactive`. A `resume` answering `ErrInvalidFlags` in the collision ladder (b.jg5 SRJ-104; the real installed re-check over `makeStubResolveSystemBinary` on a fake clock never moved): exactly one resolve call and no stop, one UNCLASSIFIED line (the class from `AD_ERROR_CLASS_UNCLASSIFIED`, the name, the JSON-quoted message and the re-check's outcome), one resume and no kill, delete, fresh spawn or second resume; a `resume` answering `ErrNoSessionId` makes no re-check and still deletes and spawns fresh. When that re-check answers stop, the launch posts no spawn-failure notice and answers `{ key, action: 'failed', stopping: true }`, with the one UNCLASSIFIED line and nothing more killed, deleted or launched, and `launchSession` answers `'skipped'` with nothing posted; a pass or could-not-run keeps the notice and a plain `failed`, and `launchSession` answers `false`. The launch attempt and the refusal marker (b.jg5 SRJ-301, SRJ-302), with a recording sink (answering true, as the controller's `arm` does) installed through `initOutageState`'s `triggerSink`: a spawn answering UNAVAILABLE (`ErrTmuxUnresponsive`, a plain `Error`) arms C's timer (`unavailable`) and only C's, the failed launch carries `refused: true` and `launchSession` answers `'refused'`; a LAUNCH FAILURE (`ErrTmuxSessionCreate`) or DIRECTORY (`ErrCwdNotFound`) spawn arms nothing, carries no marker, and `launchSession` answers `false`; with no sink installed an UNAVAILABLE spawn carries no marker and `launchSession` answers `false`; an UNCLASSIFIED collision `get` (`errInternal`) arms `read-error` and marks the launch; a joining call gets the ladder's marked result from one spawn, with one arm; a `launchSession` joining the start pass's refused launch answers `'refused'` while the start pass still tallies it failed. A start pass (concurrency 1; the real controller on a fake clock as the sink, stopped in `afterEach` with nothing pending, no retry run and its lines leak-checked) where one of three personas' spawn answers UNAVAILABLE counts the others as before (two spawned, one failed) and arms only that persona, due one base wait on. The retry timer's row read `readPersonaRowState` (b.jg5 SRJ-303, SRJ-115), with the startup-errors log and stderr captured: a row reading each state agent-director reports (every live state, `pending` included, and every dead one) with no launch start answers `{ state }`, the state as is and no `launchStartedAt` key, from exactly one `status` call for `cscb_B` and no other call, logging nothing and raising no notice or outage emission; a `pending` row showing a launch start, with or without fractional seconds, answers `{ state: 'pending', launchStartedAt }` with it raw, from one `status` call (b.jg5 SRJ-115); a `pending` row whose launch start is absent, `null` or empty, and any other state carrying one, answer `{ state }` only; `ErrSpawnNotFound` (the client's class, or a base `AgentDirectorError` by that name) answers `{ state: 'absent' }` after one `status` call, quietly; an UNAVAILABLE (`ErrTmuxUnresponsive`), an UNCLASSIFIED (`ErrInternal`), a base error with a fake token in its description and a plain `Error` each reach the caller as the same value with no line, startup error or notice (`assertNoLeak`); through the outage wrapper, agent-director unreachable or tmux not available raises B's `ad-unreachable` or `tmux-unavailable` flag (and rethrows), a later read clears it with B's all-clear, and A's flag is untouched; with a recording sink installed, an UNAVAILABLE or UNCLASSIFIED error inside a recovery attempt for B (`runInAttempt`) is reported once, for B, with its cause (`unavailable` or `read-error`) and recorded in the attempt's `lastError`, while the same error outside any attempt or inside one for A reports nothing, and all three still propagate; inside B's attempt, `absent` and a read that succeeds report nothing. UNAVAILABLE and read errors in the collision ladder (b.jg5 SRJ-105; AC 25), every case on `makeRecoveryHarness` (both settings 0, the real retry controller as the trigger sink, the condition installed as `main()` installs it; each SRJ-105 describe's `afterEach`, `srj105AfterEach`, runs `assertNoLeak` over the harness's `captured()` before `cleanup()`), launching P as the start pass does and again through `launchSession`, with errors from the stub's by-name builders and the stub's `spawn`, `resume`, `kill`, `delete` and `send-keys` counts exact: UNAVAILABLE is never destructive, a `test.each` of the five forms (`SRJ105_UNAVAILABLE`, built by the shared `unavailableForms`: `ErrUnknownErrorName`, `ErrCallTimeout`, a wrapped `UnknownError`, `ErrTmuxUnresponsive`, and at kills and deletes `ErrTmuxKillFailed`) over every spawn and resume site (the first spawn, the self-heal retry after `ErrTmuxSessionCreate`, the retry after the collision `get`'s `ErrSpawnNotFound`, the resume of an `ended` or `missing` row, the spawns after the resume's `ErrTmuxSessionCreate`, `ErrNoSessionId`, `ErrSpawnNotResumable` or `ErrSpawnNotFound`, a replacement's fresh spawn and its self-heal spawn), every replacement kill (`resume_enabled` false, a row in another directory read `waiting` or `ended`, a missing `config_dir` label after a dead-session verdict, after the resume's `ErrSpawnNotResumable`), every delete (the same replacements and after the resume's `ErrNoSessionId`) and every reconnect `send-keys` (a `waiting` row, or a `working` row its wait reads `waiting`, at the first try and at the retry after `ErrTmuxSendKeys`): no kill, delete or launch after it, never a dead-session resume, no spawn-failure or outage notice, no `spawn-failed` entry, one refusal line, `{ failed, refused }` and `launchSession` `'refused'`, P's timer armed with its cause (`kill-failed` for `ErrTmuxKillFailed`), the failure count at 0, and B launching as before; `ErrTmuxKillFailed` never starts the condition. A contrast at every kill site (`KILL_SITES`): an UNCLASSIFIED `ErrInternal` or `ErrKillBroken` there is not refused, and the delete and a fresh spawn follow (`spawned`), with no refusal line, trigger or armed timer and nothing counted. The kill after the resume's `ErrSpawnNotResumable` is declared a kill of a row read live: each form there starts P's condition (`kill failed`), where the same answer to the kill of a row in another directory read `ended` starts nothing. Regressions: the persona teardown's kill (`killPersonaInstance`) still rethrows each form unchanged and quietly; the `PASS_THROUGH_SITES` table (the dialog approver's pane read and Enter, and the working-row wait's pane read) keeps its outcomes for each form (logged, the launch goes on, no refusal line, notice or `spawn-failed` entry). The read-error row, over `READ_SITES` (the collision `get`, the working-row wait's poll and timeout `status`, and the `ErrJsonlMissing` diagnosis `get`, made after a resume answers `ErrJsonlMissing` and before its delete and fresh spawn): each site, fed the four forms, an UNCLASSIFIED `ErrTimeout` and a store that cannot be opened (`ErrSchemaMismatch`; cause `read-error`), gives the same refused outcome (one refusal line, `{ failed, refused }`, `launchSession` `'refused'`, the site's exact calls and nothing after them, no notice and no `jsonl-diagnosis-inconclusive` or `spawn-failed` entry) and never starts P's condition; `ErrSpawnNotFound` keeps each site's meaning with nothing refused or armed: one retry spawn at the collision `get`, `not-reconnected` at the poll and through the tmux fallback at the timeout, and at the diagnosis `get` `fresh-after-inconclusive-amnesia` (two spawns, one resume, one delete), still posting one inconclusive notice and writing one `jsonl-diagnosis-inconclusive` entry, both naming the failed row fetch, with no `spawn-failed` entry. The findMissing sweeps, over `SWEEP_SITES` (the before-resume sweep of a `waiting` row's dead session in `resumeOrFreshSpawn`, the working-row wait's up-front sweep and its timeout sweep, and the sweep of a `check_permission` row whose tmux session is gone in `launchOnPromptRow`), each fed the four UNAVAILABLE forms: the attempt stops there with the refused outcome (the site's exact calls and nothing after them: no resume, kill, delete or launch, no notice or `spawn-failed` entry, never counted), one `<logPrefix>: findMissing sweep refused for` line per launch, and P's condition not started (a sweep is not tmux-touching); B launches. The contrast: an UNCLASSIFIED `ErrTimeout` or `ErrSchemaMismatch` at the before-resume sweep is no refusal (`find-missing` is not a read verb): one `findMissing sweep failed … — proceeding` line, and the resume goes on (`resumed`), with no trigger or timer. The joiner: P's prompt-row sweep held in flight, B joins it, and each UNAVAILABLE form failing it refuses both launches under their own keys with one `findMissing` call (`{ failed, refused }` for each, each persona's own refusal line, trigger and armed timer, nothing counted, no condition, no notice and no `spawn-failed` entry). On each replace path (`resume_enabled` false, a row in another directory, the resume's `ErrSpawnNotResumable`) a kill refused `ErrTmuxUnresponsive` or `ErrTmuxKillFailed` is followed by no delete and no launch, answers `{ failed, refused }` from `spawnForPersona` and `'refused'` from `launchSession`, and the restart path's retry (`runRestartRetry`, its own kill succeeding) answers `refused` with the failure count at 0, no notice and no cap. |
