Bug b.d61, through the real liveness and reconnect adapters over one stub agent-director whose row reads `working` until a `findMissing` sweep and `missing` after it, with the tmux-session prober reporting the persona's session gone: one restart runs one sweep, types nothing, then reads the row dead on its second liveness probe and kills and relaunches the persona in the same run. Over the same adapters (b.jg5 SRJ-314, AC 37), a `status` error at the first probe or at the re-probe after the sweep reads `dead` only for `ErrSpawnNotFound` and `ErrSystemInstallDisappeared`, which kill and relaunch once in that run and count the failed launch; a plain `Error`, `ErrCallTimeout`, a wrapped UnknownError, a CONFIG answer or `ErrTmuxNotAvailable` reads `unknown`: nothing is typed, killed or launched, the failure count is left as it was and the arm hook is called once with the key. Bug b.jdc, the same over a row that reads `ask_user` or `check_permission` until the sweep: one probe of its own session, one sweep, nothing typed, no *Waiting on a prompt* notice, and one kill and relaunch in that run with no failure counted. The re-probe after `escalate-dead` (`reprobeDeadAfterEscalate`), with a failure already on record: only a row that now reads `dead` kills and relaunches, counting the failed launch; a row that still reads alive after the sweep kills and launches nothing and leaves the failure count unchanged, and a later restart relaunches it; a row that reads `pending` kills, launches and arms nothing, with the `Session reads pending after escalate-dead` line; a re-probe that reads `unknown` or throws is never read as dead: nothing is killed or launched, nothing counted, the arm hook is called once, and its one `Liveness unknown after escalate-dead` line names the redacted failure when it threw; exactly one re-probe line is logged in each case; shutdown or a persona no longer up at the re-probe kills and launches nothing and records nothing. `health-check.test.ts`'s controller injects `checkConfigDir` over a scratch home (`configDirCheckUnder`), and `restart.test.ts`'s injects `checkLaunchConfigDir` over the spawn home it sets. The restart run as a recovery attempt (b.jg5 SRJ-301, SRJ-302), through the real adapters over the stub client with a real retry controller as the trigger sink on a fake clock that never moves (its `afterEach` stops every retry timer, then, after the cleanup, asserts nothing was left pending and runs `assertNoLeak` over the retry and `console.error` lines of every case); P is restarted, Q never is: UNAVAILABLE (a plain `Error`, `ErrCallTimeout`, `ErrTmuxUnresponsive`) at the kill, the reconnect's `send-keys` or `read-pane`, or the relaunch's spawn, and `ErrTmuxKillFailed` at the kill (cause `kill-failed`), arms P's timer once at the base wait in full mode and leaves Q's alone, with no retry run, no failure counted and no leak, the spawn case's relaunch answering `'refused'` and the kill cases launching nothing (no `launchSession` call, no spawn) with one `Session kill refused for persona=<key> — no relaunch; not counted` line (b.jg5 SRJ-105); the reconnect adapter's `status` read failing with a non-UNAVAILABLE error arms `read-error` with nothing typed; the liveness adapter's `status` throwing UNAVAILABLE or a read error inside a restart run arms P once, calls the arm hook once and reconnects, kills, launches and counts nothing (b.jg5 SRJ-314); `ErrSpawnNotFound` there arms nothing, calls no arm hook and reads dead, so the kill and the launch run; UNAVAILABLE from it outside any attempt (the health tick's call) arms nothing and reads `unknown`, never dead. Over `RESTART_FAILURE_CAP + 2` runs whose relaunch answers UNAVAILABLE, every result is `'refused'` with its one not-counted line, the count stays 0, the cap is never reached and `onCapReached` never fires, and P was armed once; a relaunch failing `ErrTmuxSessionCreate` answers `false`, counts each run, caps and fires `onCapReached` once, arming nothing. At the counting site alone, a deps `launchSession` answering `'refused'` one short of the cap neither resets, counts nor caps and logs the refused line, and a later `false` counts and caps. The restart run's UNAVAILABLE kill, reconnect and relaunch (b.jg5 SRJ-105; AC 25, HO C3 Verify): the file's `makeDeps` takes a `killSession` answering a `KillSessionResult`, which its kill dependency returns as the kill's answer; through `runRestartRetry` at delay 0, with P one failure short of the cap and a launch that fails, a kill after a dead reading that resolves `KILL_SESSION_REFUSED` ends the run `refused` with no launch, nothing counted, no `onCapReached` and one kill-refused line, while one that resolves nothing or throws goes on to the launch, which is counted and caps. On `makeRecoveryHarness` (the real liveness, reconnect, kill and launch adapters over the stub, no alert check, P one failure short of the cap, every line checked with `assertNoLeak`): the kill after a dead reading answering each UNAVAILABLE form (`ErrUnknownErrorName`, `ErrCallTimeout`, a wrapped `UnknownError`, `ErrTmuxUnresponsive`, `ErrTmuxKillFailed`; built by the shared `unavailableForms`) is refused with one `status` and one `kill` call, no launch, the kill-refused line and P's timer armed with its cause, nothing counted, and no cap or spawn-failure notice or startup-errors entry (that this kill starts no `tmux-unresponsive` condition is asserted once, at the kill adapter in server.test.ts); `ErrSpawnNotFound` and an agent-director error of a name no class knows still launch P with no refusal; the reconnect's `send-keys` on a live row answering each form defers the reconnect with no `escalate-dead`, re-probe, kill or launch and nothing counted; the relaunch answering each form is refused and never counted; a relaunch that keeps answering `ErrTmuxUnresponsive` across retries past twice the onset floor posts exactly one onset for P and counts nothing, and one refused once that succeeds at the next retry posts nothing. A relaunch whose resume answers `ErrInvalidFlags`, with the version re-check deciding the stop, is `'skipped'`: the failure on record is kept, nothing more is counted, no cap and no timer. In the backoff cases, a reconnect answering `transient` or `pending` (b.jg5 SRJ-303) leaves a pre-seeded failure count unchanged, below the cap with no `onCapReached`, and on a live row neither relaunches nor re-enters `scheduleRestart`. The retry entry `runRestartRetry` (b.jg5 SRJ-303), called directly with `getRestartDelay` answering 0: before `initRestart` it answers not-initialised, asks nothing and logs one line; with the delay at 0, `scheduleRestart` arms nothing, yet a retry kills and launches P at once, reading no delay and arming no restart timer; a restart timer already pending for P is neither cleared nor re-armed. With the failure count at 1: shutting down, P not up, and P no longer up after the liveness read answer shutting-down or not-up with no reconnect, kill or launch and nothing counted; a live row with P connected and its stream present answers already-connected and does nothing; a live row with P not connected reconnects, and the verdict decides (success: reconnected, a success recorded; transient or no answer: reconnect-deferred; pending: pending-deferred; escalate-dead with the row still live: reconnect-deferred; escalate-dead with the row now dead: a kill and a launch in the same retry, launched; escalate-dead with the row now `pending`: pending-deferred, nothing killed, launched, counted or armed; escalate-dead with the re-probe reading `unknown` or throwing: liveness-unknown, nothing killed, launched or counted, the arm hook called once); a dead row is killed, then launched (`true`: launched, a success recorded; `false`: counted-failure, one `recordFailure`; `'refused'`: refused; `'skipped'`: launch-skipped; the last two count nothing). A `pending` reconnect logs exactly the lines a `transient` one does (the one reconnecting line), with no kill or launch; only the outcome differs. Counted failures up to `RESTART_FAILURE_CAP`: the one that reaches it answers capped with one `onCapReached` (two kills and two launches in all, no skip line), and a retry after it answers capped through the cap re-check, with no kill, no launch, no count, no second call and one `Restart retry skipped for persona=<key> — the persona is at the restart cap; nothing killed or launched` line. The cap re-check: a retry for P already at the cap answers capped right after the in-flight check, asking neither the shutdown nor the not-up check, with no probe, reconnect, kill or launch, nothing counted, no notice and the skip line as its only line; two retries queued through a real serializer one failure short of the cap, the first's launch held and then failing, both answer capped with one launch, one kill, one notice and one skip line (the second's); a fired restart timer's work queued ahead of a retry one failure short of the cap (the file's accepted real timer, behind a gated operation) launches once and reaches the cap, and the retry then answers capped with no launch, one notice and one skip line. An in-flight check that answers true, or throws (one token-safe `treated as in flight` line), answers in-flight with the skip line, before the shutdown and not-up checks, with nothing probed, killed, launched or counted. Through `serialize` (a real serializer), a retry queued behind a gated operation for P asks nothing until its turn, then runs its checks there: launched when nothing changed, not-up when P stopped being up, in-flight when a launch went in flight. The per-key active count keeps P active while a retry or a fired restart timer's work still runs, whichever ends first. With a real launch held in flight (the real adapters and `isLaunchInFlight` over the stub, `holdSpawns`), the retry answers in-flight with no agent-director call, and the held launch still completes. Liveness readings (b.jg5 SRJ-314, AC 37, HO C12), with the failure count at 1 and `RestartDeps.armRetryTimer` a recording double: through the retry entry and through the restart timer, a first probe that reads `unknown`, throws, or answers something that is not a reading (a boolean) reconnects, kills and launches nothing, counts nothing, reaches no cap, calls the arm hook once with the key and logs one `Liveness unknown for persona=<key>` line (ending `— no reconnect, kill or launch; nothing counted`, or naming the redacted failure when it threw), with no relaunch line and no leak; the retry entry answers liveness-unknown. With no arm hook nothing is armed and nothing more is logged; an arm hook that throws gives one redacted `restart: arming the retry timer failed for persona=<key>` line and changes nothing else. The `pending` deferral (b.jg5 SRJ-115, SRJ-314), with the failure count at 1 and `RestartDeps.deferPendingRow` a recording double: through the retry entry and through the restart timer, a first probe that reads `pending`, with or without a launch start, with P connected with its stream, connected but streamless or disconnected, hands P and the reading (the launch start carried) to the deferral once, reads neither connectedness nor the stream, reconnects, kills and launches nothing, counts and arms nothing, logs no line of its own and answers pending-deferred; the reading's launch start is handed on raw when it is a non-empty string and as none when it is empty or not a string; a deferral that throws gives one redacted `restart: the pending deferral failed for persona=<key>` line and changes nothing else; with no deferral member nothing is called or logged and the outcome is the same; `live` and `dead` readings never reach the deferral (a connected `live` row with its stream is already-connected, another `live` row is reconnected, a `dead` one is killed, launched and counted); and through the real liveness and reconnect adapters over the stub, with the deferral bound as `main()` binds it, a row whose `status` answers `pending` in each of those connections makes one `status` read and no other agent-director call, kills, launches and counts nothing and logs one line starting `Deferring persona=<key>`, holding the launch start when there is one. Health check (b.jg5 SRJ-314): a `dead` reading schedules a restart at once; an `unknown` reading, a thrown probe or an answer that is not a reading skips the persona for the tick, whether its session is connected or not: over three ticks nothing is scheduled, no not-connected notice is asked for (auto-restart disabled), no episode is ended, connectedness and the stream are never probed, and each tick logs one `liveness unknown for persona=<key> … skipping it this tick` line; the skip clears the disconnected streak (undeliverable, then unknown, then undeliverable schedules nothing; a fourth undeliverable tick schedules); the other persona, read dead, is still scheduled in the same tick; and a `dead` reading on the next tick schedules at once. A `pending` row is never counted healthy (b.jg5 SRJ-314), each case with and without a launch start on the reading, auto-restart disabled: connected with its stream, it is scheduled on its second consecutive tick and never its first, asks for no not-connected notice and ends no episode, and only a `live` healthy tick between resets the streak and ends the episode; disconnected, or connected with its stream gone, it keeps the alive branch, scheduled every second tick with each schedule asking for the notice with its true reason (`disconnected` or `streamless`) and no episode ended. A live, connected tick with its stream ends the persona's `tmux-unresponsive` condition (b.jg5 SRJ-310, the tick's half; production's binding is pinned in `server-startup-wiring.test.ts`), with the optional `endTmuxUnresponsive(key)` hook set on the deps and left absent in every other case: beside B read live but disconnected, or `pending` and connected with its stream, in the same tick (B checked first), the hook is called once, for P only; over four ticks it is called on each tick that finds the persona live and deliverable and on no other (`pending`, then live but disconnected, skip it); and over two ticks it is never called for a `pending` reading with or without a launch start, `unknown`, a thrown probe, `dead`, live but disconnected, or live and connected but streamless. The tick-end hook (b.jg5 SRJ-308; production binds it to the condition's `onsetAtTick`, pinned in `server-startup-wiring.test.ts`), with the optional `onTickEnd` and `now` set on the deps (a file-local `withTickEnd` binds a clock answering given start times and a recording hook) and left absent in every other case: over three ticks of two personas, `now` is read once per tick body, before its work list is asked for, and the hook is called once per body, after both personas' work, with that tick's start time; the argument is the time read at the body's start, not one read when it ends; with `now` absent it is the system clock's; the hook is still called once per tick when every persona is skipped (a restart pending, a launch in flight, at the cap, an `unknown` reading), when the work list is empty, and once, not per persona, when one persona is skipped and one checked; a persona's work that throws is logged, the next persona is checked and the hook is called after both; the tick failed: `getPersonas` throwing an error carrying fake tokens gives one redacted `the tick failed` line, the hook is still called once with that tick's start time (no persona probed), the next tick runs normally, no unhandled rejection escapes and nothing leaks; deps swapped by `initHealthCheck` mid-tick (the first body held on its `statRoute`): the held tick calls the hook of the deps it started with, with its start time, and the new deps' hook gets only its own ticks' start times; a throwing hook is logged once per tick (`the tick-end hook failed`) and every tick's work is still done; a throwing `now` is logged once (`no tick-end hook this tick`) and that tick calls no hook while its work is still done and the ticks either side call it; fires skipped while a body is in flight, or while shutting down, read no start time and call no hook, and the body in flight calls it once when it ends. |

The loader's message built on them is in config.test.ts |
| atomic-write.ts | atomic-write.test.ts | durableWriteFileSync, the reload files' atomic, durable write (b.av2 SR-8.1): on the real file system in a temp directory (any bytes written exactly: CRLF text, invalid UTF-8, none at all; an overwrite leaving no trailing old bytes; a failure the file system raises itself and a missing parent (ENOENT) reaching the caller and changing nothing); through a recording `DurableWriteFs` seam (`makeRecordingFs`, which delegates to the real `node:fs` and can make chosen steps throw): the call order (write and fsync the temporary file, close it, rename it over the target, fsync the directory), the target keeping its old bytes until the rename, short writes continued from the next offset, a unique `.tmp` sibling that is none of the reload file names; a failure at any step up to the rename reaching the caller with the previous bytes kept, the temporary file removed and every descriptor closed (and the original error still thrown when the removal also fails); a directory open or fsync failure after the rename throwing `DurableWriteUnsyncedError` (its `path`, errno `code` and `syncError`) with the new bytes in place and nothing removed; a directory close failure after a good fsync ignored; a directory fsync failure thrown as a non-`Error` whose code is a number giving an unsynced error whose `code` is undefined. durableUnlinkSync, the durable delete of `config.json.pending` (b.av2 SR-8.1, SR-8.3), on the same seam: on the real file system it removes the file and returns true, leaving its siblings; the order is unlink, then open, fsync and close of the directory, with no descriptor left open; a file already gone, a missing parent (`ENOENT`) or a parent that is a regular file (`ENOTDIR`) returns false and syncs nothing; any other unlink failure (an injected `EIO` or `EACCES` standing for every other code, a real directory's `EISDIR`, and an `Error` with no code whose message names `ENOENT`, one whose code is a number or a thrown string) reaches the caller exactly as raised, the file kept; a directory open (`EIO`) or fsync (`ENOENT`) failure after the unlink throws `DurableUnlinkUnsyncedError` keeping its errno `code` and `syncError`, and a non-`Error` fsync failure whose code is a number gives `code` undefined, each with the file already gone, every descriptor closed and no retry; a directory close failure after a good fsync is ignored. The recording seam's `codes` option picks the injected errno code per step (`EIO` by default) and `thrown` throws a given value as is. reloadFilePaths (the three SR-8.1 names beside `config.json`). `atomicWriteFileSync` is covered by the stop-hook and reply-guard suites |

a record that can't be written (the write seam failing at `openSync`, `writeSync`, the temporary file's `fsyncSync` or `renameSync`) refuses with one `reload-record-write-failed` line and leaves only `config.json` in the directory; a record written but whose directory fsync fails after the rename refuses with a line saying the next start will run it, and the next start does (from the record, whatever the config file then holds); Detection (b.av2 SR-8.2, SR-8.3, SR-8.7), each check a manual tick: `startDetection` arms nothing before the start bring-up pass returns, after `stopDetection` or after a refused start, and arms one tick once; a start pass that throws has still returned, so detection arms and its first check runs; with the production driver on the run's fake clock, the first check comes exactly 5 s after `startDetection` and a later check finding the file current writes nothing; a clean start (one persona up, one retrying, one broken) does nothing over many checks (`NO_RUN_ACTIVITY`); an edit held over many ticks (a persona added, removed or changed, a server-wide setting changed) writes the pending file once, logs its preview once and makes no lifecycle call, Slack client or Slack call (AC 55), and one newline added is previewed and logged once as `no effective change`; a revert removes the pending file and logs `reload-nothing-pending` once, leaving no temporary file (AC 62); an unconfirmed destructive edit survives a restart from the record, which brings the applied set back, and the first check leaves the edit pending (AC 70); the fingerprint is stable over unchanged checks and follows the config bytes and each referenced credentials file (bytes, empty, missing, unreadable), never an unreferenced one. A real FIFO at the config or a referenced credentials path keeps each check bounded and counts as unreadable: one pending-file write and one line, with no lifecycle call, Slack client or Slack call (child process through `runHarnessInChild`, 10 s bound). Pending-file write and delete failures (`h.failWrites`, `h.failRemoves`) are logged once per episode and retried at each check, and the next success ends the episode, so a later failure in the same pending state is logged again; a write whose directory sync fails is written again at the next check; a delete whose directory sync fails counts as removed (`unsynced`), is logged once, followed by `reload-nothing-pending`, and never retried; each change of the pending state opens a new episode. steps 2–6 run after step 1, in order, each settled before the next, and a failing step is logged while the later ones still run (`opts.applySteps`); the default step 3 body, `lifecycleApplySlots` called directly with recording members and no run, gives each persona modified in place exactly one `updateInPlace` call (its first entry when the list names it twice) and runs no other member; the step inputs are split by class (`recordingSlots` records step 2's `removed` and `destructiveOld`, step 4's `credentials` and `credentialsBroken`, step 6's `added` and `destructiveNew`): a destructive modify is only its two halves (old and new declaration) and a rotation of a persona broken by its credentials only `credentialsBroken`; what the default step bodies do to the personas is pinned in `tests/reload-apply.test.ts`; a confirmed no-op whose config directories changed lexically runs only the template refresh and logs `reload-noop`. Confirmed credentials changes end to end (AC 65, b.av2 SR-6.4, SR-8.3): a persona credentials-broken at start (its file missing, locally invalid, or its token refused by Slack; one row each over the recorder's stand-ins and over the real composition) is previewed as brought up once a good file is placed, stays broken over many ticks and a long clock advance, and after one rename comes up with the new tokens, with no restart and nothing done to the healthy persona; a persona Slack refused at start stays broken with no `auth.test` retry after its app is fixed on Slack's side, until a byte-only re-save of its file is confirmed; a Slack-unreachable persona whose confirmed new file is unusable keeps retrying with its current content, the change pending, and a good file confirmed then is what its next retry comes up with. The 64 KiB cap on the tick path (`h.writeOversized`): an oversized config file logs one `reload-invalid` line and previews INVALID naming the limit; an oversized referenced credentials file is previewed as an unusable credentials change and never leaks; a persona whose credentials file is oversized at the start is broken, holds the unreadable marker and leaves nothing pending. Token secrecy on the reload path (b.av2 SR-10.3, AC 20): a fake token pasted under a `bot_token` or `app_token` key (in a persona entry or at the top level) is, on a running server, an INVALID candidate naming the key and never the value, and its confirmation applies nothing; a start from `config.json` or from the record with it refuses the same way. A throw from the server's `onApplied` is logged with its message redacted and the apply goes on (steps 2–6 run, `reload-applied` is logged). A token-shaped persona name (b.av2 SR-1.2: no format rule) is an ordinary name: renaming a persona to one (a removal plus an addition) or adding one is a valid candidate, previewed with the name as written and applied by one confirmation, and a start from the config file or the record with one starts. The preview is asserted line for line (the header with its counts, a removed line for a rename, then the added line with the name as written). The name comes from `fakeToken`, so those runs' artifacts are asserted to hold it; their leak check masks the name and its key with `withoutName` and runs `assertNoLeak` over the rest (`run.captured()` and the server-side files, the credentials files the runs read included), before and after the confirmation. Every case leak-checks `run.captured()` while the pending file exists, and the operator's own files (the config file, credentials files, a hand-edited record) are left out of the check |

Every wait comes from `UNAVAILABLE_RETRY_BASE_S` and `UNAVAILABLE_RETRY_CEILING_S` through `doublingBackoffDelay`, bar the one pin case. Never gives up: refusals past `RESTART_FAILURE_CAP * 3` leave the timer armed at the ceiling, `getFailureCount` 0 and `isAtCap` false; a failure count recorded beforehand changes no wait and is left as it was. Arming while armed: while waiting, the due time and wait count are kept and the new cause recorded; while a run is in flight, nothing is armed, the re-arm is measured from the run's end and the next retry sees the cause; a run in flight when stopped has its answer dropped, and the next arm's retry waits for that run to settle. What `arm` answers (SRJ-301): whether the persona has a timer after the call, as `isArmed` does: true when newly armed, when already armed and waiting, and while its retry runs; false on a closed controller and when its clock throws at the first arm; `reportAttemptError` inside an attempt, with a sink that answers false, one that answers nothing (an untyped caller) or a closed controller as the sink, answers false and records the error in the attempt's `lastError` as not armed. Isolation: two personas keep separate schedules; stopping one leaves the other's due time; stop-all clears exactly the retry timers (an unrelated timer on the clock stays); two controllers on two clocks share nothing. The arming predicate (`unavailableRetryCauseFor`) over 17 rows: UNAVAILABLE from `resume`, `spawn`, `delete` or no known verb arms `unavailable`; `ErrTmuxKillFailed` from `kill` or `status` arms `kill-failed`; a STATE answer from `get` or `list` and a GONE answer from `status` arm `read-error`; `ErrSpawnNotFound` from `get` or `status`, a CONFIG answer from `get`, an UNUSABLE NAME answer from `status`, a STATE answer from `read-pane` or with no verb, DIRECTORY from `spawn` and LAUNCH FAILURE from `resume` arm nothing. Inside a launch, a STATE `resume` answer (`ErrJsonlNeverWritten`), a DIRECTORY spawn, a LAUNCH FAILURE `resume` and `ErrSpawnNotFound` from the collision `get` or the readiness read arm and mark nothing. With `triggerSink: false`, a launch fed UNAVAILABLE arms nothing, throws nothing and is not marked. Attempt frames run on the bare controller as the sink: a `test.each` over an attempt started later from inside a closed attempt (by a real 0 ms `setTimeout` callback, set inside the attempt and awaited, or by a promise continuation), with an enclosing attempt still open or none, finds the new attempt is not nested in the closed one: `isInsideAttempt` is true for its own key and the open outer key only, a report arms only those keys and is recorded in their own `lastError` (the closed attempt's stays unset), the closed key is never armed, and every closed key answers false afterwards. The full-mode retry end to end (SRJ-302, SRJ-303, SRJ-305) on the harness's default action, with a file-local `modelRow` answering each persona's `status` from a given state (`absent` as `ErrSpawnNotFound`) until a spawn for it resolves and `waiting` after (`set` starts that over from another state): AC 26, with both settings 0 and agent-director's default settings, a bring-up refused by `ErrTmuxUnresponsive` starts the condition at its time and spawns again at each due time and never 1 ms early, each refused retry logging its re-armed line (`retry <n>: unavailable: … — re-armed, next retry in <s> s`) with the next wait, through `RESTART_FAILURE_CAP` plus the refusals to the ceiling plus two, counting nothing and never reaching `onCapReached`; along the way it posts one onset (`tmuxUnresponsiveOnsetText`) at the first retry at or past `TMUX_UNRESPONSIVE_ONSET_FLOOR_MS` and one alert (`tmuxUnresponsiveAlertText` over `adAlertThresholdMsInEffect`) 1 ms past the threshold, between two retries and none at it, each with one `posted` line, after which the retry timer is the only timer pending, at its due time, the first refusal's time is kept and every later retry keeps its schedule; once the refusal clears it launches once, the launch's spawn ending the condition after its onset with one recovery (`tmuxUnresponsiveRecoveryText`), and re-arms at the next wait in pending-only mode with the `launched` reason (`— re-armed in pending-only mode, next retry in <s> s`), and the next retry, a pending-only one, makes one `status` call for the persona and no other call and stops (`stopped (pending-only, row waiting) — …`) with nothing pending, nothing counted and no post beyond those three; AC 30's alert part, with both settings 0: after retries that each read the row `pending` (ending nothing) and post the onset at the first at or past the floor, the alert check pending beside the retry timer, a successful `read-pane` ends the condition with its ended line and one `recovery posted` line, posts one recovery, answers `kept` at the condition-end entry (one kept line, row pending), cancels the alert check and leaves the retry timer armed with its view unchanged; that retry then fires at its due time, past the alert check's, and no alert is ever posted, with nothing counted; a `test.each` over the row a retry finds (live and connected: stopped, one `status` call; live and not connected: a reconnect, no spawn, stopped; `pending`, not connected or connected with its stream: the liveness read defers it (b.jg5 SRJ-314), one `status` call, no reconnect and no spawn, never stopped with nothing left to recover, re-armed in full mode with the `pending-deferred` reason and its last row read `pending`; `ended` or missing: a kill and a spawn, re-armed in pending-only mode), each checking the stub's call counts, the whole view on a re-arm, the other persona still armed and no failure counted; with the harness's restart deps given the `pending` deferral as `main()` binds it (b.jg5 SRJ-314, SRJ-115), a retry that finds the row `pending` with a launch start and the session connected with its stream hands it, with its launch start, to the deferral once (one `status` call, one deferral line naming the launch start), with no kill, spawn, resume or send-keys, nothing counted, no cap and no trigger, re-arms at the next wait in full mode with the `pending-deferred` reason and its last row read `pending`, is kept through either condition end, and a following retry that finds the row live, connected and with its stream makes one `status` call and stops with nothing left to recover; a retry that finds the launch in flight (`holdSpawns`) makes no agent-director call and re-arms at the doubled wait with the `launch-in-flight` reason, and once the launch settles the next retry stops with nothing left to recover; a retry fired while the persona's serializer turn is held stays running with no call until the turn frees, then acts; a `test.each` over a liveness `status` answering a plain `Error`, `ErrCallTimeout` or a CONFIG answer (b.jg5 SRJ-314), with the harness's `armRetryTimer` arming the controller with the read-error cause: after a first retry that leaves the last row read `pending`, each retry up to `RESTART_FAILURE_CAP + 2` makes its one `status` call and nothing else, logs its re-armed line with the `liveness-unknown` reason and re-arms in full mode at the next wait with the last row read still `pending`, the arm hook called once per retry, nothing counted and the cap never reached; once `status` answers `ended`, the next retry kills and launches once and switches to pending-only mode. The stop rules that exist now (SRJ-305, AC 28) on the harness: the restart cap, where each counted launch failure (`ErrTmuxSessionCreate`) re-arms at the doubled wait with the `launch-failed` reason until the cap stops the timer, with one `onCapReached` and its notice, the other persona still armed; a retry queued behind restart work (a `runRestartRetry` whose held launch fails one failure short of the cap) answers capped from its entry once that failure reaches the cap: one relaunch line and one spawn in all, the count at `RESTART_FAILURE_CAP`, one `onCapReached` and one cap notice, one `Restart retry skipped for persona=<key> — the persona is at the restart cap; nothing killed or launched` line in `errors`, and the timer stopped with the capped line; a `test.each` over a persona not up (`setUp`), out of the applied configuration (`remove`) or already at the cap, whose next retry stops the timer with no agent-director call while the other persona's due time is kept and its retry proceeds; a trigger for a key no longer applied arms it, and its first retry stops it with no call; `teardown` stops the persona's timer at once, which never fires, the other staying armed; `shutdown` stops every timer, a later launch refused UNAVAILABLE logs one `not armed` line and is a plain failure, not marked refused (the closed controller's sink answers false), and nothing fires however far the clock goes. Pending-only mode (SRJ-301, SRJ-303, SRJ-305) on the harness's default action, armed directly through `controller.armPendingOnly`: a row still `pending` is armed at the base wait with one `armed in pending-only mode (pending-row)` line, cause `pending-row` and last row `pending`, and each of two retries logs its `retry <n> (pending-only) — reading its row` line, makes one `status` call for `cscb_<key>` and no other, and re-arms at the doubled wait with the `row-pending` reason, with no trigger, the other persona unarmed and nothing counted (both settings 0); a `waiting` row not connected gets one `status` call and no other, and the timer stops (`stopped (pending-only, row waiting)`); a `test.each` over `ended`, `missing` and no row stops the timer with the row-gone reason, then runs the restart decision once (three `status` calls, one kill, one spawn at the due time), with no trigger and nothing pending; a refused launch (`ErrTmuxUnresponsive`) in that run arms a fresh full-mode timer at the base wait through the trigger sink, after the stopped line; a launch refused UNAVAILABLE while pending-only promotes the timer to full mode with its due time kept (one `promoted to full mode (…) — its due time is kept` line, both causes recorded), and the next retry runs the full decision (one `status` call, the liveness read deferring the `pending` row, and the `pending-deferred` reason, not the pending-only `row-pending`); a pending-only arm while in full mode keeps the timer in full mode with its due time, logs nothing, records the `pending-row` cause and last row, and the next retry runs the full decision (one `status` call, `pending-deferred`); a full-mode retry whose launch succeeds re-arms in pending-only mode, the wait count carrying on, with `pending` as the last row read in the view, and once the launch's row reads `pending` the next retry makes one `status` call and re-arms at the doubled wait; a row read failing UNAVAILABLE (`ErrCallTimeout`) or GONE (`ErrTmuxCaptureFailed`, `read-error`) is a trigger inside the attempt that promotes the timer to full mode, the failed retry re-arms at the next wait (`re-armed in full mode`), and the next retry runs the full decision (connected: stopped, nothing left to recover); a pending-only retry while a launch is in flight (`holdSpawns`) makes no agent-director call and re-arms at the doubled wait with the `launch-in-flight` reason, the last row read `pending`, and once the launch settles the next retry makes its one read; a `test.each` over shutdown, teardown, not up, out of the applied configuration and at the restart cap (the last three at the next retry) stops a pending-only timer with no agent-director call for it and a pending-only stopped line, while the other persona, also armed pending-only, keeps retrying (one `status` call per retry, all its own, still pending-only with its one timer pending), except under shutdown, which stops it too with no call at all. The pending and kill-failure exceptions (SRJ-306), the condition-end entry `conditionEnded(key, condition)` called directly on the harness with no reading: with the last row read `pending` (armed pending-only directly, or a full-mode retry deferred on the pending row), the end of `tmux-unresponsive` and of `tmux-unavailable` each answer `kept` with one `kept — <condition ended>, but its row last read pending` line, the view and due time unchanged; a full-mode retry that finds the row ended and relaunches leaves the timer pending-only with the launch's `pending` row as its last read, kept through the end of either condition; a `test.each` over both conditions finds that with the row not pending and no other cause the entry stops the timer with that condition's reason, and with no timer armed answers `not-armed` and logs nothing; a timer armed `kill-failed` by `ErrTmuxKillFailed` inside a `runRestartRetry` run (refused, b.jg5 SRJ-105: one `status` and one `kill` call, nothing spawned, nothing counted) is kept by both condition ends (`a kill-failed cause is recorded`), then stopped by each of nothing left to recover (its row reading `waiting` and the persona connected), the restart cap, teardown and shutdown; a full-mode retry whose kill fails with `ErrTmuxKillFailed` launches nothing (b.jg5 SRJ-105: one `status` and one `kill` call, nothing spawned or counted) and re-arms in full mode with a line naming the `kill-failed` cause, both causes recorded; the next retry, whose kill answers and whose launch succeeds, is not switched either: it re-arms in full mode with the `launched` reason, its last row read `pending`, is kept through both condition ends (row pending and kill-failed), and, once the persona is connected, its next retry, a full-mode one, makes one `status` call and stops with nothing left to recover. The switch, the last row read and a condition end during a run (SRJ-301, SRJ-306), on the bare controller with a held stand-in action: a `launched` answer takes the switch to pending-only mode with no other cause, and stays in full mode with a `kill-failed` cause recorded before the run or another arm during it, its last row read `pending` either way and the next retry run in that mode; `armPendingOnly` on a full-mode timer during a run, then an `again` with no row, leaves the last row read `pending` (a condition end keeps the timer); during a run, then an `again` with its own row, the answer's row wins (a condition end stops it); before the run, then an `again` with no row, clears the last row (a condition end stops it); before the run, then an `again` with no row that keeps the last row read (`keepsLastRow`, a liveness reading of `unknown`), leaves it `pending` (a condition end keeps the timer). A condition end during a run answers `deferred` and logs nothing, and is applied once the run has answered: stopped with the condition's reason and nothing pending after an `again` with no row and after an `again` with row `waiting`; kept, one kept line and then the re-armed line, after a `launched` answer (re-armed in pending-only mode), after a failed run with the row last read `pending` (armed pending-only) and after an `again` with a `kill-failed` cause recorded; moot, only the stopped line, after a `stop` answer and after a stop during the run (its `again` answer dropped); two ends during one run apply the latest condition once: kept after a `launched` answer, a later run applying none, and stopped with the latest condition's reason after an `again` with no row. An arm after a deferred end in the same run cancels that end and its reading: on the bare controller, an end then an arm during one run leaves the timer re-armed at its doubled wait with no kept or stopped line; on the harness, a retry whose reconnect `read-pane` succeeds (the end deferred) and whose `send-keys` is then refused re-arms, the condition holding. A stop's hand-off, and a pending-only stop that yields to a full-mode cause (SRJ-301, SRJ-303, SRJ-305, SRJ-306), on the bare controller (the hand-off cases with a scripted stop answer, the rest over `createFullModeRetryAction` on stand-in deps, armed pending-only): a hand-off that throws or rejects is logged once, redacted, after the stopped line (`hand-off after the stop failed`), the run settling with nothing pending and the next arm asking for the first wait; a pending-only retry that reads its row live (`waiting`), gone (`ended`) or absent while a full-mode cause is armed during it is not stopped: one promoted line, then a re-arm in full mode naming that cause, its last row read the stop's row and nothing handed off, and the next retry runs the full decision (one entry call, no row read); with a condition end deferred to that retry (row `ended`), the overridden stop counts as `again`, so the condition's rule stops the timer, and the held hand-off then runs once, after the stopped line with the timer no longer armed; with a `kill-failed` cause landing in it instead, the deferred end is kept (one kept line), the timer re-armed in full mode and nothing handed off; each early stop (shutting down, not applied, not up, at the restart cap) is taken even with a full-mode cause armed during the pending-only retry: one promoted line, then that stopped line, with no row read and no entry call. The `tmux-unresponsive` condition's ends and the retry timer (SRJ-305, SRJ-306, SRJ-310), on the harness's default action with the condition wired as `main()` wires it, each end of a holding condition reaching the condition-end entry once (`conditionEnds`), `assertNoLeak` over `captured()` after each case: a `test.each` over a full-mode retry whose restart decision finds the persona already connected (reading `live`) and a pending-only retry whose row read finds it live out of `pending` (`waiting`), connected with its stream, ends the condition with that reading and the timer stops; a `test.each` over a full-mode retry that reads its row `pending` (deferred) or live but not connected (its reconnect deferred), and a pending-only retry that reads it `pending` or live but not connected (the pending-only row rule stops the timer, not a condition end), leaves the condition holding with its first refusal's time and never reaches the entry; a full-mode retry whose own launch succeeds ends it through that spawn with its `pending` row, so the timer is kept and runs on in pending-only mode with the wait count carrying on; a start-pass launch whose kill of a live row in another directory is refused UNAVAILABLE stops there (b.jg5 SRJ-105: no delete, no spawn), ends `failed` with the refusal marker and counts nothing, leaves the condition holding from the refusal's time with no condition end and no kept line, and arms the timer at the base wait with no row read; AC 30's `tmux-unresponsive` half: an end through a successful `read-pane` while the row was last read `pending` leaves the timer armed with its due time; `tickEnd`'s `live` reading stops the timer although a retry last read the row `pending`, the tick's reading being the latest row read; a tick's end during a full-mode retry whose launch then succeeds is deferred with its `live` reading, and the launch's `pending` row is the later read, so the timer is kept and runs on in pending-only mode; a `test.each` over a tick's end deferred during a retry that fails or answers a liveness reading of `unknown`, after a retry read the row `pending`: the answer carries no row, so the `live` reading is the latest read and the timer stops with no kept line; a launch's successful spawn during a retry ends the condition with its `pending` reading, deferred, and it wins over the live row the retry's answer carries, so the timer is kept (one kept line, then re-armed in full mode, last row read `pending`); a `test.each` over a successful `read-pane` (no reading) and `tickEnd` (`live`) finds that a condition ending after a kill-failure cause (a `runRestartRetry` whose kill fails `ErrTmuxKillFailed` and which launches nothing, then a retry whose kill answers and whose launch is refused UNAVAILABLE) leaves the timer armed with its due time. The retry observer (SRJ-308), on the bare controller over a scripted or held action (the file-local `makeRig` takes an optional `onRetryFire`, passed only when given), and for the in-flight skip over `createFullModeRetryAction` on stand-in deps: `onRetryFire` is called exactly once per retry with the clock's time at the retry, after its retry line and before its action, in full mode and, for a pending row or a live row, in pending-only mode; each persona's retry with its own key and due time; once for a retry skipped for work in flight (the full-mode entry answering in-flight, or the pending-only in-flight predicate), before the action answers `launch-in-flight`; once for a retry whose timer fired while the last run, outliving a stop, was in flight, when it runs after that run settles; never for a timer stopped by `stop`, `stopAll`, `close` or a condition end before its retry, with nothing run or pending; and a `test.each` over four scenarios (full mode refused or throwing at every retry, a launch switching to pending-only then a live-row stop, pending-only then a gone-row stop with its hand-off) finds a throwing observer swallowed, observed at every retry and logging no line of its error, leaving the retry lines, actions, re-arms and stops as they are on a rig with no observer |

The trigger sink (b.jg5 SRJ-301), over a recording fake sink (no timer; it answers true, as the controller's `arm` does) with each wrapped call run inside a real attempt context (`runInAttempt`): inside P's attempt, UNAVAILABLE from `resume` (`withSpawnDetection`), `read-pane` or `status`, `ErrTmuxKillFailed` from `kill`, and a `status`, `get` or `list` read error each call the sink once with P, the cause kind and the error itself, the attempt recording it armed; `ErrSpawnNotFound` from `get` or `status`, a CONFIG `status` answer, an UNUSABLE NAME `get` answer, a STATE error from `kill` and an ENVIRONMENT error from `resume` call no sink and are recorded unarmed. A call for P inside Q's attempt, or Q's inside P's, calls no sink and records nothing; one for P inside Q's attempt nested in P's arms P only, recorded in P's attempt; outside any attempt, or after P's attempt settles, nothing is armed. With no sink an arming error is recorded unarmed and rethrown unchanged; a sink that throws leaves the call's own error rethrown, recorded unarmed, as does a sink answering false, nothing (void) or a truthy non-boolean. For an arming flag-raising error (`ErrSystemInstallDisappeared`), an arming error with no flag (`ErrTmuxKillFailed`) and a non-arming flag-raising one (`ErrTmuxNotAvailable`), the rethrown value, flags and notices are exactly those of a run with no sink and no attempt. A token in the error text reaches no notice, attempt record, cause kind or flag (`assertNoLeak`). Every wrapped call declares its verb (an `AdCall`), so `fn` gets the installed client itself, inside P's attempt and outside any; a success inside one clears the flags as outside. `reportAgentDirectorError`: inside P's attempt, UNAVAILABLE from any verb, `ErrTmuxKillFailed` and a `status` error each call the sink once with P and the cause, recorded armed; a non-arming error, a `status` error from an undeclared call, an arming error inside Q's attempt and a call outside any attempt call none; with no sink, or a sink answering false or nothing (void), it records the error unarmed; before `initOutageState` or with a throwing sink it does not throw; it raises no flag and posts no notice, even for a flag-raising error. The declared verb and the condition sink (b.jg5 SRJ-307, SRJ-310), over a recording condition sink beside a recording trigger sink: a pin case finds `TMUX_TOUCHING_VERBS` is the SRD glossary's six (`kill`, `pause`, `read-pane`, `resume`, `send-keys`, `spawn`); the call tables run through `withOutageDetection` only (`withSpawnDetection` delegates to it), plus one `withSpawnDetection` row in the start table (`spawn`) and one in the success table (`resume`): for each tmux-touching call (`kill` as `AD_CALL_KILL_ROW_READ_LIVE`), each UNAVAILABLE form (`ErrTmuxUnresponsive`, `ErrCallTimeout`, an unknown name, CSCB's `UnknownError` wrapper, a plain `Error`; built by the shared `unavailableForms`) inside P's attempt starts P's condition once with the declared verb, with no end, flag or notice, and `ErrTmuxKillFailed` starts nothing and arms as before; a success, or each GONE answer (`ErrTmuxSendKeys`, `ErrTmuxCaptureFailed`), inside P's attempt or outside any ends P's condition once with no start, the result or error unchanged; for each call that is not tmux-touching (`status`, `get`, `list`, `find-missing`, `delete`, a kill of a row not read live) an UNAVAILABLE starts and ends nothing while the attempt still records the declared verb, and a success or GONE ends nothing; a tmux-touching UNAVAILABLE outside any attempt, inside Q's attempt or after P's attempt settled starts nothing, and one for P inside Q's attempt nested in P's starts P's only; `reportAgentDirectorError` inside P's attempt starts the condition for a tmux-touching UNAVAILABLE and not for a `status` or `find-missing` one, an undeclared call or `ErrTmuxKillFailed`; over seven scenarios (starts, ends, a flag raised, a kill failure, a success clearing flags) the rethrown value or result, flags, notices and attempt record are the same with no condition sink, a recording one or a throwing one; a token in the error text reaches no notice, attempt record or flag, and the sink gets the error value itself |

Apply bring-up (b.av2 SR-6.1, SR-6.2): storage check, bring-up, launch only when up, awaited; failing launch or storage check logged token-safely. Serialization (b.av2 SR-6.6): a teardown waits for the key's launch in flight, for its restart work (real `initRestart`) and for a launch after a Slack retry (real bring-up controller), cancels a pending restart timer, and never delays another persona; beside the real restart module, the real UNAVAILABLE retry controller on a fake clock, with A's and B's timers armed and never fired and `stopRetryTimer` bound to its `stop` with the torn-down reason, finds B's timer stopped once and A's still the only one pending after B's teardown, B's stopped at submit when B is still applied and left for its turn when removed; a teardown of a still-applied B submitted while a `runRestartRetry` for B is in flight starts only after that work settles, and B ends with no retry timer while A's stays armed. Slack validation, both checks always made (exit 1, reported per key, no file): `ok:false` from either or both, `ok:false` with a nested `"ok":true`, a body whose leading field is not `"ok":true`, curl unable to connect (exit 6), an empty body, a timeout after part of an ok body, `ok:false` with no error code, and an error field echoing the token (reported as `unexpected response`); a reply passes only when its leading field is `"ok":true`. The file's other local helpers: `deferredAnswer` (a resolve answer the test settles by hand), `trackAnswers` (each trigger waiter's answer as it settles, `undefined` until then, so a case can tell an answer given at once from one that waits on the call), `NOT_RUNNING` (the not-running answer, built from the exported kind), `WELL_PAST_INTERVAL_AND_LIMIT` (a clock advance well past both the interval and the time limit, from the exported constants), `expectNothingAfterTheCall` (the rig's only event is its one call: no record, stop, log line, tick or version change, and no timer), `expectNextRecheckArmed`, `expectStoppedOnce`, `expectCouldNotRunLineNaming` (the line is `buildAdVersionRecheckCouldNotRunLine` of the error's `errName` and never carries its message) and `installDeps` (deps for the module-level install over a recording stub resolve). `afterEach` disposes every rig, resets the module's installed re-check, its tick and version-changed listeners and the client singleton, and removes the temp directories. Schedule: `AD_VERSION_RECHECK_INTERVAL_MS` is 120 s and `AD_VERSION_RECHECK_TIME_LIMIT_MS` is positive and shorter; building the re-check arms and calls nothing; `start` calls nothing at once and arms one timer at the interval; nothing is called just under the interval and one call at it; N intervals give N calls, one timer pending after each; a second `start` is a logged no-op. A version that passes (the floor, its release candidate, a later release): no record or stop, it becomes the last version seen, the next call one interval later. A version below the floor (`OLD_AD_VERSION`, the development placeholder, `DEV_UNPARSEABLE_VERSION`): record then stop, each once, label `ad-below-phase1-floor`, `AD_VERSION_RECHECK_STOP_EXIT_CODE` (non-zero), no timer left; the message names the version found, the floor, the binary path, `RUNTIME_RECHECK_PHRASE` and `PHASE1_RUNBOOK_SECTION_TITLE`, carries no install-skill block and matches none of the `UPGRADE_FORMS`; the last version seen is unchanged. The client's too-old error: the same single stop with label `ad-system-install-too-old`, the message naming the error's two versions, the floor, the path, the phrase and the runbook title and ending with the install-skill block; it is recognised by its `errName`, so a plain object carrying it is decided the same way. A re-check that cannot run (unreachable for a timed-out probe or an unparseable version, not found, Bun too old, a plain error, a synchronous throw): no record, no stop, the last version seen unchanged, one timer re-armed, and the next interval calls again; a rejection with a value the description cannot name (a thrown string carrying a fake token, `null`, a plain object with no name, an error whose `errName` is token-shaped) is could-not-run the same way: no record, no stop, one timer re-armed, and one line starting with `AD_VERSION_RECHECK_COULD_NOT_RUN_LOG_PREFIX` with every credential redacted (`assertNoLeak`). Dispose: before the first tick and after one, no further call or tick runs after any advance, dispose is idempotent and a later `start` arms nothing; after a dispose before `start`, `start` is a logged no-op; during a timed or a triggered call in flight, any trigger waiter is answered not-running before the call settles and the later below-floor answer records, stops, logs, ticks and signals nothing, with no timer left; with a trigger joined to a timed re-check, or two triggers, waiting on a call that never settles, dispose answers every waiter not-running at once, with no clock advance, nothing is re-armed, fired, logged, ticked or signalled, even well past the interval and the limit, and a later trigger answers not-running without a call. Tick hook: a listener runs once per timed re-check after it finishes (on a pass and on could-not-run, the time limit included) with the next re-check already armed (the stop and dispose cases above show it runs on neither); a listener that throws or rejects is logged once per tick, the listeners after it still run and the next re-check is armed. Read failures, through the injected `PersonaConfigFs` seam with stubbed calls: not found gives the defaults and no line; a directory (`EISDIR`), a non-regular file (`CONFIG_NOT_REGULAR_FILE_CODE`), a file over `MAX_RELOAD_FILE_BYTES` (`MAX_RELOAD_FILE_SIZE_TEXT`), `EACCES`, `ENOTDIR`, an `EIO` on the read and bytes that are not valid UTF-8 inside a TOML comment (`it is not valid UTF-8`; decoded leniently the file would be valid TOML) are each refused with the defaults kept and one line, the reason and the line each giving that cause, and the stub errors carry a fake token that `assertNoLeak` checks never reaches the line or the outcome (the line holds no stub error text); a home getter that throws after an accepted read is refused (reason `the home directory cannot be found`) with the last values kept and one line naming the settings path under `~`, and the thrown error's fake token never reaches the line or the outcome (`assertNoLeak`); on the real file system, a directory at the settings path after an accepted read is refused and keeps the last values. Runs of refused reads: refused reads with different reasons log once; refused, accepted, refused logs twice and the accepted read takes effect; a change is used from the next read, not before; a file removed after an accepted read gives the defaults at the next read with no line. The 64-bit bound: per key (`test.each` over `AD_TMUX_KEYS`), a `[tmux]` value at `AD_SETTING_INTEGER_MAX` is accepted and used with no line; all nine keys and `[pause] timeout_seconds` at `AD_SETTING_INTEGER_MAX` together are accepted with no line (the grace minimum stays exact at the bound); per key, a value one above it after an accepted read is refused as too large, the last values kept, with one line whose reason names the table and the key. `[pause] timeout_seconds`: no file, no `[pause]` table and a `[pause]` table without the key give the default; a positive integer and `AD_SETTING_INTEGER_MAX` are used; 0, a negative value (each kept as `found`), a string, a float, `pause` given as a value and an integer above `AD_SETTING_INTEGER_MAX` (kept as `found` `an integer too large for agent-director`) are `not-used` while the nine `[tmux]` values are accepted with no line; a refused read keeps the last accepted pause value. Install and re-read, with E3's re-check installed on `createFakeClock()` over a passing `makeStubResolveSystemBinary` and no health check: with nothing installed `adSettingsInEffect()` answers the defaults; the production home getter (`productionAdSettingsHome`) answers `process.env.HOME`, not the launch-time `os.homedir()`; `installAdSettings` reads once at once, arms no timer and the accessor answers what it read; a change takes effect at the first tick (`AD_VERSION_RECHECK_INTERVAL_MS`), not a millisecond before; refused ticks keep the values and log once; a tick whose re-check could not run (`errSystemInstallNotFound`) still re-reads; a stopping tick (`OLD_AD_VERSION`) does not; nothing is read after `disposeAdVersionRecheck()`; a second install writes one line, returns the installed reader and never reads. Leak safety: text that is not TOML with a fake token on its offending line and a `[tmux]` string value holding a fake token are each refused with one line, and a `[pause]` string value holding a fake token leaves `[pause] timeout_seconds` `not-used`; in each, the fake token reaches no line, outcome or value in effect (`assertNoLeak`). Source audits over comment-stripped `src/`: only `ad-settings.ts` imports `smol-toml`; following its runtime imports transitively through `src/` (type-only imports excluded), the walk reaches more than one module with `smol-toml` among the packages loaded, and it loads neither the session manager nor the `agent-director` package, a client, outage-state, notifier or Slack module; `DIALOG_READY_TIMEOUT_MS` is declared only in `src/ad-settings.ts`, as an exported const, and `session-manager.ts` imports it from `./ad-settings.ts`; no other `src/` module holds one of the nine key names as a string literal. The derived waits (b.jg5 SRJ-210), G (`adGraceMs`), the alert threshold (`adAlertThresholdMs`) and B (`adLaunchBoundMs`), each checked against the file's own expected values computed from the exported addends and `DIALOG_READY_TIMEOUT_MS` (the approver's cap, imported from `src/ad-settings.ts`): the defaults (no file); `pending_grace_seconds`, `stopping_window_seconds` and `starting_session_seconds` all written; `starting_session_seconds` the longer window, `stopping_window_seconds` the longer window, the two windows equal, and G plus B's addend exactly the approver's cap; and, as a second `test.each`, below the cap B is `DIALOG_READY_TIMEOUT_MS` and above it B is G plus the addend. The waits at check time: with nothing installed the accessors (`adGraceMsInEffect`, `adAlertThresholdMsInEffect`, `adLaunchBoundMsInEffect`) answer the waits of the defaults; after an accepted read at a re-check tick they answer the new waits (all three changed), not a millisecond before; a refused read at a tick (one refused form, one line) leaves them answering the last accepted waits. A derived wait beyond the timer maximum (`MAX_TIMER_DELAY_MS`), on fake clocks the `afterEach` checks: the hazard, a plain timer of a G above twice the maximum firing after 1 ms on the same clock; `armNeverEarlyWait` armed at the start and armed after the start (past the maximum) does not fire past the maximum, fires once exactly at start plus G, and never has more than one pending timer or one asking more than the maximum; never-ending waits (`AD_WAIT_NEVER_ENDS`): `pending_grace_seconds` at `AD_SETTING_INTEGER_MAX` and one above the largest grace whose milliseconds a number holds exactly (G and B never end), at that largest exact grace (G exact, B never ends), and `stopping_window_seconds` or `starting_session_seconds` at `AD_SETTING_INTEGER_MAX` (the threshold never ends); a never-ending wait arms no timer and never runs the callback; cancel mid-chain leaves no timer pending and the callback never runs, and a second cancel does nothing; cancel after the callback ran does nothing (another timer on the clock stays pending); timers that fire early (a clock that halves every delay) never run the callback before the deadline. `armNeverEarlyWait` arming: a NaN, infinite or negative infinite start, a NaN wait and a wait getter whose first value is NaN each throw a `RangeError` and arm nothing; a wait getter that throws at the arm throws that same error out of the arm and arms nothing; a wait of 0, a deadline already passed and a getter whose deadline already passed at the arm do not run the callback during the arm and run it once from the next timer. `armNeverEarlyWait` with a wait getter, read at every fire (a getter the test steers, on fake clocks the `afterEach` checks): raised while armed, to a longer wait under the timer maximum and to one beyond it, the callback does not run at the old deadline and runs once exactly at the new one; lowered between fires to a deadline already passed, the wait ends at the next fire, not before; lowered to a deadline after the next fire, the next timer is armed for it and the wait ends exactly there; a getter at `AD_WAIT_NEVER_ENDS` keeps one timer of `MAX_TIMER_DELAY_MS` pending and never runs the callback, and lowering it later ends the wait at the next fire; cancel mid-chain with a getter at `AD_WAIT_NEVER_ENDS` leaves no timer pending and the callback never runs, even after the getter is lowered; a getter that calls the cancel while it runs at a fire (a two-row `test.each`: the deadline already passed at that fire, and not yet passed) stops the wait: the callback never runs, the getter is not read again and no timer is left pending; a getter that answers NaN or throws at a fire throws nothing and logs nothing (console spied), re-arms one timer of the maximum and ends the wait once a valid value returns; wired to `adGraceMsInEffect` over the installed re-check, an accepted read at a tick raising `pending_grace_seconds` mid-wait ends the wait exactly at the raised G, not the armed one. Whole minutes, rounded down (`wholeMinutes` against `Math.floor`): the threshold at the defaults and less one second, B at the defaults, the threshold with `starting_session_seconds` at twice its minimum, one millisecond under a minute, and a wait that never ends. The verb ceilings (b.jg5 SRJ-213; ADSRD SR-13.2), each `adVerbCeilingsMs` value checked against the file's own paths written out from the record's Q, A, C, W, E and B: per verb of `AD_CEILING_VERBS` (`test.each`), the ceiling equals its formula at the defaults, with Q raised (a query timeout derived from the defaults, above E - W and 2A + W) and with every value changed; for each two-path verb (`kill`, `resume`, `spawn-with-reuse`, `plain-spawn`) the first path sets the ceiling at the defaults and the second with Q raised; `pause` is `send-keys`' ceiling plus the `[pause] timeout_seconds` wait (the default wait, a wait of 60, and no wait for an unused 0); a plain spawn's ceiling, and a reuse's, equals `resume`'s at the defaults, with Q raised and with `create_timeout_ms` 40000; `expire` is the one verb CSCB does not call. The need (`adCallTimeoutNeed`: the largest ceiling among the verbs CSCB calls plus `AD_CALL_TIMEOUT_NEED_MARGIN_MS`), each from values read from a file and checked against the file's own formula: set by `pause` at the defaults; by `resume` (the launch ceiling a reuse and a plain spawn share) with `create_timeout_ms` 40000 and `pending_grace_seconds` 61; by `pause` with `[pause] timeout_seconds` 60; by `find-missing`, never `expire`, with a sweep budget derived to make the sweep's ceiling the largest; and, per unused `[pause] timeout_seconds` form (0, a negative value, a string, a float), by the unused value (`unused-pause-value` with its `found`), `pause` counted without a wait. The startup check (`checkAdCallTimeoutAtStartup`, through a capturing log sink and injected values; each line it writes starts with `AD_SETTINGS_LOG_PREFIX`, `agent_director_call_timeout_ms` (typed against `ServerSettings`) and the setting's value, names the need in ms and `PHASE1_RUNBOOK_SECTION_TITLE`, and is what the check returns): at the defaults the default setting gives no line; a setting 1 ms below the need and one equal to it each give one line naming the `pause` ceiling and the margin, and one 1 ms above gives none; `create_timeout_ms` 40000 and `pending_grace_seconds` 61 with the default setting give one line naming the launch ceiling by its three verbs, `resume/spawn-with-reuse/plain-spawn`; per unused pause form, the default setting (above the need) and a setting equal to the need each give exactly one line naming the unused value and no verb's ceiling; on the installed values (one case with a verb line, one with an unused-value line) the check throws nothing, calls no `process.exit`, `setTimeout` or `setInterval`, changes no value in effect and arms no timer, and two re-check ticks (`AD_VERSION_RECHECK_INTERVAL_MS`) re-read the file without running the check again; a log sink that throws still leaves the check returning its line, and values that cannot be read give no line and no throw; a `[pause]` string value holding a fake token gives a line naming the unused value that holds no token (`assertNoLeak`). The file's one SRD pin table (a single `test.each` in `ad settings: SRD pins (b.jg5 SRJ-209, SRJ-210, SRJ-213)`) holds SRJ-209's, SRJ-210's and SRJ-213's numbers against the exports: the settings path, the nine defaults, the pause default, the fixed minimums and the grace minimum's floor and addend, the grace minimum at the default timers and with `create_timeout_ms` 40000, the SRD's refused and accepted examples, `60.0` refused, and the 64-bit bound (the largest setting integer, 2^63 - 1, with `sweep_budget_seconds` accepted at it and refused one above), the alert threshold addend (60 s), B's addend over G (60 s), the approver's cap as B's floor (300000 ms), G, the threshold and B at the defaults (60 s, 360 s, 300 s) and with `pending_grace_seconds` 120, `stopping_window_seconds` 30 and `starting_session_seconds` 120 (120 s, 180 s, 300 s), B with `pending_grace_seconds` 300 (360 s), whole minutes: the threshold at the defaults 6, B at the defaults 5, 359 s 5, 180 s 3 and 59.999 s 0; the ceilings at the defaults (`kill` 12.4 s, `read-pane` 6.9 s, `send-keys` 9 s, `pause` 9 s plus 30 s, `resume`, reuse and a plain spawn 10.9 s, `find-missing` and `expire` 16.6 s), the need's margin (15 s), and the need at the defaults (54 s, set by `pause`), with `create_timeout_ms` 40000 and `pending_grace_seconds` 61 (60.9 s, `setBy` `resume`, the launch row's tie-winner) and with `[pause] timeout_seconds` 60 (84 s, set by `pause`) |

Spawns go to a recording fake; nothing runs docker. New features that add significant logic should get their own test file (e.g., `session-manager.test.ts`).

## Isolation (SR-13.2)

Tests never touch the real home or a real install. A dev-tree test once ran against the real home and caused a production outage.

- **Temp directories only.** Every file a test reads or writes (config, credentials, state directory, crontable, logs, working directories, agent-director store) sits under the test's own `mkdtempSync` directory, passed explicitly, and removed in `afterEach`.
- **agent-director's settings file.** A test writes `config.toml` only through `writeAgentDirectorConfig` (`tests/test-helpers/ad-settings.ts`) under a temp HOME, never as a hand-written TOML literal and never under the real `~/.agent-director`, and reads it through a reader whose home getter answers that HOME. It takes every settings value (defaults, minimums, key names, the path, refused forms) from `src/ad-settings.ts` or the helper, never typed; the SRD's numbers appear only in the pin table of `tests/ad-settings.test.ts`.
- **Never the real home.** No test reads or writes `~/.claude`, `~/.claude/channels/slack`, `~/.agent-director` or `~/.npm`, and no path in a fixture resolves to them. Code that falls back to `os.homedir()` is tested through `runInFakeHome` (see Credentials Fixtures and Leak Checks); code that takes a home or `SLACK_STATE_DIR` gets a temp one. `runPostinstall` falls back to the OS home (and so the real `~/.claude/skills`) when `homeDir` is unset, so `tests/postinstall.test.ts` calls it only through its `postinstall()` wrapper, which refuses options without `homeDir`, with paths from `makeSandbox()` (home, state dir and MCP path under one `mkdtempSync` root).
- **No real server or CLI.** No test calls `main()`, runs `src/server.ts` or `src/cli.ts` as an entry point, or starts a real daemon in-process. The one exception is the usage-text tests in `tests/cli.test.ts` (`unknown subcommand`, and the two `credentials` cases that stop before the script: a usage error and an unknown persona), which run `src/cli.ts` in a child process through the file's `runCli` helper. `runCli` starts Bun by absolute path (`process.execPath`) with its `env` a direct `hostSafeChildEnv` call (see `host-safe-env.ts` below): the temp HOME, no tool, so `PATH` is `EMPTY_CHILD_PATH` and names no directory, its own `TMUX_TMPDIR`, and as extras `SLACK_STATE_DIR` and the fixed `BUN_RUNTIME_TRANSPILER_CACHE_PATH=0`, so Bun writes no transpiler cache under the temp HOME for a tree snapshot to see. Nothing else comes from `process.env`. It has a 15 s time limit and checks that the child spawned and was not killed by it. These cases never reach a subcommand that acts. A new test that spawns the CLI uses `runCli`. The CLI is otherwise tested through `createCli` with injected `CliDeps`; `server.ts` through its importable modules (see Importing server.ts).
- **Inject the `claude_config_dir` check.** The bring-up controller's default `checkConfigDir` resolves an unset `claude_config_dir` against the real OS home's `.claude`, so a dangling real `~/.claude` would hold every persona. Every controller a test builds injects `checkConfigDir`: `checkPersonaConfigDir(persona, { home })` over a scratch home holding a real `.claude` (the `configDirCheckUnder(base)` helpers in `tests/persona-lifecycle.test.ts`, `tests/registry.test.ts`, `tests/health-check.test.ts` and `tests/persona-relaunch-gate.test.ts`; `tests/persona-connections.test.ts`; the reload harness without a real launch), or `checkLaunchConfigDir` where the suite already set the session manager's spawn home (`tests/session-manager.test.ts`, `tests/restart.test.ts`, `tests/persona-bringup.test.ts`, the reload harness with a real launch). `tests/session-manager.test.ts` sets a scratch default spawn home (`_setSpawnHomeDir` over a `default-home` fixture holding a real `.claude`) in `beforeEach`, so no launch resolves against the process home; a test that needs its own replaces it with `useSpawnHome`.
- **CLI log seams.** `openLogAppend`, `closeFd` and `initLogging` are `CliDeps`, so CLI tests inject fakes: no real descriptor is opened and `console` is never taken over. To see which lines reach the log and which the terminal, inject an `initLogging` that swaps `console.error` for a recorder and restore it afterwards. The agent-director calls are built by the exported `createDirectorOps(getClient)`; test it by passing a stub client, not by installing a Client singleton.
- **Fake tokens only.** See the rules under Credentials Fixtures and Leak Checks.

### Host safety (b.jg5 SRJ-1301)

Host `bun test` never executes an agent-director binary. The client's discovery (`Client.create()`, `resolveSystemBinary()`) finds the host's real install on `PATH` or at the standard install path under `~/.agent-director`, and a real client opens its store. Redirecting HOME alone moves neither: `PATH` still names the install, and `src/` code that calls `os.homedir()` still sees the launch-time home.

- **Every client is a stub.** A test that needs an agent-director client gets it from `tests/test-helpers/agent-director-stub.ts`: a `makeStubClient()` installed with `setClientForTests`, or a seam filled with `makeStubCreateClient(...)` (the start gate's `createClient`) or `makeStubResolveSystemBinary(...)`. `makeCloseCountingStubClient(opts?)` gives a `makeStubClient(opts)` stub whose `close()` only counts its calls (`{ client, closes() }`), for a case that checks which client was closed and how often; `makePassingGateDeps(overrides?)` gives the start gate's seams for a run that passes every step (a default `makeStubCreateClient()`, the three API-surface probes passing, an ENOENT state.db stat, UID 1000, a swallowing startup-error recorder and an `exit` that throws), with `overrides` winning over every default. `makeStubClient`'s default `binaryVersion` is `PHASE1_RC_VERSION` (from `tests/test-helpers/agent-director-versions.ts`), which passes CSCB's Phase 1 floor; a test that needs an older or development binary sets `binaryVersion` explicitly, taking the value from that helper. `tests/install-check.test.ts` passes `runInstallCheck` a `makeStubResolveSystemBinary(...)` resolver, and `tests/postinstall.test.ts` passes the probe an injected install check answering canned results; neither mocks `agent-director`.
- **No value import of `Client` or `resolveSystemBinary`.** No test file or test helper holds either as a value from `agent-director`: no named import or re-export (aliased or not), no `export *`, and no read through the module namespace (a property or element read, destructuring, a spread or rest copy, a default or `require` binding, a dynamic `import()`). There is no exception, not even inside `expect(...)`: an identity assertion on `ad.resolveSystemBinary` is a read. Type-only imports (`import type`, an inline `type`) are allowed, and so is spreading the namespace, or a copy of it, into a `mock.module` factory (`{ ...REAL, resolveSystemBinary: stub }`) or comparing a copy with the live namespace entry by entry (see Module Mocks).
- **Every child's `env` is a direct `hostSafeChildEnv` call.** Every child-process call in a test file or test helper (the `child_process` functions, `fork` included, however imported; `Bun.spawn`, `Bun.spawnSync` and `$`) passes an options object literal with exactly one `env`, and that `env` is a direct call of `hostSafeChildEnv` imported from `host-safe-env.ts` (see the helper list under Factory Functions). A `Bun.$` template ends in `.env(hostSafeChildEnv(...))`. A child-process function referenced without being called (put in a deps object, re-exported) is refused: the audit can't follow it.
- **The preload guard.** The `[test]` preload of `bunfig.toml` and of `tests/bunfig.toml` loads `tests/test-helpers/host-safety-preload.ts` once, before any test file, when `bun test` starts in the repository root or in `tests/`. Bun reads only the `bunfig.toml` in the directory the run starts in (no upward search) and resolves a relative preload path against that directory, so each file names the guard relative to its own directory, and every entry starts with `./`, `../` or `/` (a bare `a/b.ts` is looked up as a package). The guard computes its variables with `preloadRedirectedEnv(process.env, home, tmuxTmpDir)` from `host-safe-env.ts`, which is pure, and applies them to `process.env`: each of `PRELOAD_ENV_NAMES` is set or deleted, nothing else is touched. Its only writes to disk are the new HOME directory and, when the run inherited no valid one, the `TMUX_TMPDIR` directory, both directly under the OS temp directory (`osTempDir()`, the normalized `os.tmpdir()`, so a `TMPDIR` such as `/tmp//` names the same directory as `/tmp`):
  - `HOME`: a new, empty directory directly under the OS temp directory, named with `PRELOAD_HOME_PREFIX`. Every test file shares it; it is never removed, so each run leaves one.
  - `PATH`: the inherited absolute entries, in order, duplicates dropped, without every directory that holds an `agent-director` entry; `EMPTY_CHILD_PATH` when none is left.
  - `TMUX` and `TMUX_PANE`: unset. `TMUX_TMPDIR`: the process's one fenced directory from `childTmuxTmpDir()` (the inherited one when valid, else a new one), the same one `hostSafeChildEnv` hands every child, so a run makes at most one. It is never removed.
  - `SLACK_STATE_DIR`: `<new HOME>/.claude/channels/slack` (not created). It is set rather than unset because the state-directory resolvers fall back to `os.homedir()`.

  It then runs `preloadCheckFailures(process.env)`, the one check shared with `tests/host-safety.test.ts`, and throws, failing the run before any test file loads, when it reports anything. The message names the failed checks by their `PRELOAD_CHECK` labels and HOME, never the environment. No file imports the guard. With the guard in place, a test that forgot its stub fails as not found (`ErrSystemInstallNotFound`, the class label `AD_SYSTEM_INSTALL_NOT_FOUND` exported from `src/install-check.ts`) instead of reaching the host's install.
- **HOME under the preload.** Bun fixes `os.homedir()` at start-up from the HOME the run was launched with, so the preload moves only what reads the environment (the client's discovery, the `SLACK_STATE_DIR` resolvers). `src/` code that calls `homedir()` directly, such as the start gate's `state.db` path, still sees the launch-time HOME. So every run still starts with a scratch HOME (`tests/README.md`), and code that falls back to `os.homedir()` is tested through `runInFakeHome`.
- **What a test may change.** A test never widens what the guard set: it never sets HOME or `TMUX` process-wide and never puts back a `PATH` directory the guard removed. It may narrow `PATH` (a subset of the guard's entries, or a scratch directory of the test's own that holds no `agent-director`) and set `TMUX_TMPDIR` and `SLACK_STATE_DIR`, and restores each to the guard's value in `afterEach`, as `tests/stop-hook-bootstrap.test.ts` (`PATH`, `SLACK_STATE_DIR`) and `tests/host-safety.test.ts` (`PATH`, `TMUX_TMPDIR`) do (see also Credentials Fixtures and Leak Checks).
