One exception: a host-safety refusal test may point `PATH` at a fixture directory of its own that holds a plain, non-executable `agent-director` file, to prove the refusal, and restores `PATH` in `afterEach`. `tests/host-safety.test.ts`'s "a tool's own PATH directory holds an agent-director file" row is the one such case.
- **Enforcement.** `tests/host-safety.test.ts` audits the tree with the TypeScript parser, so text in strings, comments, templates and regex literals never counts: the value-import rule and the child-environment rule over every file `bun test` runs and every test helper; that no file in the repository imports the preload and that the `[test]` preload of both `bunfig.toml` and `tests/bunfig.toml` names it with a path entry that resolves to it from that file's directory; and, over `src/` and `scripts/`, the SRJ-121 call sites (`Client.create` only in the startup gate's module, `resolveSystemBinary` as a value only in the server, the install check and `/publish`'s check). It then runs the shared `preloadCheckFailures` on the live environment and, only when that reports nothing, calls `runStartupGate()` and `runInstallCheck()` un-injected: both must fail as not found.
- **Fixing a finding.** Inject the stub, or pass `env: hostSafeChildEnv(...)` directly in the call. When the preload check fails, run `bun test` from the repository root or `tests/`, so the guard loads and sets HOME and `PATH` itself; still start with a scratch HOME (`tests/README.md`). Never loosen an audit, add an exemption or widen what the preload set to make room.

### Integration scripts run only in docker CI

The integration scripts (`tests/integration/*.sh`), their shared helper (`tests/integration/lib/scenario.sh`), their fixtures (`tests/integration/fixtures/`: the Test 4 and Test 11 drivers, `stub-claude.sh` and the Slack stub server) and `tests/runner.sh` install the packed package, write a persona config, start the server and spawn bots. They run only inside the `cscb-ci` docker container, started by the `ci` skill (`/ci`), which runs `tests/runner.sh` and reports `PASS` or `FAIL`.

- Never run them on a dev box or on any host without container support, and never against the real home. If docker is not available, report the integration run as not done; don't run the scripts directly.
- Their config and credentials files are written inside the container; the scripts never read a config from the host.
- `bun test` does not run them. `tests/integration/session-leader.test.ts` is the one `bun test` file under `tests/integration/`: it spawns only a throwaway child and touches no CSCB state. The Slack stub server has no `bun test` suite of its own: Test 10 exercises it end to end.
- `tests/README.md` has the suite's layout, the runner's discovery order (Tests 1 to 4, then every other `test-*.sh` in version order), the helper's contract and how to add a scenario. A new scenario sources the helper, which gives it its own scratch state dir, port and persona-name tag, bounded polling waits and a cleanup trap.
- `CSCB_SLACK_API_URL` (the Slack API base URL override, honoured only for an `http://127.0.0.1…` or `http://[::1]…` URL) exists for the scenarios alone. Test 10 sets it to point a live start at the Slack stub. A unit test passes an env object to `resolveSlackApiUrlOverride`, or a `slackApiUrl` to the connection manager or the option builders. The one case that sets it in `process.env`, to prove it is not read from there, restores it in `finally`.

### The /ci-live runner

The `/ci-live` runner (`ci-live/`, see `docker/README.md`) has its own dependency, `playwright-core`, installed only in `ci-live/node_modules`. Its unit tests live in `tests/` like every other suite.

- Import only from `ci-live/lib/`, `ci-live/provision/`, `ci-live/checks/` and `ci-live/dry-run/`. Never import `ci-live/run.ts`, `ci-live/main.ts`, `ci-live/maintenance.ts`, `ci-live/runtime/` or `ci-live/browser/` (`driver.ts` and the flows): they load `playwright-core`, directly or through `main.ts` or `ci-live/runtime/workspace.ts`, so `bun test` and `bun run typecheck` would fail on a clone where the runner's dependency is not installed. Put logic a test must reach in `ci-live/lib/` or `ci-live/provision/`, as the memory watchdog (`lib/memory-watchdog.ts`), the copy of the container's own logs (`lib/container-logs.ts`), the stray-app rules (`provision/app-listing.ts`) and the prompt guard (`checks/prompt-guard.ts`, driven through `PromptGuardDeps` with a fake trail and the shared fake clock) are.
- Put no `*.test.ts` or `*.spec.ts` file under `ci-live/`: `bun test` would pick it up.
- Drive I/O through the runner's seams (`SecureFs` for the secret store, `ScanFs` for the secrecy scan, `LockDeps` for the run lock's file access and PID liveness test and a collecting warning sink for its stale-lock `WARNING`, the spawn function for docker and the host probes, the injected fetch and clock; `ContainerLogsSink` for the copy of the container's logs (an in-memory sink, or `containerLogsSink` over a `mkdtempSync` results dir) with a fake `ContainerExec` for the container and the shared fake clock as the collector's `clock` (`CollectorClock`), which a caller's wait runs on; for the memory watchdog, its `WatchdogSources` and `WatchdogClock`, the file reader `readHostCgroup` takes and the `/proc` reader, `ProcReader`). A test never reads `~/.config/cscb-test/` or the host's cgroup files, takes the real run lock, starts a container or reaches Slack.
- The code no test may import (`ci-live/run.ts`, `ci-live/main.ts`, `ci-live/maintenance.ts`, `ci-live/runtime/`, `ci-live/browser/`) is pinned by the source audit in `tests/ci-live-docker.test.ts`: its environment reads, spawn sites, host commands, docker subcommands (the exact `docker stats` and `docker stop` argv included), leftover removal, the run lock's acquires and their warning sinks, signal handling (`main.ts`'s `stopRun` and `maintenance.ts`'s stop), the memory watchdog's wiring, the cleanup order, the copy of the container's own logs awaited before every removal or stop of the container (so a failed check can still be traced once the container is gone) and sealed before the closing scan, `appsCommand`'s order, the prompt guard's wiring and the browser's bounds in `browser/driver.ts` (`ensureSignedIn`'s idle and the one-at-a-time flows included). A change there that adds a host command, a spawn site, a lock acquire, an environment read or a removal or stop of the container, or reorders a pinned sequence, fails the audit; widen or update the audit for that one item in the same change, and never loosen a pattern to get it green.
- The runner's tokens are fakes built at runtime, as everywhere under `tests/` (see Credentials Fixtures and Leak Checks); `tests/secrecy-audit.test.ts` rejects a token literal.

## Fixture Patterns

### Factory Functions

Every test file defines factory functions that create fixtures with sensible defaults and optional overrides:

```
makePersonaConfig(overrides?, baseDir?) — a resolved PersonaConfig with one default persona
makeMultiPersonaConfig(specs, baseDir, overrides?) — a resolved PersonaConfig with one persona per spec
makeTransport() — minimal transport stub
makeServer() — minimal MCP server stub
```

Always use factory functions instead of hardcoding fixture values in individual tests. When a new field is added to a type, update the factory function — all tests automatically pick up the default.

Reload-controller tests build their configuration directory, personas and runs with `makeReloadHarness` (see Reload Tests), not by hand.

Persona-loader tests, and tests of persona-keyed code such as `spawnForPersona`, `startupSessionManager` and `launchSession` in `src/session-manager.ts`, use the shared persona-config helper, `tests/test-helpers/persona-config.ts`. It is the only config fixture; a route-keyed config appears only as raw JSON in a pre-persona rejection case. The CLI's `loadConfig` dependency returns a resolved `PersonaConfig`, so CLI tests build it with these helpers too. The persona-config helper provides:

```
makePersona(overrides?, baseDir?) — one PersonaInput entry in file form; default paths sit under baseDir
makePersonaConfigInput(overrides?, baseDir?) — a PersonaConfigInput file-form config with one default persona
makePersonaConfig(overrides?, baseDir?) — a resolved PersonaConfig, as loadPersonaConfig returns it, except mcp_config_path (see below)
makeMultiPersonaConfig(specs, baseDir, overrides?) — a resolved PersonaConfig with one persona per PersonaSpec, in order
makeStandInPersonaConfig(personas, baseDir, overrides?) — makeMultiPersonaConfig for stand-in personas keyed and named by one channel ID each
writeConfigFile(dir, input) — writes input as JSON to <dir>/config.json and returns the path
```

Use `makeMultiPersonaConfig` when a test needs several personas. Each `PersonaSpec` is any resolved `Persona` field except `index`; `overrides` sets the server-wide fields as for `makePersonaConfig`.

- It runs no validation, so a stand-in persona can set `key` to a channel ID directly.
- Each persona defaults to its own `all` channel, with prompts to that channel and DMs off. A spec with no channels gets `permission_prompts: 'dm'` but DMs still off, a pair the loader rejects; set `dm` in that spec yourself.
- It throws when two personas share a name or a key.
- `baseDir` is required; pass the test's `mkdtempSync` directory.

`makeStandInPersonaConfig` is kept for older suites whose fixtures key personas by channel ID; new tests use `makeMultiPersonaConfig` with real persona names. Each record key is a channel ID that becomes the persona's `name` and `key`, its one `all` channel and its `permission_prompts` target; the record value sets further fields such as `working_directory`. Personas keep record order, and everything else, including the required `baseDir`, is as for `makeMultiPersonaConfig`.

For the other helpers, `baseDir` defaults to the OS temp directory; pass the test's own `mkdtempSync` directory when paths must exist or be unique to the test. `writeConfigFile` writes only into the caller-supplied temp directory and has no default location. Pass `loadPersonaConfig` a temp home as well, so `~` never expands to the real home. Remove both the temp directory and the temp home in `afterEach`.

Build agent-director rows in tests with the canned-row helpers in `tests/test-helpers/agent-director-stub.ts` (`cannedListRow`, `cannedGetResult`, `cannedGetResultPlural`), never by hand. Every row they build is persona-labelled (b.av2 SR-13.4):

- **Default form** — `cannedListRow({ claude_instance_id })`: the row stands for the persona whose key is the instance ID without its `cscb_` prefix. Labels are exactly `service=cscb` and `persona=<key>` (no `config_dir`, no `channel`), tmux `slack_bot_<key>`, and `cwd` is `defaultCannedRowCwd()`, the working directory of the default persona fixture (`makePersona()`, `<tmpdir>/personas/test_bot/work`; nothing creates it). The stub client's default `get` row uses this form. Use it for permission-poller and click-handler rows, and pair it with a `getPersona` stub that knows that key.
- **Persona form** — `cannedListRow(overrides, persona, home)`, likewise `cannedGetResult` and `cannedGetResultPlural`.

A `channel` label appears only in explicit negative rows that test the rejection of pre-persona rows (the poller skipping a row with no `persona` label, the start sweep killing a live one and keeping it): override `labels` to carry only the old `channel` label.

For the persona form, pass a resolved persona (e.g. from `makePersonaConfig`). The row then carries what a spawn of that persona writes: `cwd` = its working directory, `cscb_<key>`, `slack_bot_<key>`, and the `service`, `persona` and `config_dir` labels. The collision ladder and the start sweep replace a row whose `cwd` or `config_dir` label doesn't match (b.av2 SR-6.2, SR-6.3), so a fixture meant to reach a resume, reconnect or wait path must use this form. `home` is required and the persona form throws without it; there is no OS-home fallback. The `config_dir` label must be computed under the same home the session manager's `_setSpawnHomeDir` seam uses, or it won't match the one the code computes, so pass that home. To test a guard, override `cwd` or `labels` explicitly. `spawnForPersona` joins a launch already in flight for the same key, so a suite that can leave a launch unsettled calls `_resetInFlightLaunches()` in `afterEach`.

To test that only one launch per persona is in flight, wrap the stub with `holdSpawns(stub, shouldHold?)` from the same file. Each `spawn` whose instance ID passes `shouldHold` (all by default) stays open until the test settles it; other spawns go to the stub's original `spawn`. The handle exposes `calls` (every spawn received), `held()` (instance IDs still open), `entered(id)` (resolves once a spawn for `id` is issued), `release(id)` and `fail(id, err)` (settle the oldest held spawn for `id`), and `releaseAll()` for teardown.

To assert which agent-director calls a case made (for example, none for a persona that is not up), use the shared stub call-log helper in the same file rather than a hand-rolled recorder, so every suite reads the stub's calls the same way.

To stand in for `resolveSystemBinary` (the start gate's system-install lookup, the runtime version re-check's resolve, `/publish`'s SR-2.5 check), use `makeStubResolveSystemBinary(opts?)` from the same file; no test runs a binary to learn a version. It returns an `(opts?) => Promise<{ path, version }>` function. With no options every call resolves with `PHASE1_RC_VERSION`, which passes CSCB's Phase 1 floor, at `/usr/local/bin/agent-director`; `path` changes that path, `version` the version of every call, and `throws` rejects every call with that error. `outcomes` answers the calls in order, one entry per call, and once the list runs out its last entry answers every later call: `{ version, path? }` resolves with that version (at `path`, else the builder's `path`), `{ throws }` rejects with that error, and `{ never: true }` returns a promise that never settles, for a time-limit case. `outcomes` given with `throws` or `version`, or empty, throws when the stub is built. `calls` records each call's argument, a call that never settles included.

**agent-director errors.** Build an agent-director error in a test with the stub's builder wherever one exists. The three errors only the Phase 1 client declares (`ErrTmuxUnresponsive`, `ErrTmuxKillFailed`, `ErrTmuxSessionConflict`) are built only with their builders, never from a class. For an error the stub has no builder for (for example `ErrCwdNotFound`), a test may use `new` on the 0.10.0 client's own class. A test of a site's error handling builds the error with the stub builders and imports the class it expects from `src/ad-error-class.ts` (`AD_ERROR_CLASS_*`), never a literal label. Assert a call-capture array with `toHaveLength`: Bun's `toEqual([])` also passes on `[undefined]`.

Each builder uses the 0.10.0 client's own class, except:

- `errGeneric(verb, errName, message?)`, which builds the base `AgentDirectorError` for any `errName`;
- `errAmbiguousRequest()` and `errPermissionRequestNotFound()`, which build the base `AgentDirectorError` with the error's `errName` although 0.10.0 exports a class for each, because the code under test recognises both by `errName`;
- the builders for the three Phase-1-only errors.

Until the Phase 1 client is adopted (b.jg5 E37), those Phase-1-only builders return the 0.10.0 client's `AgentDirectorError` carrying the error's name (as both `name` and `errName`, from the `ERR_TMUX_*_NAME` strings in `src/agent-director-errors.ts`), its verb and its description, so they classify by name. No test imports a Phase-1-only class, in a named import or a re-export.

The description words CSCB matches come from `src/ad-description-phrases.ts`, and the builders take them from there. A test that checks for one imports the constant from that module, never a literal. The builders' default names are the default persona fixture's: `STUB_TMUX_SESSION_NAME`, `STUB_INSTANCE_ID`, and `STUB_TMUX_SOCKET_PATH` for the socket.

- **`ErrTmuxUnresponsive` (by name)**: `errTmuxUnresponsive(verb?, description?)` (verb `resume`; `status` only reads the store and never returns it; by default a tmux call that did not answer and did nothing). The variants CSCB tells apart: `errTmuxUnresponsiveLaunchTimeout(verb?, instanceId?)` (verb `spawn`, or `resume`; carries `LAUNCH_TIMEOUT_PHRASE`), `errTmuxUnresponsiveStillStopping(verb?, sessionName?)` and `errTmuxUnresponsiveStillStarting(verb?, sessionName?)` (verb `resume`, session `STUB_TMUX_SESSION_NAME`; the description names the quoted session name, as agent-director's does, and carries `STILL_STOPPING_PHRASE` or `STILL_STARTING_PHRASE`).
- **`ErrTmuxKillFailed` (by name)**: `errTmuxKillFailed(sessionName?, description?, pids?)`, verb `kill`. `description` is a `KillFailedDescription`: `'outlived-exit-wait'` (the default; a kill was sent and the agent process outlived the exit wait), `'pane-process-survived'` (a kill was sent and another process of the labelled session's panes outlived it), `'unverifiable-session-present'` (a kill was sent, the process cannot be checked and the labelled session is still there) or `'no-session-no-kill'` (no session or pane of this launch while the process runs; "no kill was sent"). Every description carries the quoted session name, "retry kill later" and "never delete this row" (`RETRY_KILL_LATER_PHRASE`, `NEVER_DELETE_ROW_PHRASE`). Only `'pane-process-survived'` names a pid: each of `pids` as `pid <n>`, joined with ", " (default `STUB_SURVIVOR_PIDS`, one fake pid above Linux's largest), the form `SURVIVOR_PID_PATTERN` matches and `survivorPids` lists in order; the other three ignore `pids` and name none. The builder checks its own text with `survivorPids` and throws if the pids it names are not exactly those given, or if `'pane-process-survived'` is given no pid. `KILL_FAILED_DESCRIPTIONS` lists all four for `test.each`.
- **`ErrTmuxSessionConflict` (by name)**: `errTmuxSessionConflict(verb, conflictCase, sessionName?, options?)`. `conflictCase` is a `ConflictCase`, one per case of ADSRD SR-1.4: `no-valid-id`, `different-id`, `another-store`, `own-id`, `leftover`, `scan-leftover` (the pre-spawn scan's refusal: "left over from an earlier life", the session's tmux id `STUB_TMUX_SESSION_ID`, "nothing was written and no row was created"), `duplicate-session-leftover` (a plain spawn at "duplicate session": "its label names this instance id", "left over from an earlier life", "the new row was ended"), `not-this-launch`, `pane-not-found` (also "this row's own id"), `conflicting-labels`, `never-reported-in` (also "this row's own id" and "no kill was sent"), and `unrecognised`, which carries none of the case words. `not-this-launch` from `kill` also carries "this row's own id" and "no kill was sent". `CONFLICT_CASES` lists all twelve. Every description carries the `agent-director list --tmux-session-name <quoted name>` line. `different-id` and `another-store` ("another agent-director store") say the session is another row's (another store's) agent and must not be ended, and point to no "Operator actions"; every other description points a human to "Operator actions". `options` is a `ConflictOptions` selecting the variants the positional form cannot: `plainSpawn` on `different-id` or `another-store` (a plain spawn at "duplicate session": also "its label does not name this instance id" and "the new row was ended"), `notAdopted` on `pane-not-found` (a lost create reply: also "the agent's pane was not adopted"), `scan` on `conflicting-labels` (the pre-spawn scan found an agent-director label value set at the server, global or global-window scope: also "nothing was written and no row was created"); an option set on any other case throws. No description names a command that ends a session; "no kill was sent" is the one mention of a kill. The case words and extras come from `src/ad-description-phrases.ts`.
- **0.10.0 classes**: `errTmuxNotAvailable(socketPath?, verb?)` (verb `spawn`; with `socketPath` that socket is not accessible to this user, without it tmux could not be run); `errTmuxNotAvailableDifferentServer(socketPath?, verb?)` (verb `resume`, socket `STUB_TMUX_SOCKET_PATH`; other single-row verbs that call tmux, such as `read-pane`, also return it, `status` never; carries `DIFFERENT_TMUX_SERVER_PHRASE`); `errTmuxCaptureFailed(sessionName?, verb?)` (verb `read-pane`; the session is gone); `errSpawnNotPausable(verb?)` (verb `pause`); `errSystemInstallDisappeared(verb?, binaryPath?)` (verb `status`, path `STUB_RESOLVE_DEFAULT_PATH`). Beside `errSpawnNotInteractive(verb?)`, `errSpawnNotInteractiveLeftover(sessionName?, verb?)` (the session is a leftover of an earlier launch) and `errSpawnNotInteractiveNoLaunchStart(verb?)` (a `pending` row with no launch start), both verb `send-keys`. `errInvalidFlags(verb?)` builds the client's `ErrInvalidFlags` (verb `decide`; pass `spawn` or `resume` for a launch verb's refusal).
- **No class in any client**: an unknown name arrives as `ErrUnknownErrorName`, built as the client builds it: `unknownName` is the name and `envelope` is the binary's `{ err_name, err_description }`. The client's own `verb` and `errDescription` stay as it sets them, so read the binary's description from `envelope.err_description`. `errUnknownErrorName(unknownName?, description?)` builds any name (default `ErrFromALaterBinary`); `errInternal(description?)` an `ErrInternal`; `errSchemaMismatch(description?)` an `ErrSchemaMismatch` (by default the store could not be opened), while the other two store-open names, `ErrSchemaMigrationRequired` and `ErrStoreOpen`, are built with `errUnknownErrorName` and the `ERR_*_NAME` constants from `src/agent-director-errors.ts`; `errUnusableName(fault?)` the unusable-recorded-name `ErrInternal`, carrying `UNUSABLE_RECORDED_NAME_PHRASE`, where `fault` is an `UnusableNameFault` (`'empty'`, the default, `'control-character'` or `'stored-differently'`, all in `UNUSABLE_NAME_FAULTS`); `errConfigMalformed(key?, value?, minimum?, unit?)` an `ErrConfigMalformed` in agent-director's refusal form, `config <path>: refused [tmux] values: [tmux] <key> = <value>, below its safe minimum <minimum> <unit>. A missing key, or 0, gives the default.` (default `starting_session_seconds` = `30` below `60` `s`; `unit` is `s` or `ms`).
- **Every UNAVAILABLE form**: `UNAVAILABLE_FORMS` is the one table of UNAVAILABLE forms (b.jg5 SRJ-104), each an `UnavailableForm` tuple `[label, make(verb), causeKind]`: `ErrTmuxUnresponsive` and its three variants (`ErrTmuxUnresponsive, still stopping`, `ErrTmuxUnresponsive, still starting`, `ErrTmuxUnresponsive, launch timeout`), `ErrCallTimeout`, `ErrUnknownErrorName`, `a wrapped UnknownError` (CSCB's `UnknownError` wrapper), `ErrTmuxKillFailed` (cause `kill-failed`; only a kill answers it) and `a plain Error`, every other form with cause `unavailable`. A file takes its subset with `unavailableForms(...picks)`, in the order given: a label picks its row as is, a `[label, as]` pair relabels it, and a label the table lacks throws. Never write a file-local copy of the table.

**Phase 1 results.** The Phase 1 result fields `kill_sent`, `launch_started_at` and `pre_trust` are typed by `src/ad-phase1-types.ts`, which also names the `liveness_note` values CSCB tells apart (`LivenessNote`); tests take those types from there, never from `agent-director`. A Phase 1 field given as `undefined`, or left out, is absent from the built result, as from a binary older than Phase 1.

- `cannedKillResult(killSent?)`: `{ kill_sent }`, or `{}` without an argument. The stub's default `kill` result is `{}`.
- `cannedStatusResult(overrides?)`: `{ state: 'waiting' }` by default. Its one Phase 1 override is `launch_started_at`: agent-director's `status` result has no `liveness_note`.
- `cannedGetResult` and `cannedListRow` take `launch_started_at` and `liveness_note` as overrides, unset by default. Launch starts: `SAMPLE_LAUNCH_START_FRACTIONAL`, `SAMPLE_LAUNCH_START_WHOLE` and `SAMPLE_LAUNCH_START_NONE` (`undefined`), all three by form in `SAMPLE_LAUNCH_STARTS` for `test.each`. Notes: `provenanceNote` (`provenance_conflict`, which latches a persona with the CONFLICT case "conflicting labels") and `nonLatchingNotes` (the other notes CSCB tells apart, which must never latch a persona).
- `cannedSpawnResult(claudeInstanceId?, preTrust?)` and `cannedResumeResult(claudeInstanceId?, preTrust?)`: `preTrust` sets `pre_trust` (`ok`, `skipped` or `failed`).
- `cannedFindMissing({ rows })`: `rows` maps each row ID to where a sweep reports it, `'ids'`, `'unverified_ids'` or `'neither'`; both ID lists are sorted and `count` and `unverified` are their lengths. `cannedFindMissing(overrides?)` builds the result field by field (empty by default).

**Stub client knobs.** A `<verb>Queue` on `makeStubClient` holds `cannedOk(value)` and `cannedErr(error)` entries; N entries answer the next N calls in order. Once the queue is empty the verb falls through to `<verb>Error`, then `<verb>Result`, then its default. `killQueue` and `findMissingQueue` work this way (defaults `{}` and `cannedFindMissing()`).

- `readPaneQueue` wins over `readPaneError` and `readPaneResults` while it holds an entry. Once empty, the call falls through to `readPaneError`, then `readPaneResults` (whose last entry sticks), then `{ pane: '' }`; the queue's own last entry does not stick.
- `statusFn(params)` and `getFn(params)` compute each `status` or `get` answer from the call's params and take precedence over every other knob of their verb. Returning an `Error` rejects; the call is still recorded in `statusCalls` or `getCalls`.
- `makeStubCreateClient({ calls })` records the options each client is built with in `calls`, typed `ClientOptions[]`, one entry per call in order (a rejected call included), so a test reads `calls[0].callTimeoutMs`. A field the caller did not pass is absent.
- `makeCloseCountingStubClient(opts?)` returns `{ client, closes }`: a `makeStubClient(opts)` stub whose `close()` is replaced by a counter, and `closes()` the number of calls so far.
- `makePassingGateDeps(overrides?)` returns `Partial<StartupGateDeps>` for a start gate run that passes every step: `createClient` from `makeStubCreateClient()`, `probeGetPermission`, `probeErrorCatalog` and `probeDecideArgv` passing, a `statSync` that throws ENOENT (the same-user check passes silently), `geteuid` 1000, a `recordStartupError` that swallows, and an `exit` that throws, since a passing run never reaches it. `overrides` wins over every default (a test that checks the exit code passes its own `exit`).

Other shared fixture builders in `tests/test-helpers/`:

- `ad-settings.ts`: agent-director's settings file for tests (b.jg5 SRJ-1304). `writeAgentDirectorConfig(home, input)` writes `.agent-director/config.toml` (the path `src/ad-settings.ts` exports as `AD_SETTINGS_RELATIVE_PATH`) under `home`, creating the directory, and returns the path; it throws, writing nothing, when `home` is the real home (`isRealHome`) or is not a directory under the OS temp directory (the temp directory itself included). `input` (`AdConfigInput`) is either `AdConfigTables` (`tmux`: any of the nine keys; `pauseTimeout`: `[pause] timeout_seconds`; `extraTmuxKeys`: unknown or misspelt `[tmux]` keys; `extra`: other top-level entries, which can replace `tmux` or `pause` with a plain value) or `AdConfigNotToml` (`{ notToml: true, embed? }`: fixed text smol-toml refuses, with `embed` on its offending first line so a leak case can put a fake token there; the one raw-text write). Every other file goes through smol-toml's `stringify` with `numbersAsFloat: true`, and a value (`AdConfigValue`) is a `bigint` for a TOML integer, a plain number for a float or a string. Without that option the serializer writes an integral number such as `60` as the integer `60`, so a float case could never be produced; with it every number is written as a float (`60` as `60.0`), and integers are written as `BigInt`, the type the reader's values have. `REFUSED_AD_CONFIG_FORMS` (rows of `RefusedAdConfigForm`: `name`, `input`, and the `key` the refusal concerns, undefined for text that is not TOML) is SRJ-1304's list of refused forms for `test.each`: text that is not TOML, a string, a float, a float with a zero fraction, a negative value, `stopping_window_seconds` and `starting_session_seconds` one below their minimums, `pending_grace_seconds` one below the minimum a raised `create_timeout_ms` gives, and that raised `create_timeout_ms` with `pending_grace_seconds` missing and 0. `RAISED_CREATE_TIMEOUT_MS` (the default grace period in milliseconds) and `RAISED_GRACE_MINIMUM_SECONDS` (the grace minimum it gives with the default pipe-close wait) are exported for the grace cases. `DEFAULT_AD_SETTINGS` and `AD_SETTING_MINIMUMS` are re-exported from `src/ad-settings.ts`, never copied, and every threshold in the forms is derived from them and `pendingGraceMinimumSeconds`, so a change to a default or a minimum moves every form. It holds no module-scope state and starts no process.
- `agent-director-versions.ts`: the agent-director versions tests check against CSCB's Phase 1 floor (b.jg5 SRJ-1304). A test imports every agent-director version it checks from here or from `src/`, never as a literal, so a change to `PHASE1_FLOOR_VERSION` (`src/ad-version-gate.ts`) moves every case with it. `PHASE1_RC_VERSION` is the floor with `-rc.1` appended, built from the constant; it passes the floor. `OLD_AD_VERSION` is the last release before Phase 1, which the client admits and the floor refuses. `DEV_PLACEHOLDER_VERSION` is the client's own `DEV_SENTINEL_VERSION`, re-exported (never typed); the floor refuses it. `DEV_UNPARSEABLE_VERSION` is a development version that is not strict SemVer; the floor refuses it. `CLIENT_MIN_VERSION` is the installed client's `min_binary_version`, read once at load from its `dist/version-floor.json` through the package's subpath export `FLOOR_SUBPATH` (exported: `agent-director/dist/version-floor.json`, the file `src/install-check.ts` reads); the module throws a clear error at load when the file cannot be resolved or read or holds no string `min_binary_version`. `BELOW_CLIENT_MIN_VERSION` is the plain release one step below the client minimum's core (one patch below, else the previous minor's or major's `999` release), which the client refuses as too old; the module throws at load unless `compareAdVersions` puts it below the minimum. It starts no process, holds no value import of `Client` or `resolveSystemBinary`, and imports only `src/ad-version-gate.ts` and the client's sentinel, so the agent-director stub imports it without a cycle.
- `archive-db.ts`: `buildTempArchiveDb(rows, defaultChannel)` builds a real message-archive SQLite file through the production `openArchiveDatabase` in its own `mkdtempSync` directory (rows at chosen timestamps, optional per-row channel, WAL checkpointed so a read-only open sees them) and returns `{ dbPath, cleanup }`; `messagesSince(startedAt, channelId, count)` gives `count` rows strictly after a spawn's `started_at`, the one place that boundary rule lives. Used by `tests/jsonl-persistence-check.test.ts`, `tests/session-manager.test.ts` and `tests/persona-routing.test.ts`; call `cleanup()` in teardown.
- `ci-live.ts`: the shared fakes for the `/ci-live` runner suites (`tests/ci-live-*.test.ts`). `memSecureFs()` is an in-memory `SecureFs`, the secret store's seam: files and directories with their modes, plus a log of every call, so a suite can assert the order of a private write (temp file, chmod, rename) and that a path was never read. `virtualClock()` is the runner's `Clock` over the shared fake clock: `sleep(ms)` advances virtual time by `ms`, so a bounded wait runs to its deadline with no real delay. It imports the runner only from the directories The /ci-live runner allows.
- `fifo.ts`: real FIFOs. Node and Bun have no mkfifo API; a test makes a FIFO only through this module, never by starting `mkfifo` itself. `mkfifoAvailable()` is probed once per process: `false` with no child started when `mkfifo` is not on `PATH`, otherwise whether `mkfifo --version` exits 0. Guard a real-FIFO case with `test.skipIf(!mkfifoAvailable())`, the reason in the test name. `makeFifo(path)` creates a FIFO at `path` and throws, naming the path, the exit status or signal and `mkfifo`'s stderr, when `mkfifo` fails; it neither removes an entry already there nor creates the parent directory. Each `mkfifo` child is bounded (`MKFIFO_TIMEOUT_MS`) and its `env` is a direct `hostSafeChildEnv` call naming only `mkfifo`, over a fresh temp HOME removed as soon as the child exits. A `HostSafetyError` (for example `mkfifo`'s `PATH` directory holding an `agent-director`) is thrown, never read as "unavailable". `mkfifoAvailable()` runs while tests are collected (inside `test.skipIf`), so the error makes the whole test file fail to load rather than its FIFO cases skip; from `makeFifo` inside a test it fails that test. The reload harness's `mkfifoAvailable` and `h.makeFifo` delegate to it; `tests/fifo-helper.test.ts` pins it.
- `host-safe-env.ts`: `hostSafeChildEnv(home, { tools, pathDirs, extras })`, the environment of every child process a unit test starts. Pass it as the child call's `env` option directly (not built earlier into a variable, spread, merged or wrapped); add variables through `extras`. It returns exactly `HOME` (`home` as given), `PATH`, `TMUX_TMPDIR` and the extras, nothing else from `process.env`, so no token reaches the child.
  - `PATH` is the absolute, test-owned `pathDirs` first, in order (stub or shim bins), then the directory each named tool resolves to on the current `PATH`, duplicates dropped. `tools` defaults to `DEFAULT_CHILD_TOOLS` (`bun`, `bash`, `sh`). With no tool and no `pathDirs` it is `EMPTY_CHILD_PATH` (`/dev/null`), which no lookup can search; pass `tools: []` for a child started by absolute path (`process.execPath`) that runs nothing by name.
  - The tmux fence: `TMUX_TMPDIR` is one directory under the OS temp directory whose name starts with `CHILD_TMUX_TMPDIR_PREFIX`, fixed on the first call and returned by every later call in the process, and no extra can set `TMUX` or `TMUX_PANE`, so a tmux the child starts never reaches the host's tmux server. The first call reuses the process's own `TMUX_TMPDIR` when a parent's `hostSafeChildEnv` set it (`inheritedChildTmuxTmpDir`: an existing directory, not a symlink, owned by this user, directly under the OS temp directory, named with the prefix and more); otherwise it makes one with `mkdtempSync`. So a `bun` child that loads the helper again (a harness run in a child) hands its own children the parent's directory.
  - The directory is never removed: tmux falls back to `/tmp`, the host's default socket directory, when `TMUX_TMPDIR` is missing, so it must outlive every child. Each `bun test` run leaves at most one, plus one for each child whose OS temp directory differs from its parent's: the helper does not pass `TMPDIR` on, and only a directory directly under the child's own OS temp directory is reused. A test that starts such a child and wants the parent's directory reused passes `TMPDIR` as an extra.
  - It throws a `HostSafetyError` whose `reason` is one of `HOST_SAFETY_REFUSAL`, and whose message names the path or name at fault without printing the environment, when: `home` is relative, is the real home or holds an agent-director install (`.agent-director`, or anything at the standard install path); a tool name is empty or holds `/` or the `PATH` delimiter, or the tool is not on `PATH`; a `pathDirs` entry is relative or holds the delimiter; any directory on the built `PATH` holds an entry named `agent-director` (a file, directory, symlink or dangling symlink); or an extra sets a reserved name (`RESERVED_CHILD_ENV_NAMES`: `HOME`, `PATH`, `TMUX_TMPDIR`, `TMUX`, `TMUX_PANE`) or is not a string. Fix the fixture, or take the offending directory off the test run's `PATH`; don't catch the error to skip a case.
  - The real home is the account's passwd home or the home the run started with, symlinks resolved. Test for it with the exported `isRealHome()` (or get it from `realHome()`), never `os.homedir()` or `os.userInfo().homedir`: Bun fixes both at start-up from the launch-time `HOME`. The module also exports the agent-director predicates (`dirHoldsAgentDirector`, `homeHoldsAgentDirectorInstall`), the discovery names, and the `PATH` lookups (`absolutePathEntries`, the absolute entries of a `PATH` value; `resolveToolDir`, the directory of a tool's first executable on it), so nothing else spells them. `osTempDir()` is the OS temp directory made absolute and normalized (`resolve(tmpdir())`), so `TMPDIR=/tmp//` or `/tmp/.` names the same directory as `/tmp`; every "directly under the OS temp directory" comparison uses it, never the raw `tmpdir()`. `runCli`, `runInFakeHome` and `fifo.ts` use it; `tests/host-safety.test.ts` pins it.
  - The preload guard's logic lives here too, so the guard and `tests/host-safety.test.ts` share one implementation. `preloadRedirectedEnv(inherited, home, tmuxTmpDir)` returns the guard's variables (`PRELOAD_ENV_NAMES`) computed from an inherited environment, and changes nothing: not `inherited`, not `process.env`, not the file system. `preloadCheckFailures(env)` lists why `env` is not an environment the guard produced, as `PRELOAD_CHECK` labels, or returns `[]`; it only `lstat`s and starts no process.
- `host-safety-preload.ts`: the `bun test` preload guard (see Host safety under Isolation). `bunfig.toml` and `tests/bunfig.toml` load it; it exports nothing and no test or helper imports it. It makes the HOME directory (`PRELOAD_HOME_PREFIX` under `osTempDir()`), takes the `TMUX_TMPDIR` directory from `childTmuxTmpDir()`, applies `preloadRedirectedEnv` to `process.env` and throws when `preloadCheckFailures` reports anything; all of these come from `host-safe-env.ts`.
- `install-check-fixtures.ts`: fixtures for the install check, its callers and the tests that check versions against the client minimum. Version constants: `SATISFYING_VERSION` (at or above `CLIENT_MIN_VERSION`, below CSCB's Phase 1 floor: the check passes it with the note) and `STALE_VERSION` (below the client minimum: too old), both checked against the installed client's minimum and the floor when the module loads, which throws if either is out of place; `SENTINEL_VERSION` and the re-exported `DEV_SENTINEL_VERSION` (never type the sentinel); `CANNED_BINARY_PATH`, the path every canned result reports by default (equal to the stub resolver's `STUB_RESOLVE_DEFAULT_PATH`, pinned by `tests/agent-director-stub-resolve.test.ts` rather than imported, since the stub helper pulls in the session manager and persona modules); `UNREACHABLE_REASONS`, every `ErrSystemInstallUnreachable` reason agent-director defines in a fixed order, built from a `Record<UnreachableReason, true>` so the compiler holds the set complete. Version-floor JSON builders (`makeVersionFloorJson`, `makeMalformedFloorJson`, `makeMissingFieldFloorJson`) and the `tests/fixtures/version-floor/` loaders (`loadFixtureRaw`, `fixturePath`). Canned `runInstallCheck` results in its shapes and texts: `cannedSuccessResult(opts)` defaults to `CANNED_BINARY_PATH`, `PHASE1_RC_VERSION` and `CLIENT_MIN_VERSION` (from `agent-director-versions.ts`) with no note; any success field overrides, a `binaryVersion` below the floor carries `buildInstallCheckPhase1Note(binaryVersion)`, `phase1Note: true` or `false` forces the note on or off, and an explicit `note` wins. `cannedFailureResult(label, opts)` takes an exported label value and builds that label's default detail and message (too old: `STALE_VERSION` against `CLIENT_MIN_VERSION` at `CANNED_BINARY_PATH`, the message from `buildSystemInstallTooOldMessage`); `opts.detail` and `opts.message` replace them, the message otherwise built from the effective detail. The not-found, unreachable and floor-unreadable messages are copies of `src/install-check.ts`'s text, pinned by `tests/install-check.test.ts`, which compares real results whole with the canned ones. It imports `src/install-check.ts` for types only.
- `recovery-harness.ts`: `makeRecoveryHarness(options?)`, the recovery harness (b.jg5 SRJ-1304). It wires the UNAVAILABLE retry timer and the recovery paths around it over one `createFakeClock()` and one stub client, with every file under one `mkdtempSync` directory. As in `main()`, the controller's per-fire observer (`onRetryFire`) is the `tmux-unresponsive` condition's `onsetAtRetry`, called at every fire, a skipped one included, before the action. The condition's mode accessor reads `health_check_interval` in the configuration in effect at each check (0, the default, is off: the onset comes at a retry at or past the floor), and `setHealthCheckInterval(seconds)` changes it; its alert threshold accessor is `adAlertThresholdMsInEffect` over the harness's agent-director settings, so a start arms an alert check on the harness clock, and option `alertThresholdMs?: (() => number) | false` replaces the accessor, `false` arming none. `tickOnset(tickStartedAt?)` is a health tick body's end as `main()` binds `onTickEnd` (the condition's `onsetAtTick` for a tick started at that time, the clock's now by default), beside `tickEnd(key)`, the tick's healthy end; the harness has no health tick of its own. `teardown(key)` is the teardown's submit: it stops the persona's retry timer and cancels its alert check (`cancelAlert`), the condition kept; `shutdown()` raises the shutting-down flag, closes the controller and then closes the episodes (every alert check cancelled, a later condition start answering `closed`). Build every retry-timer test that needs more than a bare controller on it; later work extends this harness in place rather than hand-rolling another clock, stub or controller.
  - Options, all optional: `action` (the retry action; `'scripted'` picks the scripted action; the default retry action, `fullModeAction`, when unset), `personas` (`makeMultiPersonaConfig` specs; two personas by default), `sessionRestartDelay` and `healthCheckInterval` (both 0 by default), `adSettings` (agent-director's settings file, written with `writeAgentDirectorConfig`; none, so the defaults, by default), `restartDeps` (replace any of the restart module's production adapters and controls), `settleFlushes` (how many clock flushes `advance` waits at most for in-flight runs), `triggerSink` (true by default: the controller is installed as the outage state's trigger sink; `false` installs no sink, so nothing is recorded or armed), `conditionSink` (true by default: the `tmux-unresponsive` condition is installed as the outage state's condition sink; `false` leaves it out of the outage state, so no wrapped call starts or ends it, while the retry hooks and `tickEnd` still reach it) and `settleMs` (how many real milliseconds `settle` waits at most; 2000 by default).
  - `clock`, `controller`: the fake clock and a real `createUnavailableRetryController` on it. Its action records each retry in `attempts` (key, retry number, cause kinds, the mode the retry ran in, clock time), then calls the current action. The default is `fullModeAction`, the server's retry action for both modes (`createFullModeRetryAction`) over the real row read and restart entry (`runRestartRetry`), wired as `main()` wires it: `readRow` is the session manager's `readPersonaRowState` (a pending-only retry's one `status` call), then the applied-persona lookup over the live applied set, the relaunch gate, the restart cap (`isAtCap` at `RESTART_FAILURE_CAP`), the harness's shutting-down flag and the session manager's `isLaunchInFlight`, and the condition's retry hooks: `isSessionConnected` and `hasSessionStream` (both over `setConnected`) and `endTmuxUnresponsive`, the condition's end with reason `TMUX_UNRESPONSIVE_END_RETRY` and the retry's reading. `scriptedAction` answers each persona's outcomes queued with `answer(key, ...outcomes)` in order and a bare refusal (`{ kind: 'again' }`) once they run out. `setAction(action)` swaps the current action; `setAction(undefined)` goes back to the default. A case arms pending-only mode directly through `controller.armPendingOnly(key)`, the entry for a covered `pending` row, which no production path calls.
  - `config`, `keys`: the resolved configuration and its persona keys, every persona applied at first. The restart module is initialised over it (`initRestart`) with the production adapters: `_buildIsSessionAliveAdapter` over the applied configuration, `_buildReconnectSessionAdapter` and `_buildKillSessionAdapter` over the applied-persona lookup, and `launchSession` over the applied configuration with the relaunch gate as `canLaunch`. `getRestartDelay` answers the configuration's `session_restart_delay` (0 by default); `onCapReached` records the key in `capReached`, then calls `notifyRestartCapReached`, whose notice lands in `notices`; `serialize` is `serializer.run`, one real `createPersonaSerializer()` exposed as `serializer`, on which a test may hold a persona's turn. A retry never arms the restart module's own real timer: its entry bypasses it.
  - Controls: the relaunch gate is the real `createPersonaRelaunchGate` over a serving connection, the live applied set and the bring-up outcome `setUp(key, up)` sets (every persona up at first); `setConnected(key, connected)` sets whether the persona's session counts as connected with its message stream (`isSessionConnected`, `hasSessionStream`; none at first).
  - Drivers, each as the server does it: `shutdown()` raises the shutting-down flag, closes the controller (`close(UNAVAILABLE_RETRY_STOP_SHUTDOWN)`) and then the episodes (`episodes.close()`: every alert check cancelled, a later condition start answers `closed`); `teardown(key)` is the teardown's submit: it stops the persona's timer (`stop(key, UNAVAILABLE_RETRY_STOP_TORN_DOWN)`) and cancels its alert check (`tmuxUnresponsive.cancelAlert(key)`), the condition kept; `remove(key)` drops the persona from the applied configuration.
  - `home`, `stateDir`, `stub`: the temporary HOME, a temporary directory that `SLACK_STATE_DIR` points at while the harness is live, and the stub client installed through `installStubSpawnPath(home)` and given to `initOutageState` as its client. The tmux session killer and server ensurer the launch path can reach are no-ops, the findMissing memo is reset, and each persona's working directory exists, so a row the stub answers in it is the persona's own. `script(knobs)` (`RecoveryStubScript`: the stub's `StubClientOptions` answer knobs, such as `spawnError` or `getQueue`) sets the stub's answers, read at each call.
  - `triggers` (`RecoveryTrigger`, `{ key, kind }`): every trigger the outage state sent to the sink (b.jg5 SRJ-301), recorded, then armed on the controller; the sink answers what `controller.arm` answers, so a refused arm (a closed controller) marks nothing.
  - `episodes`, `tmuxUnresponsive` (b.jg5 SRJ-307, SRJ-310): one notice-episodes instance (`createPersonaEpisodes`) on the harness clock, its posts landing in `episodeNotices` and its lines in `lines`, and the `tmux-unresponsive` condition over it (`createTmuxUnresponsiveCondition`), wired as `main()` wires them. The condition is the outage state's condition sink in the same `initOutageState` call as the trigger sink, so a tmux-touching call's UNAVAILABLE inside an attempt starts it on the harness clock and a tmux-touching success or GONE ends it. Read it with `tmuxUnresponsive.holds(key)` and `tmuxUnresponsive.firstRefusalAt(key)`.
  - `conditionEnds` (`RecoveryConditionEnd`, `{ key, reading, result }`): each end of a holding condition calls the controller's condition-end entry (`conditionEnded(key, UNAVAILABLE_RETRY_CONDITION_TMUX_UNRESPONSIVE, reading)`) once and is recorded here with what the controller answered. The reading is the tick's or the retry's, `pending` for a successful launch call, and absent for any other tmux-touching success or GONE.
  - `tickEnd(key)`: a health tick's end of the condition, as `main()` binds `HealthCheckDeps.endTmuxUnresponsive` (reason `TMUX_UNRESPONSIVE_END_TICK`, reading `LIVENESS_LIVE`), answering the condition's end result. The harness has no health tick of its own.
  - `launch(key)`: a start-pass launch of configured persona `key` through the real `spawnForPersona` over the stub, resolving with its `SpawnPersonaResult`.
  - `settle()`: awaits every configured persona's launch in flight (`whenLaunchSettled`) and every retry run in flight (`whenRunSettled`), with its re-arm or stop. The spawn path's dialog polls run on real 1 ms timers and take no fake clock, so this is the harness's one real-time wait: 1 ms steps up to `settleMs`, throwing when a launch or a run is still in flight at the bound. A retry whose launch reaches a spawn needs it before the clock moves on, since `advance`'s bounded flushes do not cover those polls.
  - Captures: `lines` (the controller's, the relaunch gate's, the settings reader's and the notice episodes' and condition's lines), `capReached` (each key `onCapReached` was called for), `notices` (the session manager's notice sink), `outageNotices` (the outage state's), `episodeNotices` (the notice episodes' posts, `{ key, text }`, which production posts through the persona notifier), `startupErrors()` (the state directory's `startup-errors.log` entries), `errors` (every `console.error` call while the harness is live, such as the session manager's and the restart module's lines, its arguments joined with spaces, in order; `console.error` is replaced at build and put back by `cleanup()`) and `settings()` (agent-director's settings in effect, through `installAdSettings` over the temporary HOME). `captured()` gathers all of them, the attempts, the triggers, the condition ends and the state directory for `assertNoLeak`.
  - `advance(ms)` moves the clock one due time at a time, awaiting every in-flight retry run before and after each firing, so a re-arm measured from a run's end lands exactly; it resolves with the number of timers fired.
  - `cleanup()` (call it in `afterEach`) stops every retry timer and forgets every episode (`episodes.forgetAll()`, which cancels every alert check; this is the episodes module's one accepted test reset, where production shutdown uses `close()`), undoes every install and reset the harness made (`console.error`, restart state, and the failure counter, backoff and cap latch, the outage state and its trigger sink, the session notifier, the stub spawn path, the tmux seams, the findMissing memo, the settings install, `SLACK_STATE_DIR`) and removes the temporary directory. It then throws when a timer was still pending on the clock or a persona still armed, so a test cannot leave a retry behind; the episodes run on the harness clock, so a timer they left pending fails it too.
  - Shared case helpers, exported beside the harness so no file keeps its own copy: `personaOf(h, key)` (a configured persona); `collided(h, persona, row, ...spawns)` (the stub answers of a launch whose optimistic spawn collides, whose collision `get` reads `row` in the persona's own directory unless `row` says otherwise, and whose later spawns answer `spawns` in order); `callCounts(h)` (the stub's calls by verb, verbs never called left out); `retryNow(h, key, { settle? })` (moves the clock to the persona's due time, fires its retry and, unless `settle` is false, settles it; resolves with the due time and throws when no retry is pending); `rowReadsUntilSpawn(h, before)` (each persona's row reads `before`, or no row for `UNAVAILABLE_RETRY_ROW_ABSENT`, until a spawn of its instance resolves, then `waiting`). The `tmux-unresponsive` condition's lines have no exported builder, so these hold their fixed words, the seconds coming from the case's clock and threshold: `conditionLinePrefix(key)`, `conditionLines(h, key)`, `conditionStartedLines(h, key)`, `conditionEndedLines(h, key)`, and the builders `conditionOnsetLine(key, where, sinceMs)`, `conditionAlertLine(key, lastedMs, thresholdMs)`, `conditionEndedLine(key, reason)`, `conditionRecoveryLine(key)` and `conditionSilentEndLine(key)`.
- `reload-terms.ts`: `RELOAD_TERMS` and `reloadTermsIn`, the one reload-term list (see Unadvertised Reload Checks).
- `reply-guard-launch.ts`: order-log instruments for launch tests. `installRecordingReplyGuard(personas, stateDir, events)` installs the real `preLaunchReplyGuard` through the session manager's `setPreLaunchReplyGuard` seam, over the test's own state directory, logging `guard` before each step and `undo` before each undo; `observeLaunchCalls(stub, onCall)` wraps the stub's `spawn` and `resume` so each call is logged first and then delegated. It has no state or I/O of its own; the caller passes `mkdtempSync` directories and resets the seam (`_resetPreLaunchReplyGuard`) and the launched-with directories (`_resetLaunchedWithDirs`) in `afterEach`. Used by `tests/session-manager.test.ts` and `tests/restart.test.ts`.
- `upgrade-forms.ts`: `UPGRADE_FORMS`, the one list of the forms an instruction to upgrade agent-director or an upgrade or install command takes (b.jg5 SRJ-208), and its row type `ForbiddenForm` (label, pattern, a synthetic string the pattern matches). Check text with its whitespace collapsed (`flat`). A suite that needs more forms appends its own rows (the install-cscb skill's `RUN_OFFER_FORMS`); the shared rows are self-checked only in `tests/shipped-docs.test.ts`. Used by `tests/shipped-docs.test.ts`, `tests/install-cscb-skill.test.ts`, `tests/dependency-check.test.ts`, `tests/ad-version-gate.test.ts`, `tests/install-check.test.ts`, `tests/install-check-script.test.ts`, `tests/postinstall.test.ts` (which scans `UPGRADE_FORMS` as is) and `tests/preflight-ad-check.test.ts`.
- `working-row-panes.ts`: Claude Code screens and transcripts for the stale-`working`-row cases (bug b.f2b). Screens: `IDLE_PANE` (a finished turn above the empty prompt box, tall enough that anything above it sits outside the bottom lines), `OTHER_IDLE_PANE`, `SPINNER_PANE`, the dialogs (`PERMISSION_PANE`, `PLAN_APPROVAL_PANE`, `SELECT_MENU_PANE`, `MODEL_PICKER_PANE`, `TRUST_DIALOG_PANE`, `DEV_CHANNELS_DIALOG_PANE`, `MCP_SERVER_DIALOG_PANE`), replies that only quote one (`QUOTED_DIALOG_PANE`, `QUOTED_MENU_PANE`) and `withLastLine(line)`; Linux screens, which draw `●` before every reply and tool call (`LINUX_IDLE_PANE`, with ellipses in those lines, `CUSTOM_VERB_SPINNER_PANE`, a spinner with a custom verb of several words, and `linuxWithLastLine(line)`). Transcripts: entry builders (`promptEntry`, `toolUseEntry`, `toolResultEntry`, `rejectedToolResultEntry`, `replyEntry`, `systemEntry`, `queuedPromptEntry`, `localCommandEntries`, `interruptEntry`, `hookAttachmentEntry`, `endedTurn`), `transcriptJsonl`, and `writeTranscript` / `appendTranscript` at a path in the caller's temp directory, named `<TRANSCRIPT_SESSION_ID>.jsonl`. Build a Claude Code screen or transcript from these rather than writing a new one in a suite. Used by `tests/session-manager.test.ts`, `tests/server.test.ts` and `tests/session-transcript.test.ts`.

`makePersonaConfig` puts `mcp_config_path` under `baseDir`, but the loader defaults it to `~/.claude/slack-mcp.json` under the home you pass. When comparing against loader output, override `mcp_config_path` with the loader's value (e.g. `join(home, '.claude', 'slack-mcp.json')`).

### Credentials Fixtures and Leak Checks

Any test that touches Slack tokens or a persona credentials file uses the shared helper `tests/test-helpers/credentials.ts`. It builds fake tokens that all carry one marker, `LEAK_SENTINEL`, and checks captured output for that marker or any token-like value. A leak then fails a test instead of reaching a log.

| Export | Use it for |
|--------|------------|
| `LEAK_SENTINEL` | The marker every fake token embeds. It uses identifier characters only, so an error that quotes an identifier quotes the whole sentinel. Refer to the constant; never copy its value into a test. |
| `BOT_TOKEN_PREFIX`, `APP_TOKEN_PREFIX` | The Slack token prefixes, for building fakes and for asserting on rule text. A bare prefix is not a token. |
| `fakeToken(prefix, suffix?)` | One fake token built at runtime: the prefix, the digit `1`, the sentinel, then `-` and the suffix when one is given. The digit after the dash gives it a real token's shape, not just `TOKEN_LIKE`'s. Use distinct suffixes to tell two fakes apart; pass a wrong prefix or none for bad-prefix cases. |
| `sentinelInMessage(label, prefix?, marker?)` | The sentinel as a test error's *message* carries it: `fakeToken(prefix, label)`, a space, then `sentinelTicketUrl(STUB_WSS_ORIGIN, marker)`. `prefix` defaults to `BOT_TOKEN_PREFIX` (`APP_TOKEN_PREFIX` for a Socket Mode leg), `label` tells two fakes apart (`''` for none), and `marker` is the ticket (a Slack stub passes its `leakMarker`). Wrap it as the message needs, e.g. `` `failed (${sentinelInMessage('x')})` ``. |
| `REDACTED_SENTINEL_TAIL` | What `sentinelInMessage(…)` reads as after `redactSlackLogText` (`<redacted-token> <redacted-url>`), built from the redactor's own placeholders. Assert the logged `message="…"` holds it in the same wrapping as the thrown message. |
| `sentinelTicketUrl(origin?, marker?)`, `STUB_WSS_ORIGIN` | A Socket Mode WebSocket URL `<origin>/link/?ticket=<marker>`, the shape a real connect error quotes; `origin` defaults to `STUB_WSS_ORIGIN` (the Slack stub's `wss://` origin) and `marker` to `LEAK_SENTINEL`. For a URL on its own; a message uses `sentinelInMessage`. |
| `withoutName(captured, name, key)` | A copy of `captured` with a persona name and its key replaced by `<name>` / `<key>` (the longer first), with the same traversal as `assertNoLeak`: `assertNoLeak(withoutName(captured, name, key))`. Only for runs whose output legitimately holds a token-shaped name (built with `fakeToken`, so it carries the sentinel); anything else token-like, a credential value included, still fails. |
| `makeCredentials(overrides?)` | A credentials object. By default it is valid (exactly `bot_token` and `app_token`, distinct fakes); an override set to `undefined` removes that key, and any other override adds or replaces one. |
| `writeCredentialsFile(dir, name?, overrides?)` | Writes a credentials file with mode 0600 inside `dir` and returns its path. An object override merges over the valid defaults; a string override is the whole file content, for non-JSON or non-object cases. `dir` is required and `name` must stay inside it. |
| `writtenFile(path)` | Marks a file or directory the code under test wrote, so `assertNoLeak` checks its content. This is the only way to check a file: a plain string is always checked as text, never read as a path. |
| `TOKEN_LIKE`, `isTokenLike(text)` | The one definition of token-like that `assertNoLeak` applies: `xox` plus a lower-case letter, or `xapp`, then a dash and a letter or digit, with a boundary before the prefix (the start of the text or anything but an ASCII letter or digit). A bare prefix in rule text, a word such as "Xoxo bot" or a prefix glued to a word (`inboxapp-bot`) doesn't match. It needs no digit after the dash (a prefix followed by a letter is flagged too), so `assertNoLeak` errs on the strict side; `tests/persona-identity.test.ts` pins its rows. Not global: build a global copy from `TOKEN_LIKE.source` to find every occurrence. The secrecy audit uses the same matcher. |
| `assertNoLeak(captured, label?)` | Fails if the sentinel (any case) or a token-like value appears anywhere in `captured`. Strings, errors (message, stack, properties, `cause`, aggregated errors), arrays, objects, maps, sets, buffers and `writtenFile` marks are all checked, recursively. |

Rules:

- **Fakes only.** Tests use only tokens from `fakeToken`, `makeCredentials` or `writeCredentialsFile`, so every token embeds `LEAK_SENTINEL`. No test file contains a token literal, not even a fake one; build it at runtime from the prefix constants.
- **Check everything captured.** Every test that handles credentials runs `assertNoLeak` over its captured log lines, errors and results, including rejected calls and failure paths. Pass them together as one object so a failure names the leaking item (for example `captured.lines[2]`). Wrap any file the code wrote in `writtenFile`.
- **Override the token environment.** Nothing in `src/` reads `SLACK_BOT_TOKEN` or `SLACK_APP_TOKEN`, so tests normally leave them alone. A suite that sets either (for example to show that `start` or the connection manager ignores them) sets both to fakes for its whole run and restores the previous values afterwards, so an ambient real token can never reach a failure message.
- **Log errors by description.** Code under test logs a thrown value on a Slack or agent-director path through `describeThrownValue` (see the engineering guide's Error Handling), which keeps the message only as `message="…"` after `redactSlackLogText`. Log lines keep the redacted message, so a test error's message carries `LEAK_SENTINEL` only through `sentinelInMessage` (inside a `fakeToken()` and a `?ticket=` URL, the shapes redaction removes); its other properties (headers, `data`, `original`, bodies, extra fields CSCB must never read) carry it bare. The case asserts the line holds its type, safe `code` or `errName` and the redacted message (`REDACTED_SENTINEL_TAIL` in the message's wrapping), then runs `assertNoLeak` over everything logged. A line that skips redaction, or a redactor that loses either pattern, then fails `assertNoLeak`; a bare sentinel in a message would survive redaction, so it proves nothing about the describer.
- **Token-shaped persona names.** A persona name may be shaped like a Slack token (b.av2 SR-1.2: no format rule), and a test builds one with `fakeToken`, so it carries the sentinel. A run whose output must hold that name is checked with `assertNoLeak(withoutName(captured, name, key))`, never left out of the check: the name and key are masked and everything else, credential values included, is still checked.
- **Why the failure is safe.** `assertNoLeak` reports which item leaked and whether it held the sentinel or a token-like value, never the leaked text. Rule text such as "must start with" a bare prefix passes.

Isolation (SR-13.2) applies to credentials files, crontables, cron logs and working-directory fixtures exactly as it does to config files:

- Create them only under the test's own `mkdtempSync` directory, pass every path explicitly, and remove the directory in `afterEach`. `writeCredentialsFile` has no default location and refuses a name that escapes `dir`.
- Never read or write `~/.claude/channels/slack` or `~/.agent-director`, and never point a credentials path at the real home.
- To test code that falls back to `os.homedir()`, use `runInFakeHome` from `tests/test-helpers/fake-home-subprocess.ts`. Setting `process.env.HOME` in a test doesn't work: Bun fixes `os.homedir()` at start-up from the launch-time HOME, which is also why the preload guard's HOME (see Host safety under Isolation) moves only what reads the environment. The helper runs the module call in a child `bun` process whose `env` is a direct `hostSafeChildEnv` call: HOME is the fake home, `PATH` holds only the directories of `FAKE_HOME_CHILD_TOOLS` (`bun`, and `sh` for code that shells out), `TMUX_TMPDIR` is the process's one fenced temp directory, and `SLACK_STATE_DIR` and the JSON input are the extras. Nothing else from `process.env` reaches the child, so no token does. Assert `observedHomedir` equals the fake home before checking anything else. The helper refuses the real home (`isRealHome`) before any child starts, `hostSafeChildEnv` throws for a fake home or tool directory that holds agent-director, and a child that hangs past its timeout (30 s by default) or dies from a signal throws. `tests/trust-bootstrap.test.ts` and `tests/stop-hook-bootstrap.test.ts` are the references.
- Code that can call `recordStartupError` writes `startup-errors.log` under `SLACK_STATE_DIR`, which the preload guard points under its shared temp HOME (unset, it would default to a path under the launch-time home). Point `SLACK_STATE_DIR` at the test's own temp directory for the whole file in `beforeEach` and restore the environment (the preload's value) in `afterEach`, as `tests/stop-hook-bootstrap.test.ts` does. A test that reads the log points it at its own directory.
- A test that needs a per-persona reply-guard record (`reply-guard/<key>`) builds it with `makeReplyGuardRecordDir` from `tests/test-helpers/reply-guard-record.ts`. It creates a `mkdtempSync` state directory (under the OS temp directory or the test's own `parentDir`) with its `reply-guard/` record directory, writes records byte-for-byte, and removes what it created with `cleanup()`. It never reads `SLACK_STATE_DIR` or HOME.
- When a test compares real paths (for example a symlinked credentials file shared with another persona), resolve the temp directory with `realpathSync` first; the OS temp directory may itself be a symlink.

Permission cases (an unreadable credentials file, an unreadable or unsearchable working directory) inject a failing operation through the check's file-system seam, so they pass under root in docker CI. The credentials seam is `openFile`, `fstatFile`, `readFileFd`, `closeFile` and `realpath`; the working-directory seam is `stat`, `access` and `realpath`, and the combined `PersonaBringUpFs` carries both. For an unreadable credentials file, make `openFile` or `readFileFd` throw an errno-style error (`EACCES`, `EPERM`, `EIO`). To test a credentials path that is not a regular file (a FIFO or a device), make `fstatFile` report `isFile()` false, assert `readFileFd` is never called and `closeFile` is called for the opened descriptor. Because the real open is non-blocking, a real FIFO, directory or `/dev/zero` is also safe to use as a control. The config reader (`readPersonaConfigBytes`, used for `config.json` and the last-applied record) has the same seam, `PersonaConfigFs`, and the same rule, reached through `configFs` on the reload controller and `readAppliedPersonaConfig`. A durable write whose directory open or fsync fails after the rename (`DurableWriteUnsyncedError`), or a durable delete whose directory open or fsync fails after the unlink (`DurableUnlinkUnsyncedError`), is driven through the `DurableWriteFs` seam. The reload detection tick's credentials reads take the same `CredentialsFs` seam, through `opts.credentialsFs` on a reload harness run. A variant that uses real permission bits is marked `test.skipIf(isRoot)`, with the reason in the test name. Restore any mode a test changed before `afterEach` removes the directory.

A test that could hang on a blocking open or read (a real FIFO with no writer) runs the risky call in a child `bun` process (`spawnSync` with a `timeout`, typically 10 s, and its `env` a direct `hostSafeChildEnv` call) and asserts the child was not killed (`child.signal` is null), with a per-test timeout above that bound. A test timeout can't interrupt a blocking synchronous call, so in-process a regression would hang the whole suite instead of failing one test. Make the FIFO with `makeFifo` from `tests/test-helpers/fifo.ts` (the reload harness's `h.makeFifo` delegates to it), never by spawning `mkfifo` yourself, and skip the case where `mkfifo` is missing (`test.skipIf(!mkfifoAvailable())`, the reason in the test name). The reload harness (`tests/test-helpers/reload-harness.ts`, its `mkfifoAvailable` and `h.makeFifo`) is the reference.

#### Secrecy Audit

`tests/secrecy-audit.test.ts` enforces these rules across the suite, and the describe-don't-echo logging rule across `src/` (b.av2 SR-10.3 closing audit, AC 20). It reads files under `tests/` and `src/` only, comment-stripped, and anchors on content, never line numbers.

- **Which suites it covers.** A suite touches config, credentials or reload when it has a value import (not `import type`, not a `type` specifier, not a SCREAMING_CASE constant) from a source module in its `SOURCE_SURFACES` list (the config loader, the credentials reader, the bring-up checks and controller, the `src/persona-slack-*.ts` client, logger and validation modules, the connection manager and its error describer, the Web API watch, the diagnostics, the start procedure, the persona lifecycle, the log redactor, the reload modules, and the modules whose output carries agent-director or Slack failure text: the MCP registry, the session manager, restart, the permission poller and click handler, the notifier, the destination modules, the health check, the CLI, the template module, the agent-director error classifier, the agent-director settings reader, the UNAVAILABLE retry timer and the persona episodes with their `tmux-unresponsive` condition), imports one of the helpers in `HELPER_SURFACES` (`fakeToken`, `makeCredentials`, `writeCredentialsFile`, `LEAK_SENTINEL`, `sentinelInMessage`, `sentinelTicketUrl`, `writeConfigFile`, `writeAgentDirectorConfig`, `makeReloadHarness`, `makeConnectionHarness`, `makeRoutingHarness`, `makeRecoveryHarness`, `makeStubSlackFactory`), or sets a Slack stub's `leakMarker`.
- **What a covered suite must do.** Import `assertNoLeak` from `tests/test-helpers/credentials.ts` and call it over its captured logs, errors, written files and tool results, or the audit fails naming the suite and why it counts as touching. A suite that builds `makeReloadHarness` must pass a run's `captured()` to it.
- **Exemptions.** A suite that matches but has no secret-bearing surface (nothing it logs, throws, returns or writes can hold a token) goes in the audit's `EXEMPT` map with a one-line reason. An exemption with no reason, for a missing file, for a suite that no longer touches, or for one that now calls `assertNoLeak` fails as stale; remove it. The named legs (`config`, `persona-connections`, `reload`, `reload-apply`) can't be exempted.
- **Literals.** No file under `tests/`, helpers, fixtures and scripts included, may hold a token-like value, and `LEAK_SENTINEL`'s value may appear only in the credentials helper. Build near-miss strings from the prefix constants (for example `BOT_TOKEN_PREFIX.slice(0, 3)`) as `tests/persona-identity.test.ts` does.
- **A new source module** that reads config or credentials, builds Slack clients or runs reload belongs in `SOURCE_SURFACES`; a new helper that builds tokens, credentials or config files belongs in `HELPER_SURFACES`.
- **Named legs.** `config` must reject a persona entry holding a fake token under `bot_token` and under `app_token` and one with a computed, token-named key; `persona-connections` must assert `attachOriginalToWebAPIRequestError` false on a socket client's `clientOptions` and on a Web API client's own options, drive `createUnhandledRejectionHandler` and keep its `rejected Web API calls` block; `reload` and `reload-apply` must leak-check a run's `captured()`. Renaming those rows or blocks fails the audit.
- **Source audit.** Every log call under `src/` (a console method, or `log`, `logFailure`, `logViaDeps`, `logDeps` (the click handler's log sink), `safeLog` (the persona episodes' guarded log), `recordStartupError` or `fatal`, bare or on an object) is checked for raw error text: a caught error passed as an argument, interpolated, read for its `message` or `stack`, or handed to a function that is not a safe describer (`String(err)`), an `errDescription` not through `redactSlackLogText`, or an `errName` not checked by `isSafeIdentifier`. A message is allowed only inside a safe describer call: every describer call, its arguments included, is blanked before the rules run, so `redactSlackLogText(err.message)` or `describeLogMessage(err.message)` passes while a bare `err.message` beside it is flagged. A caught error is a `catch` or `.catch(…)` binding, a name like `e`, `err`, `error` or `<word>Err`, or a name set from a raw use of one. The safe describers are listed in `SAFE_DESCRIBERS` (`describeThrownValue`, `describeLogMessage`, `describeSlackCallFailure`, `describeAgentDirectorFailure`, `describeCliFailure`, `describeRefreshFailure`, `redactSlackLogText`, `isSafeIdentifier`, `slackPlatformReason`, `errnoSuffix`), each of which must still be declared under `src/`; a local sink that passes its error only through one is a describing wrapper and not itself a sink. The rule table is pinned by snippet checks, positive and negative.
- **The allow-list.** A deliberate exception (a path that is not a Slack or agent-director one, or text CSCB wrote; see the engineering guide's Error Handling) goes in `RAW_ERROR_ALLOWED` as a file, an anchor (a stable fragment of the call's text, never a line number) and a reason. An entry with no reason, for a missing file or matching no flagged call fails as stale. Fix a new finding by describing the error, not by adding an entry, unless the path really is one of those exceptions.

### Slack Stub

New persona and connection suites fake Slack with the shared helper `tests/test-helpers/slack-stub.ts`, not with hand-rolled WebClient or SocketModeClient stubs. `makeStubSlack(opts?)` builds one independent fake Slack for one persona. It has no module-scope state, network, file system, environment access or timers, and its error shapes follow the installed `@slack/web-api` and `@slack/socket-mode` libraries.

| Member | What it gives a test |
|--------|----------------------|
| `identity` | The bot user ID, bot ID and team ID a successful `auth.test` returns. The bot user ID and bot ID default to random values, distinct per stub; the team ID defaults to a fixed value. Override them through `botUserId`, `botId` and `teamId`. |
| `script` | Outcome queues `authTest`, `connect` (socket `start()`), `post` (`chat.postMessage`), `update` (`chat.update`), `upload` (`filesUploadV2`), `history` (`conversations.history`), `replies` (`conversations.replies`), `info` (`conversations.info`), `open` (`conversations.open`), `reactionsAdd`, `reactionsRemove`, `usersInfo` and `apiCall` (the generic `apiCall(method, options)`, whatever the method). Seed them through the options of the same names (every queue but `open`), or push onto them at any time; a test scripts `conversations.open` by pushing onto `script.open`. Each call takes the next outcome; an empty queue means success. |
| `calls` | Capture arrays of call arguments: `authTest`, `postMessage`, `update`, `reactionsAdd`, `reactionsRemove`, `conversationsOpen`, `usersInfo`, `filesUploadV2`, `conversationsHistory`, `conversationsReplies`, `conversationsInfo`, `apiCall` (each `{ method, options }`). Every Web API client of the stub shares them. |
| `callLog` | Every Web API call in call order, as `StubWebCall` entries (`method`, a `StubWebMethod` such as `'conversations.open'`, and `args` exactly as passed). A call is logged when it is made, so a failed call is logged too. The stub's `callLog` holds the calls of all its Web API clients; each client's `callLog` holds only its own. Assert an order with `callLog.map((c) => c.method)`, for example `['conversations.open', 'chat.postMessage']`, and no call at all with `toEqual([])`. |
| `web` | A ready Web API client with default options, for direct calls. Its bot token is the `token` option; the default is a sentinel-bearing fake token with a random suffix, distinct per stub. |
| `createWebClient(token?, options?)` | Builds a Web API client as the real constructor would; recorded in `options.web`. Without `token` it uses a fixed sentinel-bearing fake token, the same for every stub. |
| `createSocketClient(options?)` | Builds a socket client; recorded in `options.socket` and `sockets`. Throws on an empty app token, as the real constructor does. |
| `apiCall`, `filesUploadV2` | As on the real `WebClient`, these two live on a shared prototype and need the client as `this`; a detached call throws a `TypeError`, so a wrapper that loses `this` fails in tests as it would against Slack. A wrapper whose prototype is the client (the Web API watch's) reaches them through the prototype chain. The namespace methods (`chat.postMessage`, …) are bound per client and work detached. |
| `token`, `hasToken(expected)` | Every Web API client carries the bot token it was built with as `token`, like `WebClient.token`. It is not enumerable, so printing or comparing a client never shows it; assert with `hasToken(expected)`, which prints no token on failure. |
| `options` | The options every client was built with, in build order (`web`, `socket`). |
| `sockets`, `socket` | Every socket client built, and the latest one (`socket` throws if none was built). |

**Opening a DM.** An unscripted `conversations.open` succeeds with a `D…` ID derived from the requested `users`: `stubOpenedDmId(users)` returns `D0OPN` followed by the user IDs without their `U`/`W` prefix (`U0BOB` gives `D0OPN0BOB`), so each user gets its own ID and none equals the event factories' default DM (`D0STUB0001`). Use `stubOpenedDmId` to compute the ID a test expects the post to go to. To choose the ID, push `openedDm(id)` onto `script.open`; to fail the open, push a `platform` outcome such as `missing_scope`, `user_not_found` or `cannot_dm_bot`.

**Passing a stub client as a `WebClient`.** `WebClient` is a class with private members, so no plain object satisfies it. Where code under test takes a `WebClient` (a persona's client in the registry's `SessionToolDeps.clientFor`, the connection manager's factory), pass `asWebClient(client)`, the helper's one cast, rather than casting in the test file. `makeStubSlackFactory()` already uses it.

The socket stub behaves like `SocketModeClient` with auto-reconnect off:

- `start()` takes the next `connect` outcome and first runs the connect leg (`apps.connections.open`). A failure there (the Web API kinds, `no-url` or `reject`) rejects `start()` with no lifecycle event.
- Otherwise `start()` emits `authenticated` and `connecting`, and the WebSocket phase settles on a later microtask according to the outcome. `startCalls` counts calls, and `start()` can be called again after a drop, so a test can drive a reopen.
- `drop()` is Slack closing the connection, the case the connection manager reopens: it emits `close` and `disconnected`, and a `start()` still waiting for `hello` rejects with no value. `disconnect()` is the client's own close, which is not reopened: it is counted in `disconnectCalls` and emits `disconnecting`, then `close` (if a WebSocket phase ever began) and `disconnected`.
- `deliver(event)` sends an Events API event and `deliverInteractive(payload)` an interactive payload, in the shape `server.ts` consumes. Both need a connected socket and resolve once every listener settles.
- `acks` records each ack in order; `lifecycle` records every lifecycle event emitted, in order.

**Scripted outcomes.** Each queue entry is one outcome kind:

| Kind | Result |
|------|--------|
| `ok` | Success. On `auth.test`, `post`, `update`, `upload`, `history`, `replies`, `info` and `open`, an optional `result` is merged over the default response; a key set to `undefined` is removed. |
| `platform` | A named Slack error (an `ok: false` answer) with that `error`, and an optional `retryAfter` in the response metadata. |
| `network`, `dns`, `timeout`, `http`, `rate-limited` | Slack unreachable: a request error, an HTTP error with a status, or a rate-limited error with `retryAfter` in seconds. |
| `no-url` | Connect only: `apps.connections.open` answered without a URL. `start()` rejects with a plain error on the connect leg, with no lifecycle event. |
