| `closed-before-hello`, `websocket-error` | Connect only: the WebSocket closes before `hello`. `start()` rejects with no value after `close` and `disconnected`; `websocket-error` emits `error` first. |
| `never` | Connect: `apps.connections.open` answers, `authenticated` and `connecting` are emitted, and the WebSocket phase never reaches `hello`; `start()` ends only on `drop()` or `disconnect()`. Web API calls: the call never settles. |
| `open-never-answers` | Connect only: `apps.connections.open` never answers. `start()` stays pending for good with no lifecycle event; `disconnect()` does not end it. |
| `deferred` | Connect: built with `makeDeferredConnect()`. `start()` waits at the connect leg until the test calls `settle(outcome?)` (default `ok`), then proceeds as that outcome. Settling after the manager abandoned the `start()` drives a late settlement. Web API calls: built with `makeDeferredWebApiCall()`; push its `outcome` onto any Web API queue (`script.open`, `script.post`, …) and each call that takes it waits until `settle(outcome?)` (default `ok`, any kind but `deferred`), then answers as that outcome. Use it to hold an open or post in flight, for example to show concurrent posts share one open or that an attempt settling after `cancel` changes nothing. `settled` says whether it was settled; a second `settle` throws. |
| `reject` | The call rejects with the given value exactly as given (a plain error, a string, `undefined` and so on). |

Set `leakMarker` to `LEAK_SENTINEL` and every error the stub builds carries the marker wherever a real error can hold secrets: the message, the wrapped `original` error and its headers, HTTP headers and body, fields of `data`, and the error the socket's `error` event carries. In a message (the error's own, the `original`'s, the `error` event's, the `apps.connections.open` failure's) the marker sits only inside a fake token and a `wss://…?ticket=` URL, since log lines keep a message after `redactSlackLogText`; everywhere else it is bare, since CSCB must never read those fields. `data.error` stays exactly as scripted, so classification still sees the real Slack error code.

Not every failure carries the marker. A rejection with no value carries nothing: `closed-before-hello`, and the `start()` rejection after `websocket-error`. `reject` passes its value unchanged, so plant the sentinel in the value if the row needs it. Run `assertNoLeak` over what the code under test produced (see Credentials Fixtures and Leak Checks).

**Event factories.** `makeChannelMessage`, `makeDm`, `makeBotMessage`, `makeWebhookPost` and `makeAppMention` build Slack events with defaults and overrides (a key set to `undefined` is removed). Text builders for message bodies:

- `mentionText(userId, label?)`: a user mention, `<@U…>`, or `<@U…|label>` with a label. Pass the stub's `identity.botUserId` to mention that stub's bot.
- `broadcastText(kind?, label?)`: `<!here>` (the default), `<!channel>` or `<!everyone>`, or the labelled form such as `<!here|here>`.
- `userGroupMentionText(groupId?, label?)`: a user-group mention, `<!subteam^S…>`, or the labelled form, with a fixed default group ID. It mentions no persona; use it to prove that a user-group mention does not deliver.

**Reference example.** The `Slack validation classification (both legs, sentinel-bearing errors)` describe block in `tests/persona-connections.test.ts` is a table test. Each row scripts one outcome and runs it against a fresh sentinel-bearing stub on each leg it applies to: `auth.test`, `socket-mode` or both. It checks the class, the reason fields and `assertNoLeak` on everything captured.

### Slack Library Log Probes

Tests of what the Slack libraries print (the socket logger's allowlist, the redacting Web API logger, the production factory's real clients) use `tests/test-helpers/slack-logger-probes.ts`, shared by `tests/persona-connections.test.ts` and `tests/persona-connection-wiring.test.ts`. Don't redefine these probes in a suite.

| Export | Use it for |
|--------|------------|
| `ticketUrl(origin)` | A WebSocket URL as `apps.connections.open` returns it, its connection ticket carrying `LEAK_SENTINEL`, so any line that prints it fails `assertNoLeak`. |
| `PONG_TIMEOUT_TEXT` | The library's pong-timeout warning, an allowlisted connection-health line. |
| `socketLogLine(persona, text)` | The exact `[slack] persona Socket Mode:` line a persona's socket logger writes for `text`. |
| `outputDuring(fn)`, `outputDuringAsync(fn)` | Every call the console methods, `process.stdout.write` and `process.stderr.write` received while `fn` ran (or until its promise settled), all silenced and then restored, a suite's own spy included. Pass the result to `assertNoLeak` before any assertion that could print it. |

### Fake Clock

Persona suites that need virtual time use the shared helper `tests/test-helpers/fake-clock.ts`, not hand-rolled single-shot timer fakes. The connection manager takes its clock and timers by injection, so tests pass it a clock from `createFakeClock` rather than using Bun's global fake timers. The helper also flushes pending promise continuations after each timer it fires and lets a test list the pending timers, which is what driving the backoff, the 10 s bounds on an `auth.test` or `start()` that never settles, and the bring-up and reopen retry timers without sleeping needs.

`createFakeClock({ start?, flushTurns? })` builds one independent clock. Its `now`, `setTimeout` and `clearTimeout` satisfy the connection manager's `PersonaConnectionClock`, so a test passes the clock with no cast. Virtual time moves only when the test moves it.

| Member | What it gives a test |
|--------|----------------------|
| `now()` | The current virtual time in ms (default start 0). |
| `advance(ms)`, `advanceTo(time)` | Move virtual time forward, firing every due timer in due-time order, including timers that callbacks schedule during the move. Resolves with the number fired. |
| `runNext()` | Jump to the earliest pending timer and fire every timer due then. |
| `flush()` | Let pending promise continuations run without moving time. The clock does the same before, between and after firings, so a stub's settled promise chain finishes before the next timer fires. |
| `pending()`, `pendingCount()`, `firedCount()` | Introspection: each pending timer's requested `delayMs`, `dueAt` and `scheduledAt`, earliest first; the pending count; callbacks fired so far. |

`createPersonaEpisodes` takes the same clock shape as its optional `clock` (`PersonaEpisodesClock`, absent the system clock): pass a fake clock and move it with `advance` to check an episode's `startedAt`; production passes none, which `server-startup-wiring.test.ts` pins. The `tmux-unresponsive` condition's alert check (b.jg5 SRJ-309) is a timer on that same clock, so a test drives it with `advance` and sees it in `pending()`; the recovery harness passes `alertThresholdMs: false` where a case wants the retry timer as the only timer.

Code that only reads the time takes a plain `() => number` clock (the inbound dedupe store, the routing harness's `dedupeClock`); pass the fake clock's `now` and move time with `advance`, never `Date.now` or a hand-written counter.

Code that reads the time through a module seam takes the fake clock's `now` there: the stale-`working`-row wait and evidence in `src/session-manager.ts` (`_setNow`, reset with `_resetNow` in `afterEach`; bug b.f2b). Move it with `await clock.advance(ms)`, between reconnect attempts or from the agent-director stub's status poll, one step per poll; the wait's poll interval is still a real timer (1 ms in these cases). Never freeze `Date.now()` with Bun's `setSystemTime` for this.

A zero, negative or overlong delay fires after 1 ms, as in Bun, but `pending()` still reports the requested `delayMs`. Assert on the requested delay to check what the code asked for.

A derived wait (G, the alert threshold or B) that can be longer than the timer maximum (`MAX_TIMER_DELAY_MS`) is armed with `armNeverEarlyWait` from `src/ad-settings.ts`, never a plain `setTimeout`; its test drives the chain with `runNext()`, asserting before each step that at most one timer is pending and none asks more than the maximum, then that the callback ran once exactly at the deadline. A wait that must follow the values in effect is armed with a getter (such as `adLaunchBoundMsInEffect`) rather than a number, so the helper reads it again at every timer fire; its test changes the value while the wait is armed and asserts that a raised value never ends the wait at the old deadline and a lowered one ends it at the next fire or exactly at the new deadline.

### Backoff and Retry Tests

Backoff, retry and timeout tests never wait in real time.

- **Pure schedules are called, not timed.** `createPersonaRetrySchedule` holds no timer and reads no clock: it returns the next wait. Tests drive it by calling `nextDelayMs` and asserting on the returned sequence, as the `backoff schedule` describe block does.
- **Timers and clocks are injected.** Code that schedules a wait, or abandons a call that never settles, takes its clock and timers as parameters. Tests pass the shared fake clock (see Fake Clock) and advance it; they never sleep past a real delay.
- **The UNAVAILABLE retry timer runs only on the fake clock.** `src/unavailable-retry.ts` takes its clock and timers by injection, so its tests drive it on `createFakeClock()`, bare or through `makeRecoveryHarness`, and never on a real timer; the restart module's real-timer exception (see Serialization and Teardown Tests) does not extend to it. The harness's full-mode retries call the restart module's retry entry, which arms no restart timer, so they need no real wait beyond `settle()`. The one real 0 ms `setTimeout` in its attempt-frames case (listed there) is no retry timer. Take its waits from `UNAVAILABLE_RETRY_BASE_S` and `UNAVAILABLE_RETRY_CEILING_S` through `doublingBackoffDelay`; the SRD's numbers appear only in the one pin case.
- **Never-settling cases.** A `never` outcome stays pending for good, so the test ends it through the stub (`drop()`, `disconnect()`) or through the injected clock, never by waiting for it.
- **`retryAfter` rows.** Script `retryAfter` values above and below the current step, and assert that the wait is never shorter than `retryAfter`. The outcome carries `retryAfter` in seconds and the schedule returns milliseconds, so convert before comparing. The `retryAfter` describe block is the reference.
- **One exception: the real spawn path.** A case that launches through the real `spawnForPersona` polls in real time for the spawn only (see Connection Wiring and Bring-Up Tests); its backoff and retry waits stay on the fake clock.
- **Leak checks still apply.** Every captured log line and error passes through `assertNoLeak`.

### Destination Failure Tests

A post to a persona's destination that fails is held and retried by the destination hold (`src/persona-destination-hold.ts`), shared by the permission poller and the notifier. Test it on a fake clock, never in real time.

- **Always inject a fake-clock hold.** Any test that can hold a notice or prompt passes a `destinationHold` built with `createPersonaDestinationHold({ destinations, getPersona, clientFor, clock: createFakeClock(), log })`. The notifier's and the poller's default hold uses the real clock, so a held notice would start a real timer. To build the resolver, the fake-clock hold and the notifier together, as `server.ts` wires them, call `makeNotifierStack({ getPersona, clientFor, log, clock?, isDryRun? })` from `tests/test-helpers/persona-notifier.ts`; it returns `notifier`, `hold`, `destinations` and `clock`. `makeNotifierHarness` (`h.clock`, `h.hold`), `makeRoutingHarness` (`h.holdClock`, `h.hold`) and `makeManagedRouting` are built on it; `makeHold` in `tests/permission-poller.test.ts` does it for the poller.
- **Make the failure sticky.** One scripted failure is used up by the first attempt, so the first retry would succeed. To keep the cause in place across retries, fill the queue: `stub.script.open.push(...Array<WebApiOutcome>(1000).fill({ kind: 'platform', error: 'missing_scope' }))` (or `script.post` with `not_in_channel`).
- **Clear the cause.** `stub.script.open.length = 0` empties the queue, so the next call succeeds; push `openedDm(id)` after it to choose the DM.
- **Drive the retries.** `clock.pending()[0].delayMs` is the wait the hold asked for (5 000, then 10 000, doubling to 300 000); `clock.runNext()` fires the next retry and `clock.advance(ms)` moves time, for example to show nothing is attempted 1 ms before the due time. A poller prompt has no timer of its own: advance the clock past the due time, then `tick()`.
- **Assert the episode, not the attempts.** One `persona-destination-failed` start line per episode and one `cleared:` line after the cause clears, no line per failed retry, each held item posted exactly once after it clears, and `hold.view(key)` (`held`, `heldNotices`, `nextDueAt`). For a notice, `onPostFailure` runs once, at its first failed attempt.
- **Clean up.** A hold that can hold notices sets retry timers: call `hold.cancelAll()` in `afterEach`, and assert `clock.pendingCount()` is 0 once a case's held items are delivered. A hold used only by the poller sets no timer.

`tests/persona-destination-hold.test.ts` is the reference for the hold alone; the `destination failures are held and retried` describe block in `tests/persona-notifier.test.ts` and the `a destination failure holds the persona's prompts` block in `tests/permission-poller.test.ts` for the two callers.

### Connection Manager Tests

Connection manager tests build `createPersonaConnectionManager` from injected fakes only: the `factory` from `makeStubSlackFactory()` (in `tests/test-helpers/slack-stub.ts`) and the `clock` from `createFakeClock()`, which supplies both the clock and the timers. Always pass both: an omitted `factory` falls back to `PRODUCTION_SLACK_CLIENT_FACTORY` (real Slack clients) and an omitted `clock` to `SYSTEM_PERSONA_CONNECTION_CLOCK` (real timers). A test never builds a real Slack client.

| `makeStubSlackFactory()` member | What it gives a test |
|--------|----------------------|
| `factory` | The `PersonaSlackClientFactory` to pass to the manager, uncast. It routes each build to the persona whose token it received: the app token for a socket client, the bot token for the validation and long-lived Web API clients. |
| `addPersona(key, tokens, opts?)` | Registers a persona with its own `makeStubSlack(opts)` stub, as its `INITIAL_CREDENTIALS` set. No identity, script queue, capture or socket is shared with another persona. |
| `addCredentials(key, label, tokens, opts?)`, `credentials(key, label)` | Registers another credential set (a rotated pair) for a registered persona, with its own stub: its own identity, script queues, captures and sockets. Every build that receives one of its tokens goes to that stub and is recorded with `persona: key` and `credentials: label`, so a persona's old and new tokens are scripted and asserted apart. `credentials(key, label)` is that set's stub. |
| `activity`, `activityOf(key)` | What happened to every client built, in order: `built`, `started`, `connected` (reached `hello`), `disconnected` (the client's own close) and `discarded` (its last listener removed), each with its `kind`, `persona` and `credentials` label. A credentials reconnect that opens before it closes reads `connected` for the new set before `discarded` and `disconnected` for the old one. A drop by Slack is the test's own act and is not recorded. |
| `persona(key)` | That persona's `INITIAL_CREDENTIALS` stub. `socket` is its latest socket client and `sockets` every one built, abandoned ones included; clients built from a set added with `addCredentials` are on that set's stub. |
| `builds`, `buildsOf(key, kind?)` | Every client built, in build order, each with its `kind` (`socket`, `validation` or `web`), `persona`, `credentials` label and `options` exactly as received. |
| `hasToken(expected)` | On each build record: whether the client got exactly that token. The token itself is not enumerable, so a failing assertion never prints it. |

Rules:

- **Check client options through the build records.** Assert on `options` in `builds` or `buildsOf`, per client kind. A socket build's options hold the app token, so drop that field before comparing. Check tokens with `hasToken`, never by reading or printing the token.
- **Use the file-level harness.** `makeHarness()` in `tests/persona-connections.test.ts` builds personas A and B, their credentials files and stubs (leak marker on), the manager, the fake clock and the captured lines, statuses and events. Its `afterEach` stops every harness manager with `stopAll()` and runs `assertNoLeak` over everything captured. A new connection test uses it rather than building a second harness.
- **Isolation tests check both personas.** Whenever one persona fails, drops, is rejected or hangs, the test also delivers on the healthy persona (`expectDelivers`) and checks its events still arrive, tagged with that persona. Asserting only on the failing persona does not show isolation.

**Reference example.** The `AC 5: per-persona isolation walk-through (fake clock)` describe block in `tests/persona-connections.test.ts` is the model. Each case is named "AC 5: …". A's socket drops and reopens at once while B keeps delivering. A rejected reopen waits 5 s, then 10 s, and B keeps delivering throughout. A reopen whose `start()` never settles is abandoned at 10 s on the fake clock and retried. The case `AC 5: no agent-director call is made for A across a drop, a rejected reopen, an abandoned reopen and a refused reopen` counts calls on an agent-director recorder built with `makeStubClient` and asserts zero.

### Connection Wiring and Bring-Up Tests

The server's per-persona wiring lives in importable modules, so test it there, never through `main()`:

- **Connection seams and the event router.** Build personas on the real connection manager with `makeConnectionHarness(specs, baseDir, opts?)` from `tests/test-helpers/persona-connection-harness.ts`. It uses the shared stub factory and a fake clock, and builds `clientFor` and the identity lookup with the same `persona-start.ts` factories `server.ts` uses. Its `onEvent` and `onStatus` are late-bound, so a test can build the real `createPersonaEventRouter` or `createPersonaUpFlushListener` over the harness and plug it in. With `files: true` it writes each persona's credentials file and working directory under `baseDir`, so the real start procedure passes steps 1–2. The `tokens` option (by persona name, each built with `fakeToken`) registers a given token set (and, with `files: true`, writes it) instead of the default fakes, for example a rotated one. The `serialize` option hands the manager a serializer's `run`, as `server.ts` passes `personaLifecycle.run`; without it the manager gets none, so the network close of a socket detached by a refused Web API call (bug b.ujn) runs at once instead of waiting for the persona's turn. Pass it in any test about that window. `h.connections` has only `bringUp`: it records each call in `h.bringUpCalls` and appends `slack:<key>` to `h.order`, a shared step log the test may add its own markers to. Call `h.manager.stopAll()` after each test. `tests/persona-connection-wiring.test.ts` is the reference.
- **Real routing over the harness.** To drive the real `createPersonaRouting` with the manager's identities and clients, build it with `makeManagedRouting(h, baseDir, { archive? })` from `tests/test-helpers/persona-routing-managed.ts`, which wires it as `server.ts` does and registers a session per persona. Its `notify` is the real persona notifier (from `makeNotifierStack`) over the harness's persona and client lookups, with its hold on its own fake clock, and its user-name lookup is `users.info` on the persona's own client (the user ID when it has none). It returns only `routing`, `logs` and `notifications`; for a test that asserts notices or the hold, use `makeRoutingHarness` (see Inbound delivery below). Don't hand-wire the routing deps. The caller owns `initRestart`, `resetRoutingState()` and `h.manager.stopAll()`.
- **The start procedure.** Drive the real bring-up controller, `createPersonaBringUpController`, alone (`controller.bringUp(persona, applied)`) or as `startupSessionManager`'s `bringUp`. Build it over a harness with `files: true`: `{ bringUp: h.connections.bringUp, status: (key) => h.manager.status(key), stop: (key, endReason) => h.manager.stop(key, endReason) }` (or the manager itself) as `connections`, since the controller needs `status` and `stop` (a `claude_config_dir` hold closes the persona's connection) as well as `bringUp`, `h.clock` as `clock`, an injected `checkConfigDir` (see Isolation), the `fs` seam for credentials and directory failures, and a recording `launch` (or one that calls the real `spawnForPersona`, as `server.ts` does). Compose `controller.onConnectionStatus` into `h.onStatus` with `composePersonaStatusListeners`, so a persona that reaches `up` through a retry is launched. Assert the step order, each persona's outcome (`up`, `broken` or `retrying`) through `controller.state(key)` or the start pass's `perPersona`, that a broken or retrying persona launches nothing, and, in dry run, that no credentials file is read. `tests/persona-bringup.test.ts` is the reference for the order; `makeBringUpFixture` in `tests/persona-connections.test.ts` (`bring-up outcomes (E5)`) for outcomes and retry timers. Its `dropSessions` option wires the controller's `onLeftUp` as `server.ts` does (`createNotUpSessionDropper` over the real `dropPersonaSession`), for cases where a persona that stops being up must lose its MCP session.
- **MCP admission of a not-up persona.** `server.ts`'s initialized handler can't be imported, so decide a session through the real `decideSessionAdmission` with the production up check (`createPersonaUpPredicate` over the manager and the controller) and have the test promote an admitted session or close a refused one, as that handler does. `registry.test.ts` (`not-up personas: MCP session admission and drop`) and the surviving-instance cases in `persona-connections.test.ts` are the references; a source audit in `server-startup-wiring.test.ts` pins what the handler itself does with a refusal.
- **Retry timers.** Directory re-checks and Slack retries run on the harness's fake clock: advance it through the backoff steps and assert on the requested delays and the stat times, never sleep. In `afterEach`, call `controller.cancelAll()` and `h.manager.stopAll()`, then assert `h.clock.pendingCount()` is 0, so no retry timer leaks into the next test.
- **The real spawn path uses real timers.** `spawnForPersona` (its dialog polling and tmux probes) does not take the fake clock, so a case that launches through it waits for the spawn by polling in real time, in 1 ms steps with a 2 s cap, then asserts. Shorten the spawn path's own waits first (`_setDialogPollIntervalMs(1)`, `_setDialogReadyTimeoutMs(200)`, the tmux fakes). Keep every Slack and retry wait on the fake clock.
- **The start pool.** Drive `startupSessionManager` with the controller as `bringUp` and launches held open, so the test can show every persona's Slack bring-up happens before any launch settles, that no more than `concurrency` launches run at once, in readiness order, that a broken or retrying persona uses no slot, and that a retrying persona that comes up later is launched outside the pool. The `startupSessionManager` bring-up cases in `tests/session-manager.test.ts` are the reference.
- **Isolation still applies.** Every persona path sits under the test's `mkdtempSync` directory, and a persona failing its bring-up is checked alongside a healthy one (see Connection Manager Tests).

A test that needs the persona key an action ID was built for uses `personaKeyFromActionId` from `tests/test-helpers/action-id-key.ts`; production takes the key from the receiving connection instead.

### Serialization and Teardown Tests

Every lifecycle operation for a persona runs through the per-persona serializer (`src/persona-serializer.ts`), and the teardown in `src/persona-lifecycle.ts` is the one path that clears a persona's state.

- **Controllable promises, never delays.** Serializer tests settle each operation from the test (a `Promise.withResolvers` gate) and check order after flushing continuations; no timer and no real delay. Elsewhere, hold a persona's turn by submitting a gated operation to the same serializer the code under test uses (`f.serializer.run(key, …)` in `makeBringUpFixture`, the fixture's serializer in `tests/persona-lifecycle.test.ts`), never a stand-in lock.
- **The accepted real-timer exceptions.** `initRestart` (`src/restart.ts`) takes no clock, so its timer is real wherever the restart module runs. The restart-module cases in `tests/persona-lifecycle.test.ts` set `getRestartDelay` to 0.001 s (a real 1 ms `setTimeout`) and wait for it with a 1 ms-step poll capped at 2 s; only those cases poll in real time, and every other case there flushes continuations or uses the fake clock. `tests/restart.test.ts` waits for its short restart timers with `Bun.sleep(WAIT_MS)` (50 ms) throughout, as it always has, and its `RestartDeps.serialize` cases follow that pattern. That exception covers restart timers only: its retry-entry cases (`runRestartRetry`) call the entry directly, with no timer, and wait on microtask flushes and controllable promises; only the per-key active-count cases there fire one real restart timer beside a retry, waited out with the same `Bun.sleep(WAIT_MS)`. The restart-module cases in `tests/persona-lifecycle.test.ts` also build the real UNAVAILABLE retry controller, on a fake clock whose timers never fire. The attempt-frames case in `tests/unavailable-retry.test.ts` sets one real 0 ms `setTimeout` inside an attempt and awaits its callback: it is no retry timer, and it must be real because the fake clock does not carry the attempt context into its callbacks. Don't add a real timer anywhere else.
- **Teardown tests check every piece of state.** Assert the teardown's full ordered trail for the removed key (bring-up cancel, restart timer, UNAVAILABLE retry timer, launch in flight, connection, routing, destinations and hold, notifier, poller prompts, session drop, kill and delete, outage state, failure counts, disconnected streak, reply guard and launch pass) and that no step ran for another persona. A new per-persona state gets its forget in that trail and a test in its module's suite that drops only the removed key.
- **Hold notices on a fake clock.** A teardown or apply test that can hold a notice uses a destination hold on `createFakeClock()` (see Destination Failure Tests), and asserts a held notice for the removed persona is cancelled, its timer gone.

### Reload Tests

The reload controller (`src/reload.ts`) takes its paths, tick driver, Slack client factory, lifecycle operations and file writer by injection (b.av2 SR-13.1). Test it through `makeReloadHarness(opts?)` from `tests/test-helpers/reload-harness.ts` (b.av2 SR-13.4); its header comment is the full API. `tests/reload.test.ts` is the reference.

| Part | What it gives a test |
|------|----------------------|
| Temp config dir | `h.dir` under one fresh `mkdtempSync` root (`h.root`, real-path resolved), holding only the SR-8.1 files named in `h.paths` (`config`, `pending`, `apply`, `lastApplied`, from `reloadFilePaths`); `h.configDirEntries()` shows exactly what was left there. `h.home` is a temp home for `~` in a test-written config. |
| Personas and files | `h.persona(name, overrides?)` is a file-form persona whose credentials file and working directory sit under the root; nothing exists until the test calls `h.writeCredentials` (a fresh sentinel-bearing token set on each call, so a second call rotates it; `h.tokens(name)` is the latest; a persona whose first credentials are written after a run was built, as for an addition, is registered then with each live run's Slack stubs, scripted by that run's `opts.slack[name]`; a rotation of a persona a live run already routes registers the new pair with that run as another credential set of it, unscripted, so a reconnect builds its clients there while the old set keeps serving), `h.writeCredentialsContent`, `h.makeWorkingDirectory` or `h.materialize`. `h.writeConfig` / `h.writeRecord` write the same input as byte-identical files; the `…Bytes` forms write raw bytes; `h.remove` and `h.replaceWithDirectory` make a file missing or unreadable. For an added persona that cannot come up, `h.preparePersona(name, { credentials?, workingDirectory? }, overrides?)` is `h.persona(name, overrides)` with its files left in the stated states: credentials `valid` (the default, `h.writeCredentials`), `missing`, `invalid` (parseable, no `bot_token`) or `unreadable` (a directory); working directory `present` (the default), `missing` or `file` (a plain file). Singly, `h.deleteWorkingDirectory(persona)` and `h.makeWorkingDirectoryAFile(persona)`. The controller judges these with its real checks, never canned outcomes. Every helper refuses a path outside the root. |
| Edits between ticks | Config: `h.writeConfig` / `h.writeConfigBytes`, `h.deleteConfig()`, `h.replaceWithDirectory(h.paths.config)`. Credentials: `h.writeCredentials` rotates to a new valid set, and `h.rotateCredentials(persona, { slack? })` does the same, returns `{ tokens, label }` and scripts the new set's stub in every live run with `slack` (unscripted, the new tokens are accepted with an identity of their own, another bot user ID and bot ID); every set written has a harness label, `credentials-<n>` (`h.credentialsLabel(name)` is the latest), `h.writeCredentialsContent` writes invalid content, `h.deleteCredentials(persona)`, `h.makeCredentialsUnreadable(persona)` (a directory at the path) and `h.makeFifo(path)` (guard with `mkfifoAvailable()`). `h.saveCredentials(persona)` returns a function that restores the file's exact bytes (or its absence, or the unreadable directory) and its token set, to revert an edit. `h.readCredentialsBytes(persona)` reads the file; `h.credentialsDigestOf(persona)` is the production reader's digest or marker for the file as it stands, to compare with `run.bringUps.credentialsDigest(key)`, never to print. |
| The pending file | `h.pendingExists()`, `h.readPending()` / `h.readPendingText()` (undefined unless a regular file), `h.pendingFingerprint()` (through `parsePendingFingerprint`) and `h.writePendingBytes(b)` for a leftover or hand-placed file. The preview in it: `h.pendingBody()` is the text after the two fingerprint lines and the blank line (found through `parsePendingFingerprint` and `composePendingFile`, not by re-parsing the layout), without the final newline, so it equals `renderPreview(plan)`; `h.pendingLines()` is its lines, `h.pendingHeader()` the first (the counted header, the `no effective change` header or the `INVALID:` line) and `h.pendingDestructiveLines()` those starting `DESTRUCTIVE:`. Each is undefined when the file is absent, not a regular file or not in the pending layout. |
| Write and delete failure seams | `h.failWrites({ step?, code?, call? })` makes the controller's `durableWriteFileSync` throw an errno-style error at that `DurableWriteFs` step (default `openSync`, `EIO`) until `h.clearWriteFailure()`: every call of the step, or with `call` (1-based, counted from `failWrites`) only that one, e.g. `{ step: 'fsyncSync', call: 2 }` for the directory fsync after the rename. `h.failRemoves({ step?, code?, call? })` / `h.clearRemoveFailure()` do the same for the controller's `durableUnlinkSync` (default `unlinkSync`; `{ step: 'fsyncSync' }` fails the directory fsync after the unlink). The two seams count calls separately. |
| Confirmations | `h.confirm()` is the operator's gesture exactly: one rename of `h.paths.pending` to `h.paths.apply` with no content change (AC 73); it throws when there is no pending file. `run.confirmPending()` is the whole confirmation of a change already on disk: a tick that must write the pending file (it throws otherwise), the rename, then the applying tick, returned unawaited as `{ applying }` so a test can hold a lifecycle call or advance `run.clock` while it runs. `run.beforeApplySteps(hook)` runs `hook` once at the next apply's step 1, after the applied set was swapped and before step 2, for example to retarget a symlink so a credentials real path collides after the candidate was validated (b.av2 SR-1.4, apply part). `h.writeApplyBytes(b)` places any bytes at the apply path (an older pending copy kept with `h.readPending()` for a stale gesture, or garbage for a malformed one); `h.applyExists()` and `h.readApply()` show what is there. A confirmation that cannot be deleted is `h.failRemoves()`, never file modes. |
| Oversized files | `h.writeOversized(path, { prefix?, size? })` writes a file over the 64 KiB read cap (`MAX_RELOAD_FILE_BYTES + 1` bytes by default): `prefix`, then sentinel-bearing fake-token text, so a leak of its content fails `assertNoLeak`. Use it for any config, record, pending, apply or credentials path. |
| A run | `h.build(opts?)`, or `h.start(opts?)` (build, `resolveStart`, then the start bring-up when the start applied), is one server start with its own real connection manager over stub Slack (each persona's latest tokens), real bring-up controller (`run.bringUps`) and fake clock (`run.clock`). The controller's `checkConfigDir` resolves against the harness's temp home (`checkPersonaConfigDir(persona, { home })`), or is `checkLaunchConfigDir` over the harness's spawn home with a real launch, never the process home. `h.start` arms no detection tick; `h.startDetecting(opts?)` is `h.start` then `run.startDetection()`, as `main()` does, and throws when nothing was armed (call `run.startDetection()` yourself to test a refused or early call). `opts.slack` scripts a persona's stub by name: `SLACK_UNREACHABLE` leaves it `retrying` however far the clock advances and `SLACK_AUTH_REJECTED` leaves it `broken`, each holding the digest of the file it read. `opts.dryRun` reaches the connection manager, the bring-up controller and the reload controller as a flag, never through `process.env`. `opts.bringUpFs` overrides the bring-up file-system seam, `opts.configFs` passes a `PersonaConfigFs` override to the controller's record, config and pending-file reads, and `opts.credentialsFs` a `CredentialsFs` override to the detection tick's credentials reads. `opts.heldCredentialsDigest(key, held)` wraps the controller's held-digest lookup (`held` is the default, `run.bringUps.credentialsDigest`), for example to throw and so fail a detection check. The controller's `bringUpState` is bound to `run.bringUps.state`, as `server.ts` binds it; `opts.bringUpState(key, state)` wraps it the same way, for example to throw so the preview leaves that fact out. The tick's checks of an added persona are the controller's own (the real credentials content check over the bytes the tick read, none in dry run, `checkPersonaWorkingDirectory` over the temp directory, always on the real file system, and the default `checkPersonaConfigDir` against `h.home`). The bring-up controller gets the manager's `bringUp`, `status` and `stop`, as `server.ts` wires it, so a `claude_config_dir` hold that starts later closes the persona's connection. `opts.tickDriver` replaces the manual driver (see the rules). A confirmed apply: without `opts.applySteps` the controller runs its default fan-out over the recorder (step 2 tears down each removed persona and the old half of each destructive modify, step 3 updates each persona modified in place, step 4 reconnects each persona whose credentials changed and that is not credentials-broken, step 5 refreshes the template once when the plan's config directories changed, and step 6 brings up each added persona and the new half of each destructive modify and, as a recovery, each credentials-changed persona step 4 did not reconnect; a next-launch change gets no record); `opts.applySteps` replaces all of steps 2–6 (`ApplyStepSlots`), for testing the step runner itself. `opts.realLifecycle` binds the real composition behind the recorder's `teardown`, `updateInPlace`, `reconnectCredentials`, `refreshTemplate` and `bringUp` (a recovery included; `run.composition`, see the Real composition row), and hands the bring-up controller and the connection manager its serializer, and the controller the live applied set, as `server.ts` does (so a b.ujn mark's socket close waits for the persona's turn); `opts.agentDirector` passes `StubClientOptions` (e.g. `killError`) to its agent-director stub. `run.appliedConfigs` holds every configuration the controller's `onApplied` was told, in order (`opts.onApplied` is called too); `run.appliedKeys()` is the controller's applied persona keys now; `run.serverConfig()` is `server.ts`'s `personaConfig` (`configInEffect`: the applied personas over the start's server-wide settings), which every consumer and real launch reads, so a confirmed server-wide change shows in `run.appliedConfigs` and `h.readRecord()` but not there until a later `h.start()` over the same directories (the next server start). `opts.beforeWrite(path)` runs before every writer call, for example to see whether `h.paths.apply` still exists when the record is written. |
| Manual tick driver | `run.ticks` (`createManualTickDriver`): nothing runs until the test calls `tick()` or `ticks(n)`, each awaited until the pass settles; `tick` rejects when nothing is armed. `armed`, `startCalls`, `stopCalls` and `ticksRun` show how the controller used it. |
| Lifecycle recorder | `run.lifecycle` (`ReloadLifecycleRecorder`) is the controller's `ReloadLifecycleOps`. Bring-ups and launches go through the real bring-up controller (a launch is only recorded); teardowns and in-place updates are recorded, an in-place update's record (`update-in-place`) with the changed `settings` in the controller's order. A `reconnect` runs the real controller's `changeCredentials` over the manager (the real reconnect: new connection first, the 10 s `start()` bound and the SR-3.2 retries on `run.clock`), and its record gets how its first attempt settled (`change`). A recovery bring-up's record has `recovery: true`; the stand-in cancels the persona's bring-up, stops its connection and brings it up afresh. Neither stand-in ever forgets a cached DM conversation, so a reconnect or recovery of a persona that has had a `dm` destination in the run, or a recovery of a persona no longer credentials-broken when it runs (the lifecycle applies that as a reconnect), needs `opts.realLifecycle`: without it the stand-in refuses the call (it rejects), records the refusal, and `h.cleanup()` throws it, naming the persona. Read `records`, `of(op)`, `keys(op)`, `outcome(key)`, `classes(key)` and `startPasses`; `holdStartPass()` holds the next start pass open after its bring-ups and launches: call the release it returns to let the pass return, or `release.fail(err)` to make the pass throw `err`. Apply-time calls: `hold(op, key)` (`op` is `teardown`, `update-in-place`, `reconnect` or `bring-up`) holds that persona's next call after it is recorded but before its body runs; `entered` resolves when it arrives, `release()` runs the body and `fail(err)` makes it reject without running it (call `fail` early to reject at once). `timeline` lists every apply-time teardown, in-place update, reconnect and bring-up as `{op, key, phase}` (`start`, then `settled` or `rejected`) across personas and steps, so step order is asserted without timing; start-pass bring-ups are not in it. Step 5 is a `template-refresh` record under the key `TEMPLATE_REFRESH_KEY` (with the real composition, its `refresh` says how it settled; the stand-in calls nothing); `hold('template-refresh')` holds it the same way, and `applyTimeline` is `timeline` with step 5 in its place, for step order across all six steps. The teardown stand-in refuses the old half of a destructive modify of a persona that has had a `dm` destination (its new half would post through a DM conversation only the lifecycle forgets), like the other stand-in refusals. |
| Real composition | With `opts.realLifecycle`, `run.composition` is `createPersonaLifecycle` over the run's real bring-up controller and connection manager, a real serializer, the real `killPersonaInstance` / `deletePersonaInstance` over a `makeStubClient` stub, and recorders for every other dependency; `destinations.forget` is recorded and also forgets in the run's own destination resolver, so an in-place update's forget reaches the resolver the notices post through; `forgetAcks` is recorded and also calls the real `forgetPersonaAcks`, as `server.ts` binds it; `forgetNoticeEpisodes` is only recorded, as the run opens no notice episode. Read `lifecycle` (the composition itself), `calls` (every dependency call as `[member, key]`, members named as in `PersonaLifecycleDeps`, plus `outage-notice`), `agentDirector` (the stub's call log by verb) and `agentDirectorOrder` (`kill` and `delete` in order). To show an instance was never spawned, resumed, killed, deleted or typed into, assert `stubCallCount(run.composition!.agentDirector)` (from `tests/test-helpers/agent-director-stub.ts`) is 0. `agentDirectorCalls` is every agent-director call in order with its `result` (a spawn with its `cwd`, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` and `config_dir` label), and `instanceCallsOf(name)` that persona's spawn, resume, kill and delete. Step 5's refresh calls the stub's `makeTemplate` (params on `agentDirector.makeTemplateCalls`) over `installedTemplate`, what the start's install would write (`buildTemplateParams` over the start configuration; nothing is installed at the start); `failTemplateRefresh(err)` makes the next refresh reject. It installs the outage state's module dependencies, which `h.cleanup()` resets. Without it, `teardown` only cancels the bring-up and stops the connection, and `updateInPlace` is only recorded. |
| Real launch path | `opts.realLaunch` (implies `opts.realLifecycle`) runs every launch (start pass, bring-up retry, apply bring-up, `run.relaunch`) through the real `spawnForPersona` over `run.serverConfig()`: the pre-launch `claude_config_dir` check, the collision ladder, the trust patch and the reply-guard steps over `h.stateDir`, against the composition's stub and the harness's row table, never as a startup launch. The table outlives a run, as agent-director's store outlives a server restart: a spawn creates a `waiting` row, a resume sets it `waiting`, a kill `ended`, a delete removes it. `h.seedRow(persona, { state?, cwd?, configDir?, labels? })` sets or changes a row to choose the ladder's path (an `ended` row with an old `config_dir` label is deleted and spawned fresh, one with the current label resumed) and `h.rowOf(name)` reads it; `run.relaunch(name)` is the persona's next launch through the restart path (the real relaunch gate, then `launchSession`, in its serializer turn), answering `true`, `false`, `'skipped'` or `'refused'` (the launch's UNAVAILABLE retry timer owns the persona). `makeReloadHarness({ personaConfigDirs: true })` gives every `h.persona` its own temp `claude_config_dir` (`h.configDir(name)` makes one; override with `claude_config_dir: undefined` to inherit the top-level default); without it a persona's directory is the temp home's `.claude`. `h.replyGuardDir` is the reply-guard record directory and `h.readReplyGuardRecord(name)` a record's text. `run.sessionNotices` records every session-manager notice (spawn failure, restart cap, lost history). The run installs the session manager's module seams (the agent-director client, dialog and tmux fakes, spawn home, trust patcher, reply guard, `claude_config_dir` hook, session notifier) and captures `console.error` into `run.logs`; `h.cleanup()` resets them. The start-time bootstraps (sweep, trust, Stop hook, template install) don't run. |
| Consumers and post-apply drivers | Every run wires the real consumers of the applied settings as `server.ts` wires them, each reading the controller's applied configuration at each use: the routing (`createPersonaRouting`) behind the real event router on the connection manager's `onEvent`, and one destination resolver, destination hold and notifier (built by `makeNotifierStack`, the hold on that stack's own fake clock, never `run.clock`), whose up-flush listener joins the bring-up controller on `onStatus`. None of them is exposed on the run; reach them only through the drivers. Drive the next event, call or post after an apply with: `run.registerSession(name)` (registers the persona's MCP session in the real registry; `run.session(name)` is its session now, compared by identity to show it was kept); `run.deliver(name, event)` (delivers a Slack event on the persona's socket stub, through the event router and routing) and `run.deliveries(name)` (what reached its session, as `chat_id`, `via` and `content`); `run.notice(name, text)` (raises a notice through the real notifier; read its Slack calls on `run.stub(name).callLog`); `run.callTool(name, tool, args)` (calls an MCP tool as the persona's instance over `createSessionServer` with the real tool handlers, answering `isError` and `text`; needs `run.registerSession` first, and refuses every file). Deliver only to a persona with a registered session: a lost message would reach the restart module, which the harness does not set up. A delivery makes one `users.info` call for the author's name and no ack reaction. `run.noticeClock` is the destination hold's fake clock: advance it to drive the retries of a held notice. |
| Credential sets and sockets | `run.credentialsStub(name, label?)` is a credential set's stub in this run (default the latest; the set the run was built with is also `run.stub(name)`), and `run.currentStub(name)` the one whose socket connected last, the set serving the persona now. `run.socketActivity(name)` is the persona's socket clients' events in order, labelled by set (`'connected credentials-2'`, `'discarded credentials-1'`, …), which tells "new opened, then old closed" apart; the full record is `run.slack.activityOf(key)`. The current identity is `run.connections.manager.identity(key)`. |
| Not-up personas | `run.isUp(name)` is `server.ts`'s up check (`createPersonaUpPredicate`), `run.admitSession(name)` its MCP admission (`decideSessionAdmission`: registers when admitted, else one refusal line) and `run.bringUps.state(key)` the outcome. To break a running persona: `run.revokeBotToken(name, { error?, credentials?, queues? })` puts `token_revoked` (or `error`) at the front of every Web API queue of its current stub, so the first call any consumer makes marks it credentials-broken (bug b.ujn), and returns a function that removes the entries still queued; `run.refuseReopen(name, error?)` drops its socket with a refused reopen queued and resolves once it is broken. `run.linesOf(name)` is every log line naming the persona. |
| Token environment | `h.poisonTokenEnvironment()` sets `SLACK_BOT_TOKEN` and `SLACK_APP_TOKEN` to distinct sentinel-bearing fakes and records every read of either through `process.env` in `reads`, to show nothing uses the environment (AC 19, AC 22). One at a time; `restore()` puts both back, and `h.cleanup()` calls it. |
| Captures | `run.logs` (one `[slack]` stream, in order) and `run.logsOf(label)` (its lines starting `[slack] <label>: `, a class such as `RELOAD_PREVIEW` or `'reload'` for the unclassed failure lines) and `run.invalidLines()` (`logsOf(RELOAD_INVALID)`); `run.tickCredentialsReads` (every credentials path the detection tick opened, empty for a whole dry run) and `run.tickDirectoryChecks` (every working directory the tick's added-persona check stat'd), which are not part of `since`, so slice them by length around a stretch of ticks; `run.writes` (every writer call with its path and `ok`); `run.removes` (every durable delete as `{path, ok, removed, unsynced}`: `ok` means the file is gone after the call, `removed` whether this call removed it (undefined when the unlink failed), `unsynced` that it was removed but the directory sync failed); `run.pendingWrites()` / `run.pendingRemoves()` (those on `h.paths.pending`); `run.slackCalls()`, `run.slackPosts()`; and `run.captured(extra?)`, which gathers all of them plus every file the run's writer wrote that still exists (as `writtenFile`) for `assertNoLeak`. |
| Preview emissions | One logged preview spans several `reload-preview` lines; its header starts `PREVIEW_EMISSION_START` (`[slack] reload-preview: ` then `PENDING_PREVIEW_TITLE`). `run.previewEmissions()` groups `run.logs` into emissions (a header and the `reload-preview` lines right after it), `run.previewEmissionCount()` counts them, `run.lastPreviewEmission()` is the latest one's full log lines and `run.lastPreviewText()` the same as body lines (prefix removed, and the header's pending-file suffix), which equals `h.pendingLines()` when the file was written. The exported `previewEmissions(lines)` and `previewEmissionText(emission, pendingFile)` do the same over any slice, such as `run.since(cp).logs`; a header whose write failed keeps its text, so assert the missing suffix on the full log line. A `reload-invalid` line is not an emission. |
| Checkpoints | Take `run.checkpoint()`, drive ticks, then `run.since(cp)` is `{logs, writes, removes, lifecycle, slackBuilds, slackCalls}` since the checkpoint. `run.since(cp)` equal to `NO_RUN_ACTIVITY` shows the ticks did nothing at all; spread `NO_RUN_ACTIVITY` and set only the expected fields to show they did exactly that. |
| Stop | `await run.stop()` stops detection, cancels every bring-up retry and the destination hold, closes the tool clients and stops every connection; it is idempotent. |

Rules for reload tests:

- **Use the harness.** Build every SR-8.1 path through the harness (`h.paths`, `h.persona`, `h.write…`), never by joining `config.json.pending`, `.apply` or `.last-applied` onto a directory by hand. The harness keeps each path under its temp root and refuses one outside it.
- **Manual ticks only.** Drive detection with `run.ticks.tick()` / `ticks(n)`. Never use real timers, `sleep` or a timer-backed tick driver; bring-up retries run on `run.clock`, and advance only when the test moves it, so they never mix into a stretch of ticks. One exception: the test that shows the first check comes 5 s after `startDetection` passes the production driver on the run's fake clock through `opts.tickDriver` (a factory returning `createReloadTickDriver` over the `clock` and `log` it is given) and moves `run.clock`; it never uses a real timer. Every other reload test drives ticks by hand.
- **Assert effects through the recorder.** Which personas were brought up, launched, torn down, reconnected or updated in place, and with what outcome and class, is read from `run.lifecycle`, and file effects from `run.writes`, `run.removes`, `h.readRecord()`, `h.readConfig()` and `h.configDirEntries()`. Don't stub `ReloadLifecycleOps` by hand.
- **Detection applies nothing.** A detection test takes a checkpoint before the edit and asserts `run.since(cp)` in full: `NO_RUN_ACTIVITY` when nothing should happen, or `NO_RUN_ACTIVITY` spread with only the expected pending-file write or delete and log lines. At the least, `run.since(cp).lifecycle` is `[]`, which is how a test shows a tick made no lifecycle call (AC 55).
- **Held states come from the harness.** Put a persona in `retrying` or `broken` with `SLACK_UNREACHABLE`, `SLACK_AUTH_REJECTED` or a credentials or directory fixture, and revert a credentials edit with the function `h.saveCredentials` returned. Compare held content through `h.credentialsDigestOf` and `run.bringUps.credentialsDigest`, never by printing a digest or reading a token.
- **Fake tokens only.** Credentials files come from `h.writeCredentials` (sentinel-bearing fakes, rotated on each call) or `h.writeCredentialsContent`; a hand-shaped file that holds a token builds it with `fakeToken`.
- **Paths explicit, temp dirs only (b.av2 SR-13.2).** Pass every path explicitly and keep every file under the harness's `mkdtempSync` root. Never read or write `~/.claude/channels/slack` or `~/.agent-director`, never let a path resolve to the real home, and never start a server or run the CLI against the real HOME.
- **Leak-check every run and assert no Slack post.** Call `assertNoLeak(run.captured())` for each run in every test, failure paths included, so the logs and reload files carry no token. Every detection test also asserts that `run.slackPosts()` is empty for each run, so no reload output reached Slack (b.av2 SR-7.2); `tests/reload.test.ts` wraps both in `expectNoPostNoLeak(...runs)`. `captured()` includes only files the run's writer wrote that still exist, so to check the pending file's content, call it while the file is there. A test-written file holding `LEAK_SENTINEL` (a malformed config with a pasted fake token) is left out of `captured()` on purpose; a test that checks what the code wrote passes it through `writtenFile` itself.
- **Sweep the whole tree for AC 20.** `captured()` misses a file the run's writer didn't write (a reply-guard record, a leftover confirmation, anything under the state directory or the home), so a secrecy case also checks `h.serverSideFiles(...operatorWritten)`: every regular file under the harness root but the operator's own (`config.json`, credentials files, a hand-edited record), keyed by root-relative path, which `assertNoLeak` checks by name and content. Pass it beside `captured()` (`assertNoLeak({ ...run.captured(), server: h.serverSideFiles(...) })`), as `tests/reload.test.ts` does, or through `tests/reload-apply.test.ts`'s closing `sweep`. It walks regular files only: the server writes nothing else, a symlink or FIFO under the root is a test fixture (a `claude_config_dir` link, `h.makeFifo`), a symlink's target is swept where it lives when it is under the root, and opening a FIFO would block the test. Don't use it to replace `captured()`: the writer's log and call records are only there.
- **Clean up.** Call `await h.cleanup()` in `afterEach`; it stops every run (detection included), resets the session registry and ack tracker when a run registered a session or delivered an event, and removes the root. A test that must show no timer is left calls `await run.stop()` and asserts `run.clock.pendingCount()` is 0.
- **Permission cases use seams.** Record and pending-file write failures go through `h.failWrites`, pending-file delete failures through `h.failRemoves`, an unreadable file through `h.replaceWithDirectory`, `h.makeCredentialsUnreadable`, `opts.configFs`, `opts.credentialsFs` or `opts.bringUpFs`, never through file modes, so the suite passes as root.
- **Real FIFOs run in a child process.** A case that puts a real FIFO at the config or a credentials path (`h.makeFifo`) builds its harness inside a child `bun` process with a 10 s bound and asserts the child was not killed (see Credentials Fixtures and Leak Checks). In `tests/reload.test.ts` every such case goes through `runHarnessInChild(body)`, which runs `body` over a harness under the test's root, bounds the child, asserts it exited cleanly and returns the one JSON line it printed with its stderr; the case then checks from that result that nothing reached Slack (no Slack call, or no lifecycle record or write on a refused start) and runs `assertNoLeak` over it.
- **Use the reference file's helpers.** In `tests/reload.test.ts`, `running(names, opts?)` is a server running `names` from a byte-equal record and config file with detection started and its first check run (`opts` goes to `h.startDetecting`). `pendingWritten(logs?)` and `pendingRemoved(logs?)` are the `run.since(cp)` of a stretch that wrote or removed the pending file once with its log lines; by default `pendingWritten` builds the expected log from the pending file itself (`loggedPreview()`: each body line classed `reload-preview`, the header ending with the pending-file suffix), so the logged preview must be the file's body line for line. `previewHeader(counts)`, `NO_EFFECTIVE_CHANGE_PREVIEW`, `invalidPreview(error)` and `invalidLogged(error, written?, pendingFile?)` are the expected header, no-effect preview, INVALID body and `reload-invalid` line; `loggedPreview(lines?, written?, pendingFile?)` and `invalidLogged` point at `h.paths.pending` unless given another pending path (a child harness's, which the FIFO cases' child prints). `personaRef(name)` is how every preview line names a persona, and `removedLine(name)`, `destructiveLine(name, setting, path)` and `addedLine(name, causes?)` are a removal's, a destructive path change's and an addition's line; `credentialsChangedLine(persona, effect?, cause?)` is a credentials line by persona and path, with `effect` one of `reconnect` (the default), `kept`, `retry`, `bring-up`, `stays-broken` or `unchecked` (the bring-up state could not be checked); a `cause` gives the "but it cannot be used (<cause>)" form, required for `kept` and `stays-broken` and refused for `reconnect` and `bring-up`; and `credentialsChangeLines()` the pending preview's credentials lines. A line these helpers don't cover (an in-place change, say) is asserted on the tick path by the header's counts, its wording left to the pure tests. Pin a pending-file write or delete failure line with `fileFailureLine(code)`, which matches the `[slack] reload:` prefix, the pending path and the errno code, not the sentence; a line whose wording another task owns is matched by its prefix or its class only.
- **Confirm through the harness.** Make the operator's gesture with `h.confirm()` and any other apply-path content with `h.writeApplyBytes`; never rename or write `config.json.apply` by hand. Make a confirmation undeletable with `h.failRemoves`, a record write fail with `h.failWrites`, and an oversized file with `h.writeOversized`.
- **An apply test shows the untouched persona is untouched.** Assert that a persona the confirmed change does not touch gets no lifecycle record (`run.lifecycle`), Slack client or Slack call from the apply. Assert the apply's step order and effects through recording `opts.applySteps` bodies and `run.appliedConfigs` / `run.appliedKeys()`, not by stubbing the controller's internals.
- **Step order through the default fan-out uses the timeline.** A test of what a confirmed change does to the personas runs without `opts.applySteps` and asserts step order through `run.lifecycle.timeline`, holding a call with `run.lifecycle.hold(op, key)` to show the next step waits for it. Record `opts.applySteps` bodies only when testing the step runner itself (step inputs, failures, order of the six slots). Assert the exact lifecycle records for the changed personas and none for any other, and use `opts.realLifecycle` when the case is about the teardown, in-place update or bring-up body (agent-director calls, dependency order).
- **An in-place test shows the recorder and the effect.** Assert `run.since(cp).lifecycle` is exactly the one `update-in-place` record (with its `settings`) for the changed persona, and that nothing else happened to it: no teardown, bring-up or launch, `stubCallCount(run.composition!.agentDirector)` 0, `run.composition!.calls` only the expected `destinations.forget` (none when no DM destination setting changed), the same `run.session(name)` and no socket closed or client built. Then show the new value's effect on the next event, tool call or post through `run.deliver`, `run.callTool` or `run.notice`, not only the stored configuration. Use the harness's consumers and drivers; never hand-build a routing, resolver, notifier, registry session or MCP client beside the run.
- **Use the apply file's helpers.** In `tests/reload-apply.test.ts`, `running(specs, opts?, prepare?)` is a server running `specs` from a byte-equal record and config file with detection started and its first check run. Each spec is a persona name, or `[name, overrides]` for `h.persona(name, overrides)`; `prepare(personas)` may break some after their files are created and before the record is written (a deleted working directory for `retrying`, deleted credentials for `broken`). `opts` goes to `h.startDetecting`, plus `sessions: true` to register an MCP session for every persona; `REAL_WITH_SESSIONS` (`{ realLifecycle: true, sessions: true }`) is what the AC 58 routing and in-place cases use. `confirmConfig(run, personas)` writes the config, ticks, confirms and returns the applying tick unawaited (to hold a step first); `applyConfig` awaits it. `twoOfEach()` runs four personas and returns a change with two personas in each of steps 2, 3 and 6, for the step-failure rows. The next-event drivers are `deliverTo(run, name, event)` (what the routing delivered for that event, as `{chat_id, via}`), `noticeCalls(run, name)` (the Web API calls one notice made, as `<method> <target>`) and `callsSince`; `slackSideOf(run, name)` snapshots a persona's sockets, `auth.test` calls, Web API calls and client builds, to show an in-place update touched none of them.
- **A reconnect test shows the overlap and the isolation.** Rotate with `h.rotateCredentials` (scripting the new set with `slack`), confirm with `run.confirmPending()`, and assert through the harness: `run.socketActivity(name)` shows the new set `connected` before the old one is `discarded` and `disconnected` (or, for a failure or a retry, the old one never closed); `run.lifecycle.of('reconnect')` is exactly one record, for that persona, with its `change`; `run.session(name)` is the same session and, with `opts.realLifecycle`, `stubCallCount(run.composition!.agentDirector)` is 0; every other persona gets no lifecycle record, client build or Slack call. Never script the old and new tokens on one stub: the credential sets keep them apart.
- **The 10 s bound runs on the fake clock.** Script the new set's `connect` with `open-never-answers` (or `never`), advance `run.clock` to just under 10 s to show step 4 still holds the apply, then to 10 s to show the attempt is abandoned and the apply moves on. Never wait in real time; the `AC 68` start-never-settles case in `tests/reload-apply.test.ts` is the reference.
- **Break a running persona through the harness.** Use `run.revokeBotToken` for a revoked bot token (bug b.ujn) and `run.refuseReopen` for a refused reopen, never a hand-scripted queue or a direct status change. Assert the persona's lines with `run.linesOf(name)`, its state with `run.isUp` and `run.bringUps.state`, and its instance's registration with `run.admitSession`. A case about the window before the detached socket's close gets the persona's turn builds `makeConnectionHarness` with `serialize` (see Connection Wiring and Bring-Up Tests); `tests/persona-credentials-revoked.test.ts` is the reference.
- **A destructive-modify test uses the real launch.** Move the persona's `working_directory` or `credentials_file` with `opts.realLaunch` and `personaConfigDirs`, and assert exactly one `teardown` and one `bring-up` record for it, `instanceCallsOf(name)` showing its row killed, deleted and spawned fresh (never resumed), `run.socketActivity(name)` showing the old connection closed before the new one opened, and no call of any kind for the other persona. To show nothing starts it between the halves, hold its step-6 `bring-up` and drive a restart (`run.relaunch`) or a bring-up retry (`run.clock`) meanwhile.
- **A next-launch test shows no lifecycle call, then the new value at the next launch.** For `claude_config_dir` or `stop_hook_bootstrap` (own or inherited), assert `run.since(cp).lifecycle` has no record for any persona (only step 5's `template-refresh` when the directory set changed), no instance call, and the same connection, MCP session, row and reply-guard record. Then seed an `ended` row and call `run.relaunch(name)`: its spawn (`claudeConfigDir`, `configDirLabel`) and `h.readReplyGuardRecord(name)` carry the new value, and a persona running beside it keeps its own. Driving the launch directly instead of through the reload apply doesn't cover AC 59.
- **A server-wide test reads `run.serverConfig()`.** Assert the new value is in `h.readRecord()` and `run.appliedConfigs`, that `run.serverConfig()` still has the start-time one with no lifecycle call, and that a later `h.start()` over the same directories runs the new one.
- **A step-5 test uses the real composition.** Assert the `template-refresh` record, `run.composition!.agentDirector.makeTemplateCalls` equal to `installedTemplate` with only `allow` replaced, and one `[slack] template refresh:` line; a change that leaves the directory set as it was makes no `makeTemplate` call. Fail it with `failTemplateRefresh(err)`, spying on `process.exit`, and hold it with `run.lifecycle.hold('template-refresh')`; assert order across steps with `applyTimeline`.
- **The token environment is poisoned, not read.** To show an added persona's tokens come from its file, call `h.poisonTokenEnvironment()` and assert no read is added to its `reads` over the apply and no Slack client got its values; never set the variables by hand.
- **Preview wording is pinned in the pure tests.** Every sentence of the preview is pinned in `tests/reload-preview.test.ts`, against `src/reload-plan.ts` with facts injected as data. A tick-path test in `tests/reload.test.ts` asserts the pending file's lines and compares the logged preview with the pending file (`pendingWritten()`, `run.lastPreviewText()`), rather than re-pinning every sentence.
- **Assert each preview line kind exactly.** A preview test asserts each SR-8.4 line kind by its exact line or its exact prefix or keyword: the counted header (`previewHeader`), `DESTRUCTIVE:` (`h.pendingDestructiveLines()`), `INVALID:`, `no effective change`, the credentials line by persona and path, and an added persona's causes. Showing only that some text exists, or that the file is non-empty, is not enough.
- **Assert the log carries the same preview once.** Compare the whole emission, not a line count: `run.since(cp)` equal to `pendingWritten()`, one emission per pending-state change (`run.previewEmissionCount()`), and none for a rewrite caused only by a changed fact. An INVALID candidate logs exactly one `reload-invalid` line and no `reload-preview` line.
- **Leak-check previews that involve credentials.** Whenever a credentials file is involved (a rotation, a held state, an added persona's credentials cause), run `assertNoLeak(run.captured())` while the pending file still exists, so both the file and the log are checked. In the pure tests, `render()` does this for every rendered form.
- **A new config field fails the coverage test until it is classified.** `tests/reload-plan-coverage.test.ts` holds the loader's key lists in `src/config.ts` against the plan's setting classes. A key added to the loader fails it until the key is in exactly one class in `src/reload-plan.ts` (a new server-wide key also needs a row in `CHANGED_VALUES`, a new channel-entry key an entry in `CHANNEL_FIELD_SETTING`) and has a preview line in `tests/reload-preview.test.ts`. Put a field in `UNCOMPARED_PERSONA_FIELDS` only when its change genuinely has no effect; never loosen the test to make it pass.
- **No lifecycle call before confirmation.** A preview test shows the tick applied nothing: `run.since(cp)` in full, or at least `run.since(cp).lifecycle` empty, so there is no reconnect, bring-up or teardown before confirmation (AC 67), and a broken persona stays broken (AC 65, preview half).

Test `durableWriteFileSync` and `durableUnlinkSync` themselves in `tests/atomic-write.test.ts` through their `DurableWriteFs` seam, the production tick driver in `tests/reload-timer.test.ts` on `createFakeClock()`, the fingerprint and pending-file layout in `tests/reload-fingerprint.test.ts`, the change plan and the preview's wording in `tests/reload-preview.test.ts`, which class each config key lands in in `tests/reload-plan-coverage.test.ts`, and parsing and validation of the config bytes in `tests/config.test.ts`; `reload.test.ts` asserts what a start and a detection tick do with them. Where `server.ts` starts and stops detection is a source audit in `tests/reload-wiring.test.ts`, since `main()` can't run in a test.

### Unadvertised Reload Checks (AC 74)

Reload is triggered only by renaming `config.json.pending` to `config.json.apply` (b.av2 SR-8.8). The AC 74 cases pin that no CLI or MCP surface triggers or describes it: the `unknown subcommand` block in `tests/cli.test.ts` and the `tool list and instructions` block in `tests/registry.test.ts` (see their rows under Test File Organization).

- **One term list.** The reload terms are `reload` in any case, `config.json.apply`, `config.json.pending`, `.apply` and `.pending`, kept once in the shared helper `tests/test-helpers/reload-terms.ts` (`RELOAD_TERMS`, with `reloadTermsIn` returning the terms found). `tests/cli.test.ts`, `tests/registry.test.ts` and `tests/shipped-docs.test.ts` (its check that the exported `MCP_INSTRUCTIONS` carries no reload wording, AC 74's `shipped-docs` leg; the README and skills may describe the gesture and are not checked) all import it. Change the list there only; never keep a local copy or drop a term.
- **Assert the terms found, not a boolean.** Compare `reloadTermsIn(text)` with an empty list, so a failure names the term (`tests/shipped-docs.test.ts` runs one `test.each` case per term instead, so the failing case's title names it); for the tools, compare a per-tool map so it also names the tool.
- **Check whole definitions.** The tool check serializes each tool's full definition, so a hint in a schema property description is caught too; the CLI check parses the usage entries, so an added subcommand or flag fails even when its wording is clean.
- **A new CLI subcommand or flag updates the expected usage entries** in the no-arguments case, and a new MCP tool updates the tool-list case; neither may add a reload term.

### Process-Level Listeners

`bun test` runs every file in one process, so a listener added to `process` is process-global state, with the same hazard as `mock.module` (see Module Mocks). A leaked `unhandledRejection` listener would change how every later file handles a stray rejection.

- **Call the handler, don't install it.** Tests call the exported `createUnhandledRejectionHandler(log)` result directly with each rejection reason. The `unhandledRejection handler (SR-3.3)` describe block is the reference. `bun test` already fails on an unhandled rejection, so no test needs a handler of its own.
- **Run code that rejects unhandled in a child process.** When the case under test is itself an unhandled rejection (a throw inside a fire-and-forget tick), run it in a child `bun` process through `runInFakeHome` and have the child count rejections with its own listener and print the result, as the `a throw while a persona's retry is due leaves it retrying` describe block in `tests/permission-poller.test.ts` does.
- **Remove what you add.** A test that must register a process-level listener removes it in `afterEach`. No test leaves one installed. A spy on `process.exit` is restored in `finally`.
- **Check import side effects by count.** To show a module installs no listener at import, compare `process.listenerCount('unhandledRejection')` before and after a fresh import, as the `connection manager: no module-scope effects (SR-13.1)` describe block does.
- **Leak checks still apply.** Every log line the handler produced passes through `assertNoLeak`.

### Parametrization

When 3+ tests follow the same structure with different inputs, collapse them with `test.each`:

```typescript
test.each([
  ['', 'empty string'],
  ['no-prefix', 'missing type prefix'],
])('rejects invalid id %s (%s)', (id) => {
  expect(() => validateId(id)).toThrow()
})
```

Don't wrap simple domain strings in constants (`const STATUS_OPEN = 'open'`) — inline them. Constants earn their keep only for complex formats or values used in 5+ places.

### State Reset

Use `beforeEach` to reset module-scoped state between tests:

- Registry: `_resetRegistry()` (exported from registry.ts)
- Restart, backoff, health check and outage state: `_resetRestartState()`, `_resetBackoffState()`, `_resetHealthCheckState()`, `_resetOutageState()`
- Session-manager notice sink: install a recording sink with `setSessionNotifier(sink)` in `beforeEach` and clear it with `setSessionNotifier(undefined)` in `afterEach`, so no sink leaks into a later file
- Session-manager pre-launch trust patcher: production installs the real `.claude.json` patch (`trustPatchPersona`), but tests never do. A test that checks the patch runs before a launch installs a recording fake with `setPreLaunchTrustPatcher(fake)` and calls `_resetPreLaunchTrustPatcher()` in `afterEach` (the setter cannot clear it). Tests of `trust-bootstrap.ts` itself point the persona's `claude_config_dir` at a temp directory, never the real home (b.av2 SR-13.2)
- Session-manager `claude_config_dir` seams (bug b.g57): a test that makes a persona's directory unresolvable injects realpath and lstat with `_setConfigDirFs(fs)` (or uses a dangling symlink under its temp directory) and, when it installs a hold hook, `setConfigDirUnresolvableHook(hook)`; its `afterEach` calls `setConfigDirUnresolvableHook(undefined)` and `_resetConfigDirFs()`. A `makeReloadHarness` run with `realLaunch` installs and clears the hook itself (`h.cleanup()`)
- Per-persona notice episodes (`src/persona-episodes.ts`): the module holds no module-scope state and exports no reset function; build a fresh `createPersonaEpisodes` instance (over a fresh fake clock) in `beforeEach`. An instance's `forgetAll()` is the module's one test reset: it ends every episode silently and leaves the instance open, and no production path calls it (the server's shutdown calls `close()`). A harness that keeps one instance across cases, such as the recovery harness's `cleanup()`, resets it with `forgetAll()`
- Test-local Maps/Sets: reassign in `beforeEach`

### Stubbing External Dependencies

- **Slack clients**: The server builds every Slack client through the connection manager's injected `PersonaSlackClientFactory`, so persona Slack traffic is stubbed there: pass `makeStubSlackFactory()`'s `factory` and a fake clock to `createPersonaConnectionManager` (see Connection Manager Tests), and read calls from each persona's `makeStubSlack` stub. Code that takes a client directly (`clientFor`, a Web client argument) gets a stub's `web` client. Never build a real `WebClient` or `SocketModeClient`, with one bounded exception, library-integration probes (see the next bullet). A direct Web API stub that is not a Slack stub records each call in a capture array and returns a mock response
- **Library-integration probes (the one real-client exception)**: A test may build a real `WebClient` or `SocketModeClient`, through `PRODUCTION_SLACK_CLIENT_FACTORY` or the library constructor, only to check what the Slack library itself does with the options CSCB hands it: which logger a client and its own `apps.connections.open` client hold, and what the library's lines leave on the console or in the persona's log. `tests/persona-connection-wiring.test.ts` (the socket and redacting logger probes) is the model. Every rule holds:
  - **Loopback only.** Slack is replaced by local stand-ins on `127.0.0.1`, port 0: `Bun.serve` answering every Web API method (pointed at with `slackApiUrl`) and a raw `Bun.listen` WebSocket endpoint. Tokens are fakes from the credentials helper, and captured output is leak-checked.
  - **Events, not time.** The test awaits events (the client's `start()` settling, the first line reaching the log sink), never a `sleep` or a real-time poll. A library timer the probe relies on is shortened through an option (`clientPingTimeout: 30`).
  - **Library integration only.** CSCB behaviour (option contents, statuses, retries, log lines the manager writes) is tested on the stub factory; a probe covers only what a stub can't show, the library's own handling.
  - **Nothing left running.** Every client has closed or settled before the test ends, and every stand-in is closed and stopped in `finally`.
- **Global `fetch`**: `tests/registry.test.ts` stubs `globalThis.fetch` for every test, so no test reaches the network. Its file-level `beforeEach` installs the stub and its `afterEach` restores the real `fetch`; never install it at file top level. The stub records each call's URL, `Authorization` header and `redirect` mode in the harness's `h.fetches`, then answers through `h.fetchHandler`, which throws by default. A test that expects a fetch sets `h.fetchHandler` and asserts on `h.fetches`; the bearer is the persona stub client's fake bot token (`fakeToken`)
- **Persona events**: Inbound `message` / `app_mention` handling is tested by handing events from the Slack stub's event factories to `persona-routing.ts` directly (see Inbound delivery below). To test what a connection does with its events, build the real `createPersonaEventRouter` with a spy `routing.receive` and `handleClick`, and call it with a persona key, or deliver through a stub socket (`deliver`, `deliverInteractive`) on a manager whose `onEvent` is the router; assert the receiving persona's key reaches `receive` and `receivingPersonaKey`
- **Persona notices**: Notice sites never post through a Web client of their own; they take a notify sink. Assert a site's notices through a recording sink: `initOutageState({ notify, getClient })`, `setSessionNotifier(sink)` for the session manager, the `notify` argument of `runJsonlPersistenceSafeguard`, or `h.notices` on the routing harness for lost-message notices (see Inbound delivery below). The sink captures the key and the notice body, without the persona prefix. To prove destination, identity or hold-and-flush, use `makeNotifierHarness(config, opts?)` from `tests/test-helpers/persona-notifier.ts` instead of wiring stubs by hand. It builds the real `createPersonaNotifier` over one `makeStubSlack` stub per persona and installs nothing: pass `h.notifier.notify` to the site under test. It also builds the notifier's own `createPersonaDestinations` resolver (`h.destinations`) and a `createPersonaDestinationHold` over it (`h.hold`) on a fake clock (`h.clock`), so a failing notice is held with no real timer. Options: `validated` (`true` by default, `false`, or a key list), per-key `post` outcomes, `leakMarker`, `dryRun`, `clock` (a `FakeClock`; default a new `createFakeClock()`). Drive held-notice retries with `h.clock` and check the hold with `h.hold.view(key)` (see Destination Failure Tests). Assert with `h.posts(key)`, `h.totalPosts()` and `h.logs`; `h.stub(key)` is the persona's stub, so a `dm`-destination notice scripts its open with `h.stub(key).script.open` and checks the open-then-post order with `h.stub(key).callLog`; `h.validate(key)` marks a client validated, `h.setDryRun(on)` flips dry run, editing `h.personas` simulates a reload, and `h.allPosts()` feeds `assertNoLeak`. `tests/persona-notifier.test.ts` is the reference. Don't stub a module-scope `WebClient` for notices.
- **Permission poller and click handler**: Use `tests/test-helpers/permission-relay-harness.ts`, not hand-built failing Web clients or errors. `startManualPoller(deps)` starts the real poller on a manual interval: nothing runs until the test calls `tick(settleMs?)` (fire once and let it settle) or `fire()`.
